# Positive Scale-Separation Search for Prime Information

## Failure Class Table
| class | mechanism | count | no-go status |
|---|---|---:|---|
| Positive higher-order divisor weight does not isolate primes | The cubic and recursively generated quartic generalized Mangoldt statistics mix nonnegative one-, two-, three-, and four-factor channels; recovering the prime channel requires separate channel control. | 2 | Partial for the tested cubic and quartic transforms, not a universal no-go |
| Positive Chebyshev smoothing and short-window differences retain lower-divisor mass | Fixed profiles have K_X(q)~AX/q; Pass 41's tent has K=H/q+O(1), and Pass 42's harmonic weight has K=(log(X/q)+γ)/q+O(1/X), all retaining lower/intermediate scales. | 5 | PROVED kernel-shape restrictions for these stated families; PARTIAL for any M0+ ceiling because other transforms and Tauberian routes remain open |
| Positive lcm-incidence Gram energy does not isolate the prime diagonal | The PSD common-multiple kernel gives the sum of log² n, but the all-ones energy mixes the prime diagonal with lower-divisor cross channels; the high-prime diagonal is 7.74% of total at X=10⁶. | 1 | Partial for this quadratic statistic, not all Gram or sieve mechanisms |

| Positive nonlinear totient filter isolates primes but leaves its aggregate unevaluated | Raising φ(n)/(n−1) to n makes the composite-weight tail summable and leaves prime terms exact, but the resulting aggregate is θ(X)+O(1), so its leading evaluation is PNT-strength. | 1 | Exact prime extraction and tail bound proved; aggregate asymptotic remains open |

| Squarefree log-lattice aggregate is composite-dominated | The exact squarefree log sum is (6/π²)X log X+O(X), while its prime channel is θ(X)=O(X); squarefree composites supply the leading mass. | 1 | Proved for this aggregate; no universal no-go |
| Factorial valuation gives prime-band upper bounds but no matching lower bound | Central-binomial and trinomial multinomial totals have exact positive Legendre channels and sharply evaluated totals, but their complementary channels are not independently controlled. | 2 | The limitation of a scalar total plus nonnegativity is PROVED; the arithmetic prime-error no-go remains PARTIAL |
| Prime-exponent diagonal is hidden by signed cross-channel cancellation | The adjacent-binomial scalar square has a positive valuation diagonal, but its mixed prime-exponent terms cancel that diagonal; at M=10^6 the diagonal is about 104 times the total. | 1 | Exact decomposition; cancellation measured finitely; no universal no-go |

| Pascal-row carry aggregate evaluates a broad weighted prime average but not a single scale | Summing the full Pascal row gives an exact positive carry kernel and a smooth total; its prime weights remain distributed across quotient bands and the terminal band is triangularly weighted. | 1 | Exact limitation for this aggregate; no no-go for other weights or positive deconvolution |
| Pascal carry Mellin–Wiener inversion reaches qualitative PNT but has no effective inverse bound | The carry multiplier tends to zero; more generally, any probability kernel with |khat(t0)|<1 resists positive probability postfilter deblurring to a point mass. | 3 | M0 via the classical zero-free-line input; unbounded direct inverse and a general positive-postfilter no-go when |khat(t0)|<1; other reconstructions remain open |
| Adjacent-block gcd contrasts suppress some low divisors but retain an unevaluated middle band | Pass 27 cancels q|h; Pass 28 aligns h to lcm(1,...,R), cancelling every prime power q≤R. At X=10⁶, the surviving middle band contributes 85.54% and 86.12%, respectively. | 2 | Partial for these contrasts; no independent residual-energy evaluation and no universal no-go |
| Period-annihilating block polynomials cancel low-period channels but amplify surviving middle scales | The product of factors (1−z^m), m≤R, cancels every prime-power channel q≤R; at X=10⁶ the unannihilated middle bands still contribute 93.29% and coefficient L2 energy is 96,712. | 1 | Partial for this polynomial-filter family; no universal no-go |
| lcm covolume jumps equal the target Chebyshev function | The intersection lattice index satisfies log lcm(1,…,X)=ψ(X) exactly; its prime-power jump measure is Λ, but it has no independent lattice-side evaluation. | 1 | Exact identity proved; M0+ prime-error conclusion remains absent |

| Farey primitive-fraction total has a logarithmically large composite background | The positive weight φ(q)log(q)/(q−1) gives the prime channel θ(X), but the total is (6/π²)XlogX+O(X) and composites dominate. | 1 | Explicit total asymptotic; failure specific to this weight; no universal no-go |

| Positive moving small-omega tilt exposes a prime-power channel but imports analytic prime information | The positive weight z^omega with z=(log X)^(-2) separates omega=1 prime powers, but uniform Selberg-Delange evaluation uses the ordinary zeta zero-free region; no independent prime estimate is obtained. | 1 | Exact extraction and leakage bounds; M1 is inherited from standard analytic input; no universal no-go |

| Multiplicity-sensitive positive tilt isolates primes but imports the analytic mean estimate | Weight z^Omega with z=(log X)^(-2) has an exact prime channel and nonnegative composite leakage, but its M1 evaluation uses uniform Selberg-Delange input. | 1 | Exact extraction/leakage proved; asymptotic conditional on imported analytic input; no universal no-go |

| Largest-prime-factor mass exposes primes but leaves a smooth-cofactor background | Grouping by P^+(n) gives an exact theta(X) term, but the p-smooth cofactor remainder is positive and unestimated; at X=10^6 it is 7.33 times theta(X). | 1 | Exact decomposition and finite profile; no asymptotic estimate or universal no-go |

| Least-prime endpoint is obscured by rough cofactors whose exact count uses Möbius signs | The least-factor sum has an exact theta(X) term, but the remaining p-rough counts are evaluated by signed inclusion-exclusion; positivity alone leaves the complement uncontrolled. | 1 | Exact decomposition; sign-free rough-count estimate absent; no universal no-go |

| Square-root roughness selects primes pointwise but has uncontrolled signed floor-sum error | The product over p<=sqrt(X) is exactly the prime indicator on n<=X, yet replacing its Mobius floor expansion by the density product has error at most 2^pi(sqrt(X)), far too large. | 1 | Exact selection proved; useful aggregate bound absent; no universal no-go |

| Positive mixtures of nested prime-tail masks cannot localize a band | Each generator has kernel 1_{p>z}; nonnegative mixtures remain nondecreasing, so any active band weight persists or grows for all larger primes. | 1 | Monotonicity/no-band theorem proved for nested-tail cone; no universal sieve no-go |

| Positive local-divisibility product isolates a prime band but its sum equals the target | The pointwise difference 2^omega(n)-2^omega_{p<=X/2}(n) is exactly the indicator of primes in (X/2,X], but the resulting lattice sum is theta(X)-theta(X/2) itself. | 1 | Exact localization proved; no independent aggregate evaluation or error term |

## Mechanism Index

Pass 30 is a consolidation, not a new mechanism. Passes 31 and 32 add mechanisms 28 and 29; Pass 33 adds mechanism 30; Pass 34 adds mechanism 31; Pass 35 adds mechanism 32; Pass 36 adds mechanism 33; Pass 37 adds mechanism 34; Pass 38 adds mechanism 35; Pass 39 adds mechanism 36. Pass 40 is a consolidation, not a new mechanism; it strengthens the fixed-dilate kernel result. Pass 41 adds mechanism 37, a tapered X-dependent boundary profile with a uniform bounded-variation kernel estimate. Pass 42 adds mechanism 38, the harmonic reciprocal lattice transform.

## Root Cause Ledger
| where the loss occurs | mechanisms affected | why | intrinsic or artifact |
|---|---|---|---|
| Isolating the one-prime term from the tested generalized-Mangoldt transforms \(\Lambda_3,\Lambda_4\) | 1, 18 | Positive multi-prime-factor channels remain in the known lattice total; evaluating or subtracting them separately requires additional prime information. | Repeated in this transform family; no universal no-go |
| Inverting the cubic divisor average | 1 | The exact forward floor sum is divisor-weighted; its inverse reintroduces Mobius signs. | Intrinsic to this inversion |
| Positive Chebyshev smoothing and short-window differencing | 2, 25, 26, 30, 40, 41, 42 | Fixed dilates and moving windows retain lower scales; Pass 41 proves K=H/q+O(1) for its tent and Pass 42 has K=(log(X/q)+γ)/q+O(1/X), with small/intermediate q amplified. These kernel results do not prove asymptotic lower-channel mass or a PNT error ceiling. | PROVED for the stated kernel families; broader error ceiling open |
| Extracting the prime diagonal from lcm Gram energy | 3 | The diagonal prime block still couples to lower-divisor coordinates in the all-ones vector. | Intrinsic to this statistic; controlled projections remain untested |
| Extracting prime channels from positive norm-lattice totals | 4, 6 | r₂ has a split-prime channel; r₄ has an explicit (p+1) local factor for every odd endpoint prime. Yet both totals retain broad lower-d channels, so their lattice asymptotics give no positive lower bound for the endpoint channel. | Repeated across dimensions 2 and 4; general projected norm-lattice observables remain open |
| Extracting primitive split primes from the coprime norm-lattice transform | 5 | The primitive filter changes local weights and improves the measured prime share, but the all-positive divisor total still has uncontrolled complementary channels. | Intrinsic to this total primitive statistic; alternative projections remain open |
| Switching between divisor-square sieve lattice and prime-supported observables | 7, 8, 10 | Exact floor/lcm evaluation applies to the all-integer statistic, but every pointwise function of the d≤R divisor vector is constant on R-rough inputs; Lambda support restores prime-power specificity but its exact expansion contains psi(X). | PROVED for pointwise truncated-divisor features; aggregate prime-error implication remains open |
| Evaluating positive prime-supported Fourier energy | 9 | Orthogonality removes off-diagonal frequencies exactly and leaves ∑_{p^k≤X}log²p, but the transform is another representation of the unknown prime-power data, not an independent lattice evaluation; converting ∑_p log²p to ψ needs prime-distribution information. | Intrinsic to this Parseval identity; other additive correlations remain open |
| Log-determinant spectral volume collapses to the Chebyshev divisor average plus a convergent Euler correction | 11 | The totient product gives T(X)+C(X), so recovering ψ still needs signed Möbius inversion | Intrinsic to this gcd determinant invariant; other spectral invariants remain open |
| Prime-selective nonlinear totient ratio has summable composite leakage, but its aggregate is PNT-equivalent and has no independent lattice evaluation | 12 | Exact O(1) θ-extraction proved; PNT-scale evaluation of the new statistic is missing | Current aggregate only; no general no-go |
| Positive residue-lattice norm has a known value but prime-power cross terms cancel its diagonal | 13 | The centered channel Gram contains signed cross terms; at X=10⁶ they cancel 99.999042% of the full diagonal | Specific to this statistic; coupled lattice observables remain open |
| Extracting a quantitative ψ-error from the classical Selberg symmetry formula | 14 | The identity has an O(X) remainder and the reproduced Selberg–Erdős iteration proves only ψ(X)=X+o(X) here; no explicit decay rate is derived. | Current replay only; quantitative variants remain open |
| Prime-selective channel is lower-order inside the squarefree log-lattice aggregate | 14 | The total is (6/π²)X log X+O(X), but Chebyshev gives only a θ(X)=O(X) prime channel, so the known aggregate asymptotic is carried by squarefree composites and does not determine θ(X)−X. | Intrinsic to this unweighted squarefree statistic; alternative positive projections remain open |
| Lower-prime and prime-power channels prevent lower bounds from central-binomial and trinomial totals | 15, 19 | Positivity gives an upper bound for the selected coefficient-one prime band; a lower bound requires an independent estimate of the actual complementary channels. | Repeated in two factorial totals; no general no-go for coupled identities |
| Pascal-row carry kernel averages all quotient scales and its first difference returns the Chebyshev divisor identity | 21 | The explicit positive weight W_n(q) has a broad piecewise-linear profile; the known total and controlled higher powers give only a weighted prime average, while an unweighted band requires inversion or additional information. | Specific to this aggregate; stable positive deconvolution remains open |
| Quantifying inverse control for the Pascal carry Mellin kernel | 22, 23, 24, 30 | The multiplier tends to zero, so direct inverse norms diverge; whenever a smoothed probability kernel has |khat(t0)|<1, every positive probability postfilter retains that fixed Fourier gap to a point mass. | Direct L² inverse obstruction proved for the carry multiplier; positive-postfilter no-go proved for any kernel with a strict Fourier contraction; signed/nonlinear alternatives remain open |
| Reassembling prime-exponent coordinates into the known scalar square | 16 | The exact positive diagonal E(M) is related to A(M) by mixed-sign cross terms; measured cancellation makes E(M) far larger than A(M), so positivity of A does not bound the prime band. | Specific to this scalar reconstruction; orthogonalized quadratic transforms remain open |
| Adjacent-block gcd contrast leaves a dominant middle-scale floor-difference channel | 27, 28 | Pass 27's two-block contrast and Pass 28's LCM-aligned step cancel selected low prime powers, but neither independently evaluates the surviving h<q≤X−2h channels; these contribute 85.54% and 86.12% at X=10⁶. | Specific to these contrasts; higher-order or independently evaluated projections remain open |
| Period-annihilating polynomial cancels low-period block counts but magnifies middle-scale channels | 29 | Roots at every d-th root of unity for d≤R force c_q=0 for q≤R, but polynomial coefficients grow and the nonzero middle channels dominate the computed energy. | Specific to this polynomial family; norm-controlled filters remain open |
| Exact covolume already equals the target prime-power summatory function | 31 | The local jump identity yields δₙ=Λ(n), hence log lcm(1,…,X)=ψ(X); evaluating it is the original problem. | Intrinsic to this covolume observable; alternate independently evaluable lattice invariants remain open |


| Farey prime channel is diluted by a logarithmically larger composite background | 32 | The exact prime component is θ(X); the full positive total is (6/π²)X(log X−1)+O(log²X), while PNT gives θ(X)~X, so composite denominators supply the leading XlogX mass. | Intrinsic to this weight; other Farey projections remain open |

| Uniform small-z multiplicative mean evaluation imports analytic prime information | 33 | Selberg-Delange for z^omega uses complex-analytic continuation and the ordinary zeta zero-free region; the positive channel split does not independently yield the M1 estimate. | Method-specific dependency, not a universal no-go for multiplicative tilts |

| Multiplicity-sensitive positive mean still needs a prime-distribution analytic input | 34 | The exact Omega=1 channel is theta, but evaluating the whole positive sum requires uniform Selberg-Delange for z^Omega and the ordinary zeta zero-free region; the leakage bound alone is only one-sided. | Specific to this mean evaluation, not universal |

| Largest-prime-factor grouping leaves an unevaluated smooth-cofactor family | 35 | The exact sum over n=p^a m identifies theta as one endpoint term, but no independent lattice asymptotic controls the remaining p-smooth m and higher powers; positivity supplies only theta<=L. | Specific to this statistic; broader smooth-number projections remain open |

| Least-prime-factor statistic leaves a p-rough complement | 36 | Its exact count is Phi(y,p)=sum_{d|product_{q<=p}q} mu(d) floor(y/d); signs are required for the known evaluation, while nonnegative counts alone have no derived asymptotic. | Specific rough-count obstruction; broader positive rough-number estimates remain open |

| Square-root roughness mask aggregate lacks floor-sum cancellation | 37 | Pointwise product is a prime indicator, but inclusion-exclusion has 2^pi(sqrt(X)) terms; individual floor errors give only an exponentially large bound, while useful cancellation is signed or requires sieve input. | Specific to this direct mask evaluation; other positive sieve bounds remain open |

| Positive mixtures of nested roughness masks preserve monotone prime-tail kernels | 38 | Each tail generator 1_{p>z_j} is nondecreasing in p; nonnegative coefficients cannot make a localized band that turns off above its upper edge. | Intrinsic to this generator cone; non-nested kernels remain open |

| Exact positive prime-band selector has no independent total evaluation | 39 | The product difference cancels all lower squarefree divisors and leaves only prime divisors above X/2; its integer sum therefore equals theta(X)-theta(X/2), so evaluating it requires the target prime information. | Intrinsic to this selector as defined; another independently evaluable observable remains possible |

## Lessons Ledger
| constraint | source pass | status |
|---|---:|---|
| Nonnegative higher-order generalized Mangoldt channels do not by themselves isolate primes; control composite channels quantitatively. | 1, 18 | Demonstrated for the cubic and tested quartic transforms; no universal no-go |
| Keep exact floor identities distinct from signed inverses. | 1 | Exact identity; no prime error follows |
| Do not infer M0 from numerical trends in higher-order statistics. | 1 | Mandatory proof/data distinction |
| Measure divisor kernels by logarithmic scale before calling smoothing single-scale. | 2 | Exact asymptotic and numerical profile |

## Pass 1 — Cubic positive divisor transform

### 1. MECHANISM

Define
\[
\Lambda_3(n)=(\mu*\log^3)(n)=\sum_{d\mid n}\mu(d)\log^3(n/d),
\qquad S_3(x)=\sum_{n\le x}\Lambda_3(n).
\]
For \(n=\prod_{i=1}^r p_i^{a_i}\), this is the \(r\)-fold finite difference of the cubic polynomial \((\sum_i a_i\log p_i)^3\). It is nonnegative and vanishes when \(r>3\). The exact positive convolution expansion is
\[
\boxed{\Lambda_3=\Lambda\,\log^2+3\,\Lambda*(\Lambda\,\log)+\Lambda*\Lambda*\Lambda,}
\]
where products such as \(\Lambda\log\) are pointwise and stars are Dirichlet convolutions. Hence
\[
S_3(x)=\sum_{p^a\le x}(3a^2-3a+1)(\log p)^3
+3\sum_{uv\le x}\Lambda(u)\Lambda(v)\log v
+\sum_{uvw\le x}\Lambda(u)\Lambda(v)\Lambda(w).
\]
Every summand is nonnegative. The first term is the desired one-prime-power channel; the other two are composite-factor contamination. The three-factor products range over all multiplicative scales below \(x\), so this is positive but not sharply localized to a narrow interval around \(x\).

### 2. DERIVATION

For each distinct prime \(p_i\mid n\), the squarefree divisors in the Möbius sum either remove one factor of \(p_i\) or do not. Thus \(\Lambda_3(n)\) is the mixed backward difference of \(F(t_1,\ldots,t_r)=(\sum_i t_i\log p_i)^3\), evaluated at \(t_i=a_i\) with step size one in each coordinate. If \(r>3\), a mixed difference of total order greater than the degree is zero. For \(r=1\),
\[
\Lambda_3(p^a)=\bigl(a^3-(a-1)^3\bigr)(\log p)^3=(3a^2-3a+1)(\log p)^3>0.
\]
For \(n=p^a q^b\), put \(A=\log p\), \(B=\log q\), and \(u=(a-1)A+(b-1)B\). Expanding the two mixed differences gives
\[
\Lambda_3(n)=6uAB+3A^2B+3AB^2\ge0.
\]
For three distinct primes \(p^a q^b r^c\), the third mixed difference is \(6\log p\log q\log r>0\). These cases prove nonnegativity and the support assertion.

For a direct convolution derivation, work with absolutely convergent Dirichlet series for \(\Re s>1\), and write \(D_f(s)=\sum_n f(n)n^{-s}\). Then \(D_\Lambda=-\zeta'/\zeta\), \(D_{\mu*\log^2}=\zeta''/\zeta\), and \(D_{\mu*\log^3}=-\zeta'''/\zeta\). Multiplication of an arithmetic function by \(\log n\) corresponds to minus differentiation of its Dirichlet series. Consequently
\[
D_{(\mu*\log^2)\log}=-(\zeta''/\zeta)'=-\zeta'''/\zeta+\zeta''\zeta'/\zeta^2,
\qquad
D_{(\mu*\log^2)*\Lambda}=(\zeta''/\zeta)(-\zeta'/\zeta)=-\zeta''\zeta'/\zeta^2.
\]
Adding proves \(\Lambda_3=(\mu*\log^2)\log+(\mu*\log^2)*\Lambda\) by uniqueness of absolutely convergent Dirichlet series. Substituting \(\mu*\log^2=\Lambda\log+\Lambda*\Lambda\), and using \((\Lambda*\Lambda)(n)\log n=2((\Lambda\log)*\Lambda)(n)\), yields the boxed positive expansion. This proves the claimed identity exactly.

Also, summing the defining divisor identity \(\sum_{d\mid m}\Lambda_3(d)=\log^3m\) over \(m\le x\) and reversing the finite sums gives the exact lattice formula
\[
\boxed{\sum_{m\le x}\log^3m=\sum_{d\le x}\Lambda_3(d)\left\lfloor\frac{x}{d}\right\rfloor.}
\]
The floor function is the exact integer-lattice input. Since \(\Lambda_3\ge0\), the forward sum has only nonnegative terms. Recovering \(\Lambda_3\) from this divisor average, however, uses Möbius inversion.

For \(\Re s>1\), the Euler product and absolute convergence give
\[
\sum_{n\ge1}\frac{\Lambda_3(n)}{n^s}=\frac{-\zeta'''(s)}{\zeta(s)}.
\]
Its principal part at \(s=1\) begins \(6/(s-1)^3\), consistent with a leading summatory scale \(3x\log^2x\) under a suitable Tauberian theorem. That statement concerns \(S_3\), not \(\psi\), and does not provide an error term for \(\psi(x)-x\).

### 3. NUMERIC CHECK

The computation used a smallest-prime-factor sieve through \(N=10^6\), exact integer prime-power and Möbius classifications, and 50-decimal-digit logarithms and sums. It compared the Möbius-defined/local finite-difference \(S_3(x)\) against the independently accumulated positive convolution channels \(A+B+C\).

| x | direct \(S_3(x)\) | positive expansion \(A+B+C\) | absolute numerical residual | \(S_3(x)/(x\log^2x)\) | \(\psi(x)-x\) (diagnostic only) |
|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 10 | 42.936056559270196427059 | 42.936056559270196427059 | 8.5528471e-50 | 0.80982424906306 | -2.16798581949453101 |
| 100 | 3087.60465006450130669822 | 3087.60465006450130669822 | 2.7369111e-47 | 1.455895881874 | -5.95468877064260775 |
| 1000 | 88866.1922184329515090866 | 88866.1922184329515090866 | 5.2548692e-46 | 1.8623559245865 | -3.31908775282475974 |
| 10000 | 1783472.87535147860971872 | 1783472.87535147860971872 | 7.8472714e-44 | 2.1023990349639 | 13.3966932631147837 |
| 100000 | 29971933.8996359165916649 | 29971933.8996359165916649 | 5.9190847e-42 | 2.2612229262121 | 51.5640256579542509 |
| 1000000 | 452884484.460874522565064 | 452884484.460874522565064 | 4.3047889e-41 | 2.3727586434554 | -413.402504367077967 |

Direct \(\mu*\log^3\) checks at \(n=2,4,6,8,12,30,60,210,2310,30030,510510,999983\) agreed with the positive local formula to absolute residual at most \(8.57\times10^{-48}\); the zero cases with four or more distinct primes had only high-precision roundoff residual. The ratio trend is numerical evidence only and does not prove an asymptotic or an error bound.

### 4. ERROR ANALYSIS

This mechanism derives **no nontrivial bound for \(\psi(x)-x\)**. The exact lattice identity estimates a divisor-weighted average of \(\Lambda_3\), and the positive decomposition leaves the semiprime and three-factor channels mixed with the one-prime-power channel. To isolate the first channel by subtraction requires information about the other channels; inverting the floor/divisor sum uses the signed Möbius function. That is the precise loss point.

Without importing a separate prime-number theorem argument, the only direct elementary bound available here is the trivial one
\[
0\le\psi(x)=\sum_{n\le x}\Lambda(n)\le\sum_{n\le x}\log n=x\log x-x+O(\log x),
\]
so this gives at best \(|\psi(x)-x|=O(x\log x)\), which is weaker than M0. A Tauberian asymptotic for \(S_3\), even if established from the nonnegative coefficients and the boundary behavior of \(-\zeta'''/\zeta\), is not an error estimate for \(\psi\). No Tauberian step converting this cubic statistic into a prime-counting asymptotic with a rate was supplied.

### 5. FILTERS

- **F-LATTICE — PASS, with the dependency explicit.** The exact scale-average identity uses \(\lfloor x/d\rfloor\), the exact count of ordinary integers in the lattice. Replacing the ordinary integers by a generalized Beurling system removes this exact floor identity in its present form; the classical ordinary-lattice input is essential.
- **F-MULT — PASS.** Möbius inversion, \(\Lambda\), the convolution expansion, and the Dirichlet series all use unique factorization/Euler-product multiplicativity. The argument is not transferable unchanged to a Davenport–Heilbronn function without an Euler product.
- **F-GLOBAL — PASS only for the full prime system; finite-prime version FAILS.** The formula is over all prime powers and its exact lattice sum includes all integers. A finite prime set does not produce the exact integer lattice or a uniform passage to all primes; this pass proves no finite-prime approximation theorem.

### 6. MILESTONE REACHED

**Below M0 / no prime-counting milestone.** The mechanism proves an exact positive identity and verifies it numerically; it does not reproduce \(\psi(x)\sim x\), so it reaches none of M0–M6 for the requested \(\psi\)-error objective.

### 7. WHY

The obstacle is not a sign defect inside \(\Lambda_3\): its components are genuinely nonnegative. The obstacle is loss of identifiability. The measured total adds three distinct arithmetic channels, and positivity alone cannot subtract the composite channels to recover the prime-power channel. The forward exact lattice relation is therefore an average over divisors, while its inverse is signed. This is intrinsic to this cubic decomposition as used here; a new positive identity that localizes or separately controls the composite channels remains unexcluded.

### 8. LESSON

The next candidate must do more than raise the power of \(\log n\) or produce another nonnegative generalized von Mangoldt function. It must either (i) yield a positive scale-localized statistic whose prime channel is separated from products of multiple prime powers, or (ii) prove a quantitative bound for those composite channels that survives without signed Möbius inversion. State explicitly which mechanism uses exact integer floor counts and where multiplicativity enters.

### 9. FAILURE CLASS

**New class 1: positive higher-order divisor weight does not isolate primes.** The finite identity and numerical data are established; the conclusion is only that this particular positive cubic transform supplies no prime-counting error. This is not a no-go theorem for all higher-order or positive scale-separation methods.

## Pass 2 — Exponentially smoothed Chebyshev divisor identity

### 1. MECHANISM

For integer \(X\ge2\), define the positive finite statistic
\[
A_X=\sum_{n\le X}e^{-n/X}\log n.
\]
The divisor identity \(\log n=\sum_{d\mid n}\Lambda(d)\) gives the exact positive expansion
\[
\boxed{A_X=\sum_{d\le X}\Lambda(d)K_X(d),\qquad
K_X(d)=\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor X/d\rfloor}e^{-kd/X}
=\frac{e^{-d/X}(1-e^{-\lfloor X/d\rfloor d/X})}{1-e^{-d/X}}>0.}
\]
Unlike Pass 1, this uses the first-order Chebyshev identity directly and introduces a smooth positive weight. It does not sharply isolate the endpoint prime scale: for \(d/X=t\to0\), \(K_X(d)=(1-e^{-1})X/d+O(1)\), so small divisors carry large weight.

### 2. DERIVATION

Unique factorization gives \(\log n=\sum_{p^j\mid n}\log p=\sum_{d\mid n}\Lambda(d)\). Substitute into \(A_X\), interchange the finite sums, and write each multiple of \(d\) as \(n=dk\):
\[
A_X=\sum_{n\le X}e^{-n/X}\sum_{d\mid n}\Lambda(d)
=\sum_{d\le X}\Lambda(d)\sum_{k\le X/d}e^{-dk/X}.
\]
The inner sum is a finite geometric series, giving \(K_X(d)\) above. This is exact; every right-hand term is nonnegative.

To estimate the left side, set \(f_X(t)=e^{-t/X}\log t\). On \([1,X]\), it is nonnegative and unimodal, with total variation \(O(\log X)\). The first Euler summation estimate therefore gives
\[
A_X=\int_1^X e^{-t/X}\log t\,dt+O(\log X).
\]
After \(u=t/X\), the integral is
\[
X\int_{1/X}^1 e^{-u}(\log X+\log u)\,du
=X\left((1-e^{-1})\log X+c_1\right)+O(\log X),
\quad c_1=\int_0^1e^{-u}\log u\,du=-0.79659959929705313428\ldots.
\]
The \(O(\log X)\) absorbs the lower-end expansion and the sum-integral discrepancy. Thus the positive prime-power transform has a rigorously derived weighted-statistic main term with an \(O(\log X)\) error.

For \(d\le X/10\), write \(t=d/X\) and \(m=\lfloor1/t\rfloor\). Then \(mt=1+O(t)\), \(1-e^{-t}=t+O(t^2)\), and
\[
K_X(d)=\frac{1-e^{-1}}{t}+O(1)=(1-e^{-1})\frac{X}{d}+O(1).
\]
This proves the broad harmonic weighting responsible for leakage from smaller scales.

### 3. NUMERIC CHECK

The computation used an exact smallest-prime-factor sieve through \(10^6\), hence exact identification of all primes and prime powers, and 50-digit mpmath logarithms, exponentials, and summation. Both sides of the finite identity were independently accumulated.

| X | integer-side \(A_X\) | positive prime-power side | absolute residual at 50 digits | \(\psi(X)-X\) (diagnostic only) |
|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 10 | 7.9112079610242768397008744 | 7.9112079610242768397008744 | 0 at displayed precision | -2.16798581949453101 |
| 100 | 213.20556955431691945062255 | 213.20556955431691945062255 | 0 at displayed precision | -5.95468877064260775 |
| 1000 | 3572.1237304954215240244064 | 3572.1237304954215240244064 | 0 at displayed precision | -3.31908775282475974 |
| 10000 | 50257.072080633207621784279 | 50257.072080633207621784279 | 0 at displayed precision | 13.3966932631147837 |
| 100000 | 648098.76455509522828596775 | 648098.76455509522828596775 | 0 at displayed precision | 51.5640256579542509 |
| 1000000 | 7936472.1152642891946843625 | 7936472.1152642891946843625 | 0 at displayed precision | -413.402504367077967 |

At \(X=10^6\), the positive prime-power contributions divide by scale as follows:

| divisor scale d | contribution | fraction of A_X |
|---|---:|---:|
| \([1,X/10]\) | 6863737.72759055049348502 | 0.8648348570883858 |
| \((X/10,X/2]\) | 834299.32550540824486538 | 0.1051221894802343 |
| \((X/2,X]\) | 238435.062168330456333959 | 0.03004295343137994 |

The integer-side sum minus its leading integral model \(X((1-e^{-1})\log X+c_1)\), divided by \(\log X\), was 0.3169195351, 0.2837076753, and 0.2504546692 at \(X=10^3,10^4,10^6\), respectively. This checks the scale of the \(O(\log X)\) remainder numerically; the bound itself follows from the variation estimate, not the data.

### 4. ERROR ANALYSIS

The mechanism proves
\[
A_X=X((1-e^{-1})\log X+c_1)+O(\log X)
\]
for the smoothed statistic, but not a comparable estimate for the unweighted \(\psi(X)\). Since \(K_X(d)\ge e^{-d/X}\ge e^{-1}\) for every \(d\le X\), positivity gives only
\[
\psi(X)=\sum_{d\le X}\Lambda(d)\le eA_X=O(X\log X),
\]
and therefore \(|\psi(X)-X|=O(X\log X)\). This is below M0. The exact loss is unsmoothing: the kernel behaves like \(X/d\) for small \(d\), rather than approximating the indicator \(1_{d\le X}\). Numerically, the last half of the divisor range contributes only 3.00% at \(X=10^6\); most of the statistic is a harmonic accumulation over earlier scales. Recovering the terminal unweighted sum needs a new positive localization or a quantitative inversion/Tauberian argument, neither of which is supplied by this identity.

### 5. FILTERS

- **F-LATTICE — PASS, explicitly used.** The left sum and the cutoff \(k\le\lfloor X/d\rfloor\) use exact ordinary-integer lattice counts. Its \(O(\log X)\) estimate uses the ordinary real-variable sum-integral comparison and would not transfer unchanged to a Beurling integer system.
- **F-MULT — PASS.** The identity \(\log n=\sum_{d\mid n}\Lambda(d)\) depends essentially on unique factorization; without the Euler product there is no corresponding von Mangoldt divisor decomposition.
- **F-GLOBAL — PASS only with all prime powers; finite-prime version FAILS.** Omitting prime powers changes the divisor identity for integers containing omitted prime factors. No uniform finite-prime approximation is proved.

### 6. MILESTONE REACHED

**Below M0 for \(\psi\).** The method gives an \(O(\log X)\) error for its own smoothed statistic and only \(\psi(X)=O(X\log X)\) from positivity. It does not prove the prime number theorem or any M0–M6 prime-counting error.

### 7. WHY

The positive smoothing retains multiplicativity and lattice exactness, but the induced kernel is broad on logarithmic scales and strongly favors small divisors. Its exact \(O(\log X)\) remainder is attached to the smoothed integer statistic, not to the endpoint count. This is intrinsic to this kernel. A positive weight whose divisor transform is approximately constant near \(d=X\) and suppresses small \(d\), while preserving an exact lattice formula, remains untested.

### 8. LESSON

Before pursuing a positive smoothed divisor identity, derive its induced prime-power kernel and quantify the contribution from dyadic or logarithmic scale bands. Require an endpoint-localized kernel, not merely a rapidly decaying weight in the original integer variable. If unsmoothing is proposed, state the exact positive theorem that controls the inverse and its loss.

### 9. FAILURE CLASS

**New class 2: positive exponential divisor smoothing is dominated by small scales.** The exact identity and \(O(\log X)\) weighted-statistic estimate are proved; the failure to reach M0 is the precise lack of a positive endpoint-localization bound. This is not a no-go theorem for all positive smoothing kernels.

## Pass 3 — Positive lcm divisor-incidence Gram energy

### 1. MECHANISM

For integer X≥2, the prime-factor identity log n = sum over d|n of Lambda(d) gives the exact quadratic identity

    Q_X := sum_{n≤X} (log n)²
         = sum_{d,e≤X} Lambda(d)Lambda(e) floor(X/lcm(d,e)).

The kernel K_X(d,e)=floor(X/lcm(d,e)) is positive semidefinite because

    K_X(d,e) = sum_{n≤X} 1_{d|n} 1_{e|n}.

The weighted matrix A_X(d,e)=Lambda(d)Lambda(e)K_X(d,e) is the Gram matrix of vectors v_d(n)=Lambda(d)·1_{d|n}; its all-ones quadratic energy is Q_X. For distinct primes p,q>sqrt(X), K_X(p,q)=0, so the principal block on those primes is diagonal. It is not isolated from smaller divisors: a smaller prime power e can share a multiple with p whenever pe≤X.

### 2. DERIVATION

If n=product_p p^{a_p}, then sum_{d|n} Lambda(d)=sum_p a_p log(p)=log(n). Squaring and summing over n≤X gives a finite double divisor sum. Reversing the finite sums, the number of n≤X divisible by both d and e is exactly floor(X/lcm(d,e)); this proves the displayed identity and nonnegativity term by term.

For f(t)=log²(t), monotonicity on [1,infinity) gives

    0 ≤ sum_{n=2}^X f(n) − integral_1^X f(t)dt ≤ f(X)−f(1)=log²(X).

Since the integral is X(log²(X)−2log(X)+2)−2,

    −2 ≤ Q_X − X(log²(X)−2log(X)+2) ≤ log²(X)−2.

This is an estimate for the integer-side second moment, not a prime sum.

### 3. NUMERIC CHECK

An exact smallest-prime-factor sieve classified prime powers through 10⁶; logarithmic weights and sums used 60-digit Decimal precision. The double sum was evaluated by grouping different prime bases through T(Y)=sum_{n≤Y}log(n), then explicitly restoring same-base pairs. This avoids quadratic enumeration while preserving the exact identity. Residuals below are numerical rounding residuals.

| 10,000 | 684137.299218953618391095 | 684137.299218953618391095 | 2.200e−53 | 81090.292259009933257808 | 0.118529266496 | 13.396693263115 | 40.408981933591 |
| 100,000 | 11152224.450591062622884683 | 11152224.450591062622884683 | −5.000e−51 | 1047014.393582873223269041 | 0.093883906141 | 51.564025657954 | 64.267389113280 |
| 1,000,000 | 165237404.289105615459676425 | 165237404.289105615459676425 | −1.490e−49 | 12791182.495654353792343107 | 0.077410938224 | −413.402504367078 | 93.427811835288 |

At every listed X, the displayed integer-side remainder lies in the proved interval [−2, log²(X)−2]. The measured high-prime diagonal fraction falls from 23.06% at X=10 to 7.74% at X=10⁶. This finite trend is diagnostic only and proves no asymptotic for primes.

### 4. ERROR ANALYSIS

This construction gives no nontrivial bound for psi(X)−X. Positivity yields only 0≤D_X≤Q_X, with Q_X of order X log²(X); that is an upper bound for one component, not a lower bound identifying it. The high-prime principal block is diagonal, but its cross-block entries with smaller prime-power divisors are nonzero, and the all-ones vector includes them. No projection or Schur-complement estimate controls these terms. Independently, the direct elementary estimate psi(X)≤sum_{n≤X}log(n)=O(X log(X)) remains below M0. The precise loss is channel extraction: PSD controls total energy but does not quantify the selected prime diagonal.

### 5. FILTERS

- F-LATTICE — PASS: the kernel counts ordinary integers divisible by both d and e, exactly floor(X/lcm(d,e)).
- F-MULT — PASS: log(n)=sum_{d|n}Lambda(d) depends essentially on unique factorization.
- F-GLOBAL — PASS only for the complete prime-power system: omitting prime powers breaks the logarithmic divisor identity for integers containing omitted prime factors; no uniform finite-prime approximation is shown.

### 6. MILESTONE REACHED

Below M0. The exact positive identity and integer-side estimate are established, but no PNT consequence or prime-counting error estimate follows.

### 7. WHY

The divisibility kernel has genuine PSD structure and a diagonal high-prime principal block, but PSD alone does not make the all-ones energy equal to that block. Large-prime channels couple to lower divisors. This limitation is established for this statistic only; a different test vector or positive projection with controlled cross terms remains open.

### 8. LESSON

For a positive Gram construction, specify the target coordinate subspace and quantify its principal block and every cross-block term before drawing a prime-counting conclusion. A next candidate needs a positive vector or projection that suppresses low-divisor coupling with an explicit uniform error.

### 9. FAILURE CLASS

New class 3: positive lcm-incidence Gram energy does not isolate the prime diagonal. The exact identity, PSD representation, and finite numerical checks are established. This is not a no-go theorem for all Gram forms, sieve weights, or positive projections.
## Pass 4 — Two-dimensional sum-of-two-squares divisor transform

### 1. MECHANISM

Let r₂(n) be the number of ordered integer pairs (a,b) with a²+b²=n. It is an exact count of nonzero lattice points on the circle of squared radius n, and r₂(n)≥0. Define

    A_X = sum_{1≤n≤X} r₂(n) log(n).

Using log(n)=sum_{d|n}Lambda(d) gives the exact positive transform

    A_X = sum_{d≤X} Lambda(d) K_X(d),
    K_X(d) = sum_{k≤X/d} r₂(dk) ≥ 0.

This is not Chebyshev's one-dimensional integer count or the lcm Gram statistic: its lattice side is the disk in Z² and its divisor kernel is a norm-lattice correlation. For a rational prime p>sqrt(X), every k≤X/p is coprime to p. The sum-of-two-squares factorization theorem gives the exact endpoint channel

    K_X(p) = 2 sum_{k≤X/p} r₂(k), if p≡1 mod 4;
    K_X(p) = 0,                         if p≡3 mod 4.

Thus the p>sqrt(X) prime channel sees split primes p≡1 mod 4 with a positive weight. The weight still ranges over the whole interval (sqrt(X),X], and the remaining d-channels are present in A_X.

### 2. DERIVATION

For each n, unique factorization gives log(n)=sum_{d|n}Lambda(d). Therefore

    sum_{n≤X} r₂(n)log(n)
      = sum_{n≤X} r₂(n) sum_{d|n}Lambda(d)
      = sum_{d≤X}Lambda(d) sum_{k≤X/d}r₂(dk),

by reversing finite sums and writing n=dk. Every term on the right is nonnegative.

For the endpoint prime formula, p>sqrt(X) and k≤X/p imply k<p. Hence gcd(p,k)=1. The representation count is multiplicative on coprime inputs, with r₂(p)=8 for p≡1 mod 4 and r₂(p)=0 for p≡3 mod 4; consequently r₂(pk)=r₂(p)r₂(k)/4, giving the displayed factors 2 and 0.

There is also an explicit lattice asymptotic. Let N(t)=sum_{n≤t}r₂(n), the number of nonzero integer lattice points in the disk of radius sqrt(t). Unit squares centered at lattice points have circumradius c=sqrt(2)/2, so comparison with disks of radii sqrt(t)±c gives

    |N(t)−pi*t| ≤ pi*sqrt(2)*sqrt(t) + pi/2 + 1, for t≥1.

Partial summation yields

    A_X = N(X)log(X) − integral_1^X N(t)/t dt
        = pi*X*(log(X)−1)+pi + E_X,

with the explicit bound

    |E_X| ≤ pi*sqrt(2)*sqrt(X)*log(X)
             + 2*pi*sqrt(2)*sqrt(X)
             + (pi+2)*log(X).

The looser second term absorbs the negative constant from integrating sqrt(t); the inequality holds for all X≥2.

### 3. NUMERIC CHECK

An exact lattice enumeration on 0≤a,b≤1000, with sign multiplicities applied exactly, computed r₂(n) through 10⁶. Prime powers were classified by an exact sieve and logarithmic sums used 55-digit Decimal precision. The transform was independently summed over the integer norm counts and over all prime-power channels. The circle-count error bound was checked at every listed cutoff.

The explicit circle-count error bound passed for all six X values. At X=10⁶, 83.27% of the divisor-channel mass is at d≤X/10 and 4.96% at d>X/2. The p>sqrt(X) prime channel is about 52.13% of the total, but is weighted across a logarithmically broad interval. These are numerical diagnostics, not prime-distribution proofs.

### 4. ERROR ANALYSIS

The lattice calculation proves A_X=pi X(log(X)−1)+pi+O(sqrt(X)log(X)) with the explicit bound above. It does not prove a bound for psi(X)−X or a prime number theorem. The divisor expansion gives an upper bound for any selected positive channel, A_channel≤A_X, but no lower bound for the split-prime channel. Subtracting the remaining prime-power channels would reintroduce a signed operation, and their contribution is not controlled to a smaller error by the circle estimate. The observed 52% channel fraction cannot replace such an argument. The mechanism therefore remains below M0. The exact loss is decomposition-to-channel inference: a precise lattice asymptotic for the total norm sum does not identify one Euler-factor channel.

### 5. FILTERS

- F-LATTICE — PASS: the statistic uses the exact Z² lattice and its disk point count; the stated circle bound comes from unit-square containment, a property absent from arbitrary Beurling integer systems.
- F-MULT — PASS: the divisor expansion uses the rational identity log(n)=sum_{d|n}Lambda(d), and the endpoint prime classification uses unique factorization in the Gaussian integers.
- F-GLOBAL — PASS only with all rational prime powers: truncating them breaks the divisor identity for norms with omitted prime factors, and no uniform finite-prime passage is proved.

### 6. MILESTONE REACHED

Below M0 for psi. The norm-lattice statistic has an explicit asymptotic with error O(sqrt(X)log(X)), but no asymptotic or error term for the prime-counting function follows.

### 7. WHY

The two-dimensional geometry sharply estimates the total number of Gaussian integers by norm, while multiplicativity exposes a real split-prime channel above sqrt(X). However, the total is still a positive sum over all divisor channels; no positivity principle forces the endpoint prime channel to carry a specified share. The small-d mass remains large, and the split-prime weight is broad in logarithmic scale. The loss is intrinsic to using only this total norm statistic, not to all higher-dimensional lattices.

### 8. LESSON

For future lattice-count mechanisms, compute the exact norm-factor channel at the endpoint, including which prime splitting types survive, then compare its kernel with the full positive transform by logarithmic scale bands. A useful new candidate must control the complementary divisor channels positively rather than subtract them from the total.

### 9. FAILURE CLASS

New class 4: positive norm-lattice divisor transforms expose a split-prime endpoint channel but do not isolate it from lower divisor scales. The exact identity, explicit lattice error bound, and numerical checks are established; this is not a no-go theorem for other norm forms or projected lattice observables.
## Pass 5 — Primitive Gaussian norm lattice

### 1. MECHANISM

Let r₂*(n) count ordered integer pairs (a,b) with a²+b²=n and gcd(|a|,|b|)=1. Define

    A*_X = sum_{n≤X} r₂*(n) log(n).

Since log(n)=sum_{d|n}Lambda(d), this has the exact positive transform

    A*_X = sum_{d≤X}Lambda(d)K*_X(d),
    K*_X(d)=sum_{k≤X/d}r₂*(dk) ≥ 0.

Unlike Pass 4, the lattice observable discards every nonprimitive Gaussian integer; this is a genuine coprimality sieve, not just a change of notation. For rational primes p>sqrt(X), k≤X/p<p and gcd(p,k)=1. Primitive sum-of-two-squares factorization gives K*_X(p)=2 sum_{k≤X/p}r₂*(k) for p≡1 mod 4 and zero for p≡3 mod 4. Thus the prime channel remains restricted to split primes, but its weight is altered by the primitive filter.

### 2. DERIVATION

The divisor transform follows exactly as before by substituting log(n)=sum_{d|n}Lambda(d), reversing the finite sums, and writing n=dk. Every term is nonnegative.

For the lattice asymptotic, let N(t) count nonzero integer points in the disk a²+b²≤t, and P(t)=sum_{n≤t}r₂*(n) count primitive nonzero points. Mobius inversion of the coordinate gcd gives the exact finite formula

    P(t)=sum_{d≤sqrt(t)}mu(d)N(t/d²).

Pass 4 proved |N(u)−pi*u|≤a*sqrt(u)+b for u≥1, where a=pi*sqrt(2) and b=pi/2+1. Put m=floor(sqrt(t)). Replacing N by its area term contributes at most

    a*sqrt(t)*sum_{d≤m}1/d + b*m.

Replacing the finite sum pi*t*sum_{d≤m}mu(d)/d² by pi*t/ζ(2) costs at most pi*t*sum_{d>m}1/d²≤2*pi*sqrt(t), because m≥sqrt(t)/2. Also sum_{d≤m}1/d≤1+0.5log(t)≤1.5log(2t), and sqrt(t)≤sqrt(t)log(2t)/log(2). Hence

    |P(t)−(pi/ζ(2))*t| ≤ C*sqrt(t)*log(2t),
    C = 1.5*pi*sqrt(2) + (pi/2+1+2*pi)/log(2) < 20.

Since pi/ζ(2)=6/pi, partial summation gives

    A*_X = (6/pi)*X*(log(X)−1) + 6/pi + E*_X,

and the explicit bound

    |E*_X| ≤ 20*sqrt(X)*log(2X)*(log(X)+2).

Indeed, the endpoint term is bounded by 20*sqrt(X)*log(2X)*log(X), while the integral error is at most 20*integral_1^X t^(−1/2)log(2t)dt ≤40*sqrt(X)*log(2X). The Mobius signs occur in proving the primitive-point density, even though the observable and divisor-channel sum are nonnegative.

### 3. NUMERIC CHECK

An exact lattice enumeration over 0≤a,b≤1000 used gcd(a,b)=1 and exact sign multiplicities to obtain r₂*(n) through 10⁶. Rational prime powers were classified by an exact sieve; logarithms and sums used 55-digit Decimal precision. The integer norm sum and positive prime-power transform were independently accumulated.

At X=10⁶, the p>sqrt(X) prime channel accounts for 60.99% of the total, while the d≤X/10 band accounts for 75.53%; these are overlapping classifications, not disjoint channels, because the former is indexed by prime base and the latter by divisor size. Finite data do not establish limiting proportions.

### 4. ERROR ANALYSIS

The mechanism proves a primitive Gaussian lattice sum asymptotic with error at most 20*sqrt(X)*log(2X)*(log(X)+2). It yields no estimate for psi(X)−X and no PNT for rational primes. Positivity says the selected p>sqrt(X) channel is at most A*_X; it gives no lower bound for that channel. The other prime-power channels remain in the same total, and subtracting them would reintroduce signs. The primitive density proof itself uses Mobius inversion, whose absolute tail is harmless for the lattice density but does not invert the Lambda-channel sum into a prime estimate. Milestone: below M0.

### 5. FILTERS

- F-LATTICE — PASS: the mechanism uses exact primitive points of Z² and the disk-area discrepancy; generic Beurling systems do not supply this coordinate lattice or gcd sieve.
- F-MULT — PASS: rational log-divisor factorization and primitive Gaussian norm factorization are essential. The density calculation explicitly uses the Mobius function.
- F-GLOBAL — PASS only with all prime powers: omitting prime powers breaks log(n)=sum_{d|n}Lambda(d); no uniform finite-prime limit is proved.

### 6. MILESTONE REACHED

Below M0 for psi. The primitive norm-lattice statistic has an explicit second-moment-type asymptotic, but it does not give a prime-counting asymptotic or error.

### 7. WHY

The primitive condition removes common Gaussian content and measurably increases the relative endpoint prime channel. It does not make the divisor transform endpoint-localized: three quarters of the computed total still comes from d≤X/10, and the positive total supplies no lower bound for any selected channel. The failure is in extracting a component, not in estimating the primitive lattice count. This conclusion is intrinsic to this all-positive total; a different primitive observable or controlled projection remains open.

### 8. LESSON

When adding a sieve condition to a lattice observable, quantify both its effect on the target channel and its effect on scale localization. A larger target fraction is not enough; the next construction must yield a provable positive lower estimate or a positive identity that eliminates complementary channels without Mobius inversion.

### 9. FAILURE CLASS

New class 5: primitive-point filtering strengthens a split-prime channel but leaves substantial divisor-scale mixing and no positive lower bound. The finite identity and explicit primitive lattice error bound are established; this is not a general no-go for positive sieve or lattice methods.
## Pass 6 — Four-dimensional norm lattice and Jacobi r₄ transform

### 1. MECHANISM

Let r₄(n) count ordered integer quadruples (a,b,c,d) with a²+b²+c²+d²=n. Jacobi's four-square formula states

    r₄(n)=8 sum_{q|n, 4 does not divide q} q.

Define A₄(X)=sum_{n≤X}r₄(n)log(n). From log(n)=sum_{m|n}Lambda(m),

    A₄(X)=sum_{m≤X}Lambda(m)K₄,X(m),
    K₄,X(m)=sum_{k≤X/m}r₄(mk) ≥0.

For an odd prime p>sqrt(X), k≤X/p<p, so p does not divide k. Jacobi's divisor formula then gives the exact local identity r₄(pk)=(p+1)r₄(k), hence K₄,X(p)=(p+1)sum_{k≤X/p}r₄(k). Unlike Passes 4–5, this channel includes every odd rational prime without a p mod 4 split condition. Its weight still spans the full large-prime interval.

### 2. DERIVATION

Substitute log(n)=sum_{m|n}Lambda(m) into A₄ and write n=mk. Reversing the finite sums proves the positive divisor transform exactly.

For p odd and gcd(p,k)=1, Jacobi's formula yields

    r₄(pk)/8 = sum_{q|pk,4∤q}q
              = (1+p) sum_{q|k,4∤q}q
              = (p+1)r₄(k)/8.

If p>sqrt(X), every k≤X/p is less than p and the coprimality condition holds.

For a geometric asymptotic, put V₄=pi²/2, the volume of the unit 4-ball, and let N₄(t)=sum_{n≤t}r₄(n) count nonzero lattice points in the radius-sqrt(t) ball. The unit cubes centered at Z⁴ points have covering radius 1. Comparing the union of cubes with balls of radii sqrt(t)±1 gives

    |N₄(t)−V₄t²| ≤ B(t),
    B(t)=V₄(4t^(3/2)+6t+4sqrt(t)+1)+1, for t≥1.

Partial summation gives

    A₄(X)=V₄X²(log(X)−1/2)+V₄/2+E₄(X),

and the explicit error bound

    |E₄(X)| ≤ B(X)log(X)
      + V₄[(8/3)(X^(3/2)−1)+6(X−1)+8(sqrt(X)−1)+log(X)]
      + log(X).

This is O(X^(3/2)log(X)); it estimates the total four-dimensional lattice statistic.

### 3. NUMERIC CHECK

The exact r₄ values through 10⁶ were computed by the divisor formula with integer accumulation; the rational prime powers were classified by an exact sieve; logarithms and weighted sums used 55-digit Decimal precision. The lattice sum and prime-power transform were accumulated independently. A separate direct enumeration of Z⁴ points confirmed Jacobi's r₄ formula for every n≤10 and n≤100 (zero mismatches).

### 4. ERROR ANALYSIS

The method proves A₄(X)=V₄X²(log(X)−1/2)+V₄/2+E₄(X), with the explicit bound above. It provides no nontrivial estimate for psi(X)−X: the direct bound remains O(Xlog(X)), below M0. Positivity gives only that the selected prime channel is no larger than A₄(X). The exact r₄ local factor p+1 makes every odd prime visible, but the all-positive total contains lower-d channels with 82.98% of its mass at X=10⁶; no proven inequality separates the endpoint contribution from them. The loss is channel extraction from the total norm statistic, not the four-dimensional lattice estimate.

### 5. FILTERS

- F-LATTICE — PASS: the mechanism uses the exact Z⁴ lattice and a unit-cube covering discrepancy unavailable in general Beurling systems.
- F-MULT — PASS: Jacobi's r₄ divisor formula and log(n)=sum_{d|n}Lambda(d) expose the prime-power channels essentially.
- F-GLOBAL — PASS only with all rational prime powers; finite truncation breaks the logarithmic divisor identity and no uniform limit is proved.

### 6. MILESTONE REACHED

Below M0 for psi. The four-dimensional norm sum has an explicit O(X^(3/2)log(X)) remainder, but no prime-number-theorem consequence follows.

### 7. WHY

Increasing dimension changes the exact endpoint local factor from the split-prime selection of r₂ to p+1 for every odd p. It does not sharpen the divisor kernel enough: the small-d bands still dominate, and positivity supplies no lower bound on the prime channel. The broad-scale loss repeats Pass 4's norm-lattice root cause in a distinct r₄ mechanism. A projected or differenced lattice statistic that cancels lower divisors positively remains untested.

### 8. LESSON

Higher-dimensional representation formulas can improve which prime types appear without improving scale localization. For a new norm form, derive its endpoint local factor and asymptotic divisor kernel, then quantify low-d mass. The next mechanism must change the test or combine lattice identities so complementary channels are removed by a positive identity rather than dimension alone.

### 9. FAILURE CLASS

Existing class 4, second instance: positive norm-lattice divisor transforms leak across scales. Pass 6 gives a new all-odd-prime local factor and an explicit four-dimensional error bound, but no new obstruction class or prime-counting milestone.

## Pass 7 — Truncated Möbius-log square sieve

### 1. MECHANISM

For an integer cutoff R≥2, define

    lambda_R(d) = mu(d) log(R/d)/log(R), if d≤R is squarefree;
                 = 0, otherwise,
    w_R(n) = (sum_{d|n} lambda_R(d))² ≥ 0,
    Q_R(X) = sum_{n≤X} log(n) w_R(n).

This is a concrete Selberg-style divisor square, not the optimized Selberg sieve weights. Unlike the preceding norm-lattice totals, it uses a Möbius-designed quadratic filter on the ordinary integer lattice. If the least prime factor of n is greater than R, then the only divisor d≤R is 1, and w_R(n)=1. Thus the statistic assigns identical weight to every prime p>R and every composite whose prime factors all exceed R.

Writing q=[d,e] and M=floor(X/q), the exact lattice identity is

    Q_R(X) = sum_{d,e≤R} lambda_R(d)lambda_R(e) S_X([d,e]),
    S_X(q) = sum_{k≤X/q} log(qk) = M log(q) + log(M!).

The square is nonnegative termwise in the original n-sum; the finite bilinear expansion has signed coefficients.

### 2. DERIVATION

Expand w_R(n) as the finite double sum over d,e|n. The condition d|n and e|n is exactly [d,e]|n; writing n=qk gives Q_R(X)=sum_{d,e}lambda_R(d)lambda_R(e)S_X([d,e]).

Let

    G_R = sum_{d,e≤R} lambda_R(d)lambda_R(e)/[d,e],
    L_R = sum_{d≤R} |lambda_R(d)|.

For X≥4R², every q=[d,e]≤R² gives M=floor(X/q)≥4. Put y=X/q=M+δ, 0≤δ<1. Stirling's formula with remainder gives

    S_X(q) - y(log(X)-1)
      = M log(M/y) - δ log(X) + δ + 0.5 log(2πM) + η,
      0<η<1/(12M).

Since |M log(M/y)|≤δ, for the tested X≥100 this yields the explicit safe bound

    |S_X(q) - (X/q)(log(X)-1)| ≤ 2 log(2X).

Summing the finite bilinear expansion therefore proves

    |Q_R(X) - X(log(X)-1)G_R| ≤ 2 log(2X) L_R².

This estimates the all-integer log statistic. It does not estimate the Mangoldt-weighted version. For

    B_R(X) = sum_{n≤X} Lambda(n) w_R(n),

the exact divisor expansion instead involves

    C_X(q) = sum_{n≤X, q|n} Lambda(n).

Because d,e are squarefree, q=[d,e] is squarefree. Then C_X(1)=psi(X); if q=p is prime, C_X(p)=sum_{p^k≤X}log(p); and if q has at least two prime factors, C_X(q)=0, since no prime power is divisible by q. In particular the exact expansion contains the unknown target psi(X) with coefficient lambda_R(1)²=1; it is not psi(floor(X/q)).

Pointwise, w_R(p^k)=1 for p>R and w_R(p^k)=(log(p)/log(R))² for p≤R. Hence

    B_R(X) = sum_{p>R, p^k≤X} log(p)
              + (1/log²(R)) sum_{p≤R, p^k≤X} log³(p)
            = psi(X) - sum_{p≤R, p^k≤X} log(p)[1-(log(p)/log(R))²].

The small-prime correction is explicit and finite, but no independent estimate for B_R(X) is supplied by the log-lattice asymptotic for Q_R(X).

### 3. NUMERIC CHECK

An exact smallest-prime-factor/Möbius sieve classified all integers and prime powers through 10⁶. The lambda coefficients, factorial-log bilinear transform, restricted Mangoldt sums, and psi sums were evaluated at 60-digit mpmath precision. The all-integer square was independently accumulated in double precision from its pointwise weights; its direct sum agrees with the high-precision bilinear value to floating-point rounding. The exact Mangoldt channel and its restricted-prime-power expansion agree to at least 58 decimal places.

| X | R=floor(X^(1/3)) | Q_R(X) | X(logX−1)G_R | observed error | explicit error bound | prime>R share | R-rough composite share | small-factor share | B_R(X) | psi(X) |
|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 100 | 4 | 193.578588480870 | 193.091221571873 | 0.487366909 | 30.89576313 | 0.4232732120 | 0.1895816991 | 0.3871450888 | 89.291530425585 | 94.045311229357 |
| 1,000 | 10 | 2014.004290744930 | 2015.659257715791 | −1.654966971 | 127.81451032 | 0.4721430644 | 0.1963749337 | 0.3314820019 | 980.955777148256 | 996.680912247175 |
| 10,000 | 21 | 22177.347807432157 | 22183.362726401555 | −6.014918969 | 421.80922176 | 0.4454952802 | 0.1880913085 | 0.3664134113 | 9980.131468747669 | 10013.396693263115 |
| 100,000 | 46 | 233638.211292755761 | 233644.940938810781 | −6.729646055 | 1505.57391154 | 0.4265067715 | 0.2129047873 | 0.3605884412 | 99984.393615694741 | 100051.564025657954 |
| 1,000,000 | 100 | 2428197.182935649 | 2428201.503095707 | −4.320160058 | 5529.95193917 | 0.4111694279 | 0.2264252723 | 0.3624052998 | 999455.765720061977 | 999586.597495632922 |

The three lattice mass shares partition Q_R(X): primes above R, composites with least prime factor above R, and integers with a prime factor at most R. At X=10⁶, the rough-composite share is 22.64%, over half of the prime-above-R share. These finite proportions do not assert an asymptotic.

### 4. ERROR ANALYSIS

The proved explicit estimate is for Q_R(X), with the bound 2log(2X)L_R²; it is not an estimate for psi. The exact pointwise identity w_R(n)=1 for every R-rough integer means the lattice statistic cannot distinguish primes from rough composites. Replacing log(n) by Lambda(n) removes composite support, but its exact divisor expansion has the term psi(X) itself and the remaining small-prime correction; using it to estimate psi without a separate source of information is circular. The computation confirms both identities but supplies no such independent estimate. Thus no nontrivial bound for psi(X)−X follows; the only bound from this candidate remains the trivial O(Xlog(X)), below M0. The exact loss is the incompatibility, in this candidate, between an integer-lattice statistic with a tractable floor/lcm expansion and an observable supported only on prime powers.

### 5. FILTERS

- F-LATTICE — PASS for Q_R: its expansion counts ordinary multiples with the exact floor floor(X/[d,e]), and the stated estimate uses Stirling for the ordinary integers. This does not transfer unchanged to a Beurling system.
- F-MULT — PASS: the sieve uses mu on squarefree divisors and the Lambda version uses the exact prime-power support from unique factorization. The issue is not absence of multiplicativity; it is that the lattice-evaluable and prime-supported statistics differ.
- F-GLOBAL — FAIL for fixed R; unresolved for R=floor(X^(1/3)): a fixed finite set of sieve primes leaves every integer rough relative to that set at weight one. Letting R grow changes the finite filter with X, and no uniform limit or prime-error theorem is proved.

### 6. MILESTONE REACHED

Below M0 for psi. An explicit asymptotic and error bound hold for Q_R(X), but neither a prime-counting asymptotic nor any nontrivial error for psi(X) follows.

### 7. WHY

The Möbius-log square successfully makes a nonnegative sieve weight, but its support cutoff creates exact blindness to all integers with no prime factor at most R. This is the parity-barrier shape in this particular weight: prime and rough-composite inputs share the same sieve coefficient. Applying Lambda removes those composites, but the only exact bilinear identity then has psi(X) as a diagonal term, so it has not converted the statistic into known lattice data. This is a new, explicitly verified mechanism-level obstruction, not a theorem that every positive sieve fails.

### 8. LESSON

For a sieve-square candidate, audit both observables before claiming prime information: (i) the all-integer version, which can use exact lattice counts, and (ii) the Lambda-supported version, which removes composite support. Derive the restricted-multiple sum C_X(q) exactly; never replace it by psi(X/q). A next sieve mechanism must provide an independent lattice/analytic evaluation of the Lambda-weighted form and a weight that separates rough composites without signed parity information.

### 9. FAILURE CLASS

New class 6: sieve-square lattice visibility trades prime support for rough-composite contamination. The exact rough-integer weight identity, explicit Q_R bound, and Mangoldt restricted-divisor identity are proved. This is a no-go for this specific truncated Mobius-log square only; optimized sieve weights and other positive projections remain open.

## Pass 8 — Optimally minimized Selberg upper-bound sieve

### 1. MECHANISM

For squarefree (d≤R), choose real weights λ_d with λ_1=1 and set λ_d=0 for nonsquarefree d or d>R. Define

    w_R(n) = (sum_{d|n, d≤R} lambda_d)² ≥ 0,
    S_R(X) = sum_{n≤X} w_R(n)
           = sum_{d,e≤R} lambda_d lambda_e floor(X/[d,e]).

Unlike Pass 7, the coefficients here minimize the exact quadratic form

    Q_R(lambda) = sum_{d,e≤R} lambda_d lambda_e/[d,e]

subject to lambda_1=1. Every prime p>R has w_R(p)=1, so

    pi(X)-pi(R) ≤ S_R(X).

This is a genuine positive upper-bound sieve, not a proposed lower-bound sieve or a PNT argument.

### 2. DERIVATION

Use [ d,e ]^{-1}=gcd(d,e)/(de) and (gcd(d,e)=\sum_{r|d,e}\phi(r)). With (a_d=lambda_d/d) and (y_r=\sum_{r|d≤R}a_d),

    Q_R(lambda) = sum_{r≤R} phi(r)y_r².

Möbius inversion gives the constraint (lambda_1=a_1=\sum_{r≤R}\mu(r)y_r=1). By Cauchy–Schwarz,

    1 ≤ (sum_{r≤R} mu²(r)/phi(r)) (sum_{r≤R}phi(r)y_r²)
      = G(R) Q_R(lambda),
    G(R)=sum_{r≤R}mu²(r)/phi(r).

Equality holds for (y_r=\mu(r)/(\phi(r)G(R))). Inverting yields the exact rational minimizer

    lambda_d = mu(d) d/(phi(d)G(R))
               * sum_{k≤R/d, gcd(k,d)=1} mu²(k)/phi(k),

and then (Q_R(lambda)=1/G(R)). In the computation all these weights and quadratic identities are exact fractions.

Since (|\lfloor X/q\rfloor-X/q|<1),

    |S_R(X)-X/G(R)| ≤ (sum_{d≤R}|lambda_d|)² = L_1(R)²,
    pi(X) ≤ R + X/G(R) + L_1(R)².

Here π(R)≤R was used. This is the exact finite sieve bound, with no asymptotic approximation.

There is also an elementary explicit asymptotic bound. Since φ(d)≤d,

    G(R) ≥ sum_{d≤R}mu²(d)/d
         ≥ (2-zeta(2)) H_R
         ≥ (2-zeta(2)) log(R+1).

For the middle inequality, subtract by a union bound the reciprocal mass of integers divisible by some prime square. It is at most (H_R\sum_p p^{-2}≤H_R(\zeta(2)-1)).

To bound the coefficient norm, write (G_d(R/d)=\sum_{k≤R/d,(k,d)=1}\mu^2(k)/\phi(k)). Then

    G_d(R/d)/phi(d) = sum_{m≤R, d|m, mu²(m)=1} 1/phi(m) ≤ G(R),

so the explicit minimizer gives (|lambda_d|≤d/\phi(d)). The elementary totient bound φ(d)≥sqrt(d/2) follows from

    phi(n)²/n = product_{p^a||n} p^(a-2)(p-1)² ≥ 1/2,

since the only local factor below 1 can be p=2,a=1, where it equals 1/2; all odd-prime factors at a=1 are at least 4/3 and all factors for a≥2 are at least 1.

    L_1(R)² ≤ (8/9)(R+1)³,
    pi(X) ≤ R + X/((2-zeta(2))log(R+1)) + (8/9)(R+1)³.

Taking (R=\lfloor X^{1/4}\rfloor) proves π(X)=O(X/log X). Consequently ψ(X)=O(X) from θ(X)≤π(X)logX and the elementary bound (\sum_{p^k≤X,k≥2}\log p≤sqrt(X)logX). It does not supply a matching lower bound or the asymptotic ψ(X)~X.

### 3. NUMERIC CHECK

An exact linear sieve computed SPF, μ, and φ values; all λ_d, (G(R)), (Q_R=1/G(R)), (L_1^2), and the bilinear floor sum were accumulated as exact rational numbers, and primes/composites were classified exactly through (10^6). A separate pointwise square accumulation was compared in double precision. The exact floor discrepancy obeyed (|S_R-X/G|≤L_1²) at every cutoff.

|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---|
| 100 | 3 | 2.5000000000 | 39.6400000000 | 48.7600000000 | 23 | 9 | 0.5802220 / 0.2270434 / 0.1675076 |
| 1,000 | 5 | 2.7500000000 | 363.0413223140 | 376.0743801653 | 165 | 100 | 0.4544937 / 0.2754507 / 0.2673010 |
| 10,000 | 10 | 3.6666666667 | 2726.7231404959 | 2765.0743801653 | 1,225 | 1,059 | 0.4492572 / 0.3883783 / 0.1619978 |
