The exact-minimum identity (Q_R=1/G(R)), rational floor expansion, and finite error inequality were asserted in the computation at each cutoff. The direct floating point sum agrees with the exact rational bilinear sum after rounding to double precision. At (X=10^6), rough composites contribute 35.44% of the sieve mass versus 37.41% from primes above (R); these survivors are not separated by the weight.

### 4. ERROR ANALYSIS

The construction proves the classical-order upper bound π(X)=O(X/logX), and thus ψ(X)=O(X), but it gives no lower bound for π(X) or ψ(X). The loss is exact: every integer with least prime factor greater than (R), whether prime or composite, has only divisor 1 in the weight and therefore (w_R(n)=1). Numerically the rough-composite contribution remains comparable to the prime contribution. The minimization reduces the sieve's total quadratic mass but cannot distinguish the parity classes of its rough survivors. No error term for ψ(X)-X or PNT follows, so this is below M0; M0 requires the asymptotic, not an upper bound of the correct order.

### 5. FILTERS

- F-LATTICE — PASS: the exact divisor-incidence count is (\lfloor X/[d,e]\rfloor) for the ordinary integer lattice; the floor discrepancy is bounded explicitly by (L_1^2).
- F-MULT — PASS: squarefree support, μ, φ, and the prime target all use unique factorization essentially.
- F-GLOBAL — PASS for the moving cutoff (R=\lfloor X^{1/4}\rfloor): the full integer lattice is counted at each X, and the cutoff and its error are explicit. A fixed finite set is not claimed to suffice; with fixed R, rough composites remain forever.

### 6. MILESTONE REACHED

Below M0 for the stated prime-error ladder. Additional proved consequence: the Selberg sieve gives π(X)=O(X/logX) and ψ(X)=O(X). It does not give ψ(X)~X, a nontrivial error for ψ(X)-X, or any M0–M6 milestone.

### 7. WHY

The exact quadratic minimization removes avoidable inefficiency in the choice of sieve coefficients and proves the correct upper-order scale. It does not repair the sieve's structural blindness: all R-rough numbers receive the same weight. This is the parity barrier in this concrete construction, not a proof that every positive or arithmetic method has such a barrier. The result strengthens Pass 7 by deriving a genuine global upper estimate, while confirming its rough-composite obstruction.

### 8. LESSON

When an optimized positive sieve yields a sharp upper bound, distinguish optimization of the quadratic energy from identification of prime survivors. For a next candidate, require a new positive observable or external structure that separates primes from rough composites and supplies a lower bound; merely replacing ad hoc coefficients with the minimizer will not do it.

### 9. FAILURE CLASS

Existing class 6, second instance: truncated divisor-square sieves leave rough composites indistinguishable from primes. Pass 8 adds the exact minimizer and a rigorous π(X)=O(X/logX) upper bound, but no new no-go theorem or PNT milestone.

## Pass 9 — Additive Fourier Parseval energy of the Mangoldt polynomial

### 1. MECHANISM

For integer \(X\ge2\), define the finite additive Dirichlet polynomial on the unit circle
\[
D_X(\theta)=\sum_{n\le X}\Lambda(n)e^{2\pi i n\theta},
\qquad E_2(X)=\int_0^1|D_X(\theta)|^2\,d\theta.
\]
This differs from the earlier divisor-incidence Gram statistics: orthogonality is in the additive frequency variable, not the lcm/divisor coordinates. Expanding the square and using \(\int_0^1e^{2\pi i(m-n)\theta}d\theta=\mathbf1_{m=n}\) gives the exact positive diagonal
\[
\boxed{E_2(X)=\sum_{n\le X}\Lambda(n)^2
      =\sum_{p^k\le X}(\log p)^2.}
\]
It is a cumulative prime-power energy. Its prime-base portion \(P_2(X)=\sum_{p\le X}(\log p)^2\) is visible, while higher powers contribute the explicitly bounded remainder \(H_2(X)=E_2(X)-P_2(X)\). This is not a short multiplicative-scale window, and Parseval does not give an independent evaluation of either diagonal sum.

### 2. DERIVATION

The sum defining \(D_X\) is finite, so expanding and integrating termwise is exact:
\[
E_2(X)=\sum_{m,n\le X}\Lambda(m)\Lambda(n)
 \int_0^1 e^{2\pi i(m-n)\theta}\,d\theta
 =\sum_{n\le X}\Lambda(n)^2.
\]
Unique factorization gives \(\Lambda(n)=\log p\) if \(n=p^k\) and zero otherwise, proving the prime-power formula. For every integer \(M>X\), the same orthogonality is exact on roots of unity:
\[
\boxed{E_2(X)=\frac1M\sum_{j=0}^{M-1}
 \left|\sum_{n\le X}\Lambda(n)e^{2\pi i jn/M}\right|^2.}
\]
Indeed \(|m-n|<M\), so \(M\mid(m-n)\) holds only when \(m=n\).

Separating exponent one from higher exponents gives
\[
E_2(X)=P_2(X)+H_2(X),\qquad
H_2(X)=\sum_{k=2}^{\lfloor\log_2X\rfloor}
\sum_{p\le X^{1/k}}(\log p)^2.
\]
For each \(k\ge2\), there are at most \(X^{1/k}\le\sqrt X\) eligible primes and each summand is at most \((\log X)^2\). Since the number of exponents is at most \(\log X/\log2\),
\[
\boxed{0\le H_2(X)\le\frac{\sqrt X(\log X)^3}{\log2}.}
\]
This is an explicit but coarse higher-power bound.

The exact identity does not evaluate \(P_2(X)\) from a known integer-lattice statistic: its right side is exactly the arithmetic coefficient energy put into \(D_X\). The immediate coefficientwise estimate \(\Lambda(n)^2\le(\log n)^2\) only yields
\[
E_2(X)\le\sum_{n\le X}(\log n)^2=O(X(\log X)^2),
\]
which is not a prime-counting estimate. Partial summation writes \(P_2(X)=\theta(X)\log X-\int_2^X\theta(t)t^{-1}dt\) (with the usual endpoint convention); without an independent estimate for this quantity it does not recover \(\theta\) or \(\psi\).

### 3. NUMERIC CHECK

An exact sieve identified primes and prime-power support through \(10^6\); 60-digit Decimal logarithms accumulated \(E_2,P_2,H_2\), and an independent double-precision FFT tested the roots-of-unity formula with \(M=2^{20}>10^6\). The FFT cross-check is numerical evidence only; the root-of-unity identity above is exact.

| X | prime-power terms | prime count | E₂(X) | P₂(X) | H₂(X) | explicit H₂ upper bound |
|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 10 | 7 | 4 | 10.232113665556 | 8.064258676907 | 2.167854988649 | 55.695692 |
| 100 | 35 | 25 | 321.492594104092 | 309.092625449886 | 12.399968654205 | 1409.001943 |
| 1,000 | 193 | 168 | 5,774.176090263013 | 5,686.965758543310 | 87.210331719703 | 15,037.836867 |
| 10,000 | 1,280 | 1,229 | 81,765.373876326640 | 81,399.384884459820 | 365.988991866820 | 112,720.155452 |
| 100,000 | 9,700 | 9,592 | 1,050,021.351743417503 | 1,048,435.866172866787 | 1,585.485570550715 | 696,196.151245 |
| 1,000,000 | 78,734 | 78,498 | 12,802,986.484128914603 | 12,796,869.461412897102 | 6,117.022716017500 | 3,804,305.246517 |

The FFT relative residuals for \(X=10,100,10^3,10^4,10^5,10^6\) were respectively \(0,0,3.150\times10^{-16},1.780\times10^{-16},4.435\times10^{-16},1.455\times10^{-16}\). The finite ratios \(E_2(X)/(X\log X)\) were \(0.444375050,0.698112298,0.835897605,0.887756267,0.912036958,0.926711064\). These are diagnostics, not an asymptotic claim. At \(X=10^6\), \(\psi(X)-X=-413.402504367078\); this value is reported only as a numerical comparison.

### 4. ERROR ANALYSIS

The exact output is a nonnegative prime-power second moment, not a bound for \(\psi(X)-X\). The power terms can be separated with the explicit \(O(\sqrt X\log^3X)\) bound above, leaving \(P_2(X)\), but there is no independent evaluation of \(P_2\) in this mechanism. Estimating it at its expected scale already requires information about the prime distribution; Parseval simply changes representation. The only bound supplied here for \(\psi\) remains the elementary \(O(X\log X)\) bound, below M0. The precise loss is at the attempted evaluation of the Fourier diagonal after orthogonality: all off-diagonal terms vanish, but the surviving diagonal is the unknown prime-power energy itself.

### 5. FILTERS

- **F-LATTICE — PASS, with scope stated.** The exact roots-of-unity orthogonality uses the ordinary integer frequency lattice and the condition \(M>X\). A general Beurling integer system has no such evenly spaced additive frequencies, so this identity does not transfer unchanged.
- **F-MULT — PASS.** The coefficient \(\Lambda(n)\) and its prime-power support use unique factorization and the Euler-product von Mangoldt function essentially; an arbitrary functional equation without multiplicativity does not provide these coefficients.
- **F-GLOBAL — PASS only for the full prime-power system at each cutoff.** The finite polynomial includes every prime power through \(X\), and the sequence of cutoffs tends through the full system. No fixed finite set of primes is claimed to recover the energy or its limit.

### 6. MILESTONE REACHED

**Below M0.** The mechanism gives an exact positive prime-power energy and an explicit higher-power remainder bound, but it does not prove \(\psi(X)\sim X\) or any nontrivial error for \(\psi(X)-X\).

### 7. WHY

Additive Fourier orthogonality is maximally effective at removing cross terms, but the diagonal it isolates is the Mangoldt energy itself. It has no separate integer-only formula here, so evaluating it is not easier than estimating prime data. The failure is intrinsic to this use of Parseval, not a no-go for additive Fourier correlations that incorporate an independently evaluable lattice statistic.

### 8. LESSON

A positive Fourier norm is useful only if its diagonal can be evaluated independently of the prime coefficients. A future candidate must couple additive orthogonality to an exact ordinary-lattice identity whose value is known without reintroducing the same prime-power sum; numerical agreement or positivity alone does not provide that bridge.

### 9. FAILURE CLASS

**New class 7: positive additive Fourier energy isolates but does not evaluate the prime-power diagonal.** The Parseval identities and the higher-power bound are proved; the finite FFT checks corroborate them through \(10^6\). This is a limitation of this observable, not a universal obstruction to additive harmonic analysis.

## Pass 10 — Consolidation: divisor-feature blindness and the Beurling stress test

This is the required tenth-pass consolidation, not a new candidate mechanism. The two most frequent failure classes are tied at two instances each: class 4 (positive norm-lattice transforms leak across scales; Passes 4 and 6) and class 6 (truncated divisor-square sieves leave rough composites indistinguishable; Passes 7 and 8). I consolidate class 6 because its exact finite-feature map admits a general algebraic statement. The class-4 evidence remains only two tested scalar norm-lattice totals; no uniform theorem for other norm forms or projections has been established.

### Restricted no-go statement

Fix an integer cutoff \(R\ge1\). Let \(\mathcal D_R(n)=(\mathbf 1_{d\mid n})_{1\le d\le R}\) be the truncated divisor-feature vector. For any map \(F_R\) on these vectors, define an observable \(W_R(n)=F_R(\mathcal D_R(n))\); divisor-sum weights \(w_R(n)=(\sum_{d\mid n,d\le R}\lambda_d)^2\), with \(\lambda_1=1\), are a special case.

If every prime factor of \(n\) exceeds \(R\), then no divisor \(d\) with \(2\le d\le R\) divides \(n\). Hence
\[
\mathcal D_R(n)=(1,0,\ldots,0)=\mathcal D_R(1),
\qquad W_R(n)=F_R(1,0,\ldots,0).
\]
Thus **every pointwise statistic depending only on divisibility by integers \(d\le R\) is constant on the \(R\)-rough integers**, regardless of positivity or the choice of coefficients. In particular it cannot itself mark an \(R\)-rough prime differently from an \(R\)-rough composite. In the Pass 8 example \(R=31\), the prime 37 and the composite \(37^2=1369\) both have the same truncated divisor vector and sieve weight 1.

The algebraic pointwise-blindness claim is **PROVED**. The stronger assertion “every such sieve has prime-counting error no better than M0” is **not proved**. A scalar aggregate over all \(n\le X\), combined with other lattice identities or an independently controlled rough-composite count, could carry information not represented by pointwise classification alone. The named remaining gap is **aggregate elimination of rough composites**. Consequently the no-go is **PARTIAL** for the milestone ladder: it rules out exact pointwise prime detection from truncated divisor features alone, but establishes no universal ceiling at M0 and no lower bound for \(|\psi(X)-X|\).

### Beurling filter audit

The algebraic lemma itself does not use the ordinary integer floor function. Its extension holds for free or uniquely factorizable generalized-integer systems when divisors are induced by the canonical factorization and every nonunit factor of an \(R\)-rough object has size greater than \(R\). No claim is made for divisor relations with nonunique numerical factorizations that fail this compatibility condition.

The separate Beurling stress test limits broader claims. Diamond–Montgomery–Vorhauer construct generalized prime systems with \(N_B(x)=\kappa x+O(x^\vartheta)\), for any chosen fixed \(1/2<\vartheta<1\), while the prime-counting error has oscillation of de la Vallée Poussin scale \(\Omega(x e^{-c\sqrt{\log x}})\). This is larger than \(x^{1-\delta}\) eventually for every fixed \(\delta>0\). Thus multiplicativity plus such a regular generalized-integer estimate alone cannot justify M5. Their construction does **not** refute a method that essentially uses the ordinary integer floor counts; the floor/lattice dependence must be identified explicitly. See the original paper, [Diamond–Montgomery–Vorhauer, *Beurling primes with large oscillation*](https://deepblue.lib.umich.edu/bitstream/handle/2027.42/46253/208_2005_Article_638.pdf), and the later account/construction in [Broucke–Debruyne–Vindas, *Beurling integers with RH and large oscillation*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2004.11501).

This audit validates only the filter logic: a proof route that transfers unchanged using multiplicativity and a regular generalized-integer count is not enough for M5. It does not show that the exact floor/lcm estimates in Pass 8 transfer to Beurling systems; they do not. Nor does this Beurling example prove the truncated-divisor lemma's aggregate limitation.

### Consolidated root-cause synthesis

- Positive total identities have repeatedly made all terms nonnegative or produced a positive semidefinite Gram form, but this controls a total or an upper bound and has not supplied a lower estimate for the selected prime channel (Passes 1, 3–8).
- Scale leakage is distinct from positivity: the tested smoothed and norm-lattice kernels retain large small-divisor mass, even when the endpoint prime factor is explicit (Passes 2, 4–6).
- Prime isolation often reintroduces the lost information as a signed inverse, a composite-channel subtraction, or the unknown target \(\psi(X)\) itself (Passes 1, 5, 7–8).
- Optimizing a positive quadratic energy improves its total bound without resolving which survivors are primes (Pass 8); Pass 10 proves the pointwise version for the entire truncated-divisor-feature class.
- Additive orthogonality removes cross terms exactly but leaves the prime-power diagonal unevaluated (Pass 9). Positivity and exactness are therefore separate from arithmetic evaluability.
- All finite computations stop at \(10^6\). They verify finite identities and illustrate scale profiles; they provide no asymptotic error term and are not evidence for or against an M5/M6 theorem by themselves.

### Selection-bias audit

The nine candidates are not an exhaustive enumeration of positive identities. They were generated sequentially from earlier failures and over-sample divisor transforms, scalar norm-lattice totals, divisor-square sieves, and second-moment/Parseval energies. Passes 4 and 6, and separately 7 and 8, are related candidates, so the seven recorded classes are not seven statistically independent obstructions. The cutoffs and scale bands were chosen for diagnostic clarity, not from a pre-registered search. Numerical trends across six cutoffs cannot establish limiting behavior. The task-provided Selberg symmetry formula is a known M0 baseline, but this ledger has not independently reproduced its derivation. The term “positive mechanism” also includes distinct logical classes—pointwise nonnegative weights, PSD quadratic forms, and positive linear combinations—which must not be conflated.

### Positive scale-separation mechanisms still unexcluded

1. **Established benchmark, not an open result:** the task specification supplies Selberg's symmetry formula and its Tauberian implication as a proof of PNT (M0). Passes 1–9 did not rederive that classical argument and did not improve its error. Open work here means quantitative refinements or genuinely different positive variants that improve the stated error ladder.
2. Positive weights whose exact divisor transform is genuinely endpoint-localized and whose complementary channels admit an independently proved bound.
3. Lattice projections, differences, or several coupled lattice identities that retain exact floor/Poisson information while isolating prime channels without signed inversion.
4. Sieve observables using information beyond the truncated divisor-feature vector—especially a proved estimate for rough composites or a nonlocal/global arithmetic statistic. The Pass 10 lemma does not cover these.
5. Additive Fourier correlations beyond zero-lag Parseval, when their off-diagonal terms can be evaluated from an independent exact lattice identity and the Mangoldt dependence remains essential.
6. Primitive or higher-dimensional norm-lattice observables other than the two scalar totals tested; no general obstruction for their projected or locally weighted forms has been proved.
7. Methods that use the ordinary integer lattice in an essential way and do not transfer to Beurling systems. The Beurling counterexamples do not constrain such methods unless their key estimates are shown to transfer.

### Consolidation verdict

**PARTIAL.** The pointwise truncated-divisor blindness theorem is complete and applies to arbitrary functions of the finite divisor-feature vector. The aggregate prime-error no-go remains open. No new universal no-go theorem, prime-counting milestone, or search-saturation conclusion is claimed. The next actual candidate must escape the truncated divisor-feature class and must make the prime-channel evaluation independent of the unknown prime sum; it must still use ordinary-lattice exactness and Euler-product multiplicativity essentially, and quantify every complementary channel.

## Pass 11 — Positive determinant of the gcd-divisibility Gram matrix

### 1. MECHANISM

For an integer cutoff (Xge2), form the (X	imes X) matrix
\[
G_X=(\gcd(i,j))_{1\le i,j\le X}.
\]
The identity \(\gcd(i,j)=\sum_{d\mid i,\ d\mid j}\varphi(d)\) factors it as
\[
G_X=E_X\,\operatorname{diag}(\varphi(1),\ldots,\varphi(X))\,E_X^{T},
\qquad (E_X)_{i,d}=\mathbf1_{d\mid i}.
\]
The divisibility matrix (E_X) is lower triangular with diagonal 1. Since every \(\varphi(d)>0\), (G_X) is positive definite and its spectral-volume invariant is
\[
\det G_X=\prod_{n\le X}\varphi(n)>0.
\]
This uses the product of the whole spectrum rather than the all-ones quadratic energy of Pass 3. Factoring \(\varphi(n)\) gives an exact positive prime-power-channel expansion:
\[
\log\det G_X=
\sum_{p\le X}\Big\lfloor\frac Xp\Big\rfloor\log(p-1)
+\sum_{a\ge2,\ p^a\le X}\Big\lfloor\frac X{p^a}\Big\rfloor\log p.
\]
The first sum is the exponent-one prime-base channel (the (p=2) term is zero); the second is nonnegative higher-power contamination. These divisor-multiple weights still extend from small prime scales to (X), rather than localizing at the endpoint.

### 2. DERIVATION

For each (n\), \(\varphi(n)=\prod_{p^a\parallel n}p^{a-1}(p-1)\), so
\[
\log\varphi(n)=\sum_{p\mid n}\log(p-1)+\sum_{a\ge2,\ p^a\mid n}\log p.
\]
Summing over (n\le X\) counts \(\lfloor X/p\rfloor\) multiples in the first channel and \(\lfloor X/p^a\rfloor\) in each higher-power channel, proving the expansion. A second exact form follows from \(\log\varphi(n)=\log n+\sum_{p\mid n}\log(1-1/p)\):
\[
L(X):=\log\det G_X=\log(X!)+C(X),\qquad
C(X)=\sum_{p\le X}\Big\lfloor\frac Xp\Big\rfloor\log(1-1/p).
\]
Define the absolutely convergent constant
\[
c=\sum_p\frac{\log(1-1/p)}p.
\]
Writing (c_X) for the same sum over (p\le X), the remainder in the finite-(c_X) model is
\[
R_X=L(X)-\{X(\log X-1)+Xc_X\}
=\log(X!)-X(\log X-1)
+\sum_{p\le X}\left(\Big\lfloor\frac Xp\Big\rfloor-\frac Xp\right)\log(1-1/p).
\]
Both terms on the right are nonnegative. The integral comparison for the increasing function \(\log t\) gives
\[
1\le\log(X!)-X(\log X-1)\le1+\log X.
\]
Also, since \(-\log(1-1/p)\le1/(p-1)\),
\[
0\le\sum_{p\le X}\left(\Big\lfloor\frac Xp\Big\rfloor-\frac Xp\right)\log(1-1/p)
\le\sum_{p\le X}\frac1{p-1}\le H_{X-1}.
\]
Therefore the explicit finite-cutoff estimate is
\[
1\le R_X\le1+\log X+H_{X-1}.
\]
For the infinite constant, the omitted tail is negative and satisfies
\[
0\le X(c_X-c)\le X\sum_{n>X}\frac1{n(n-1)}=1.
\]
Consequently
\[
X(\log X-1+c)+1\le L(X)
\le X(\log X-1+c)+2+\log X+H_{X-1}.
\]
This is a proved (O(\log X)) asymptotic for the log determinant, not for a prime-counting function.

The relation to the Chebyshev divisor average is exact:
\[
T(X):=\sum_{n\le X}\log n=\sum_{d\le X}\Lambda(d)\Big\lfloor\frac Xd\Big\rfloor
=\sum_{m\le X}\psi(\lfloor X/m\rfloor).
\]
The determinant supplies \(T(X)+C(X)\), with (C(X)=cX+O(\log X)\); it does not separate the terminal \(\psi(X)\) term from the other scales.

### 3. NUMERIC CHECK

An exact sieve classified every prime through (10^6\); 45-digit mpmath arithmetic accumulated the prime-power channels, Euler-factor correction, factorial model, and constants. The factor-channel and lattice forms of \(\log\det G_X\) agreed to less than \(2\times10^{-39}\) in absolute residual at all displayed cutoffs. The finite remainder obeyed the proved bound at each cutoff.

| (X) | \(\log\det G_X\) | (c_X) | finite-model remainder (R_X) | bound (1+\log X+H_{X-1}\) | \(\psi(X)-X\), diagnostic |
|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 10 | 9.821843563496 | −0.548378862268 | 2.2797812562 | 6.131553347 | −2.167985819495 |
| 100 | 306.371220078369 | −0.578234766799 | 3.6776781595 | 10.782547704 | −5.954688770643 |
| 1,000 | 5,333.045537856296 | −0.579931470352 | 5.2217292258 | 15.392226140 | −3.319087752825 |
| 10,000 | 76,309.2854875791 | −0.580048677857 | 6.3685463864 | 19.997846408 | 13.396693263115 |
| 100,000 | 993,294.504751585 | −0.580057691563 | 7.7274108443 | 24.603061595 | 51.564025657954 |
| 1,000,000 | 12,235,460.8661512 | −0.580058426038 | 8.7342251009 | 29.208236281 | −413.40250436708 |

At (X=10^6\), \(c_X=-0.5800584260382028556035\), with \(c_X-10^{-6}\le c\le c_X\) from the explicit tail bound. The total determinant-channel mass at (X=10^6\) consists of 78.09% from (p\le X/10\), 11.66% from (X/10<p\le X/2\), 4.08% from (X/2<p\le X\), and 6.16% from higher powers. Thus the exponent-one channel itself is broadly distributed: its upper-half share is only 4.08%. These finite ratios are diagnostic and imply no limiting prime distribution.

### 4. ERROR ANALYSIS

The determinant asymptotic has a proved (O(\log X)\) remainder with the displayed explicit bound. It gives no nontrivial estimate for \(\psi(X)-X\): the identity for \(T(X)\) is the Chebyshev divisor average, and recovering \(\psi\) by \(\psi(X)=\sum_{m\le X}\mu(m)T(\lfloor X/m\rfloor)\) reintroduces signed Möbius inversion and its cancellation problem. The correction (C(X)) is an absolutely convergent Euler-factor sum with a known (O(\log X)\) approximation; it contributes no independent endpoint estimate. The exact loss is that log-determinant spectral volume collapses to the already-known lattice factorial plus a convergent prime correction, rather than isolating \(\psi(X)\).

### 5. FILTERS

- **F-LATTICE — PASS.** The gcd Gram factorization is over the exact divisibility incidence of \(1,\ldots,X\); the determinant is evaluated using the ordinary integer lattice and exact floor counts.
- **F-MULT — PASS.** The factors \(\varphi(n)\), the prime-power channel expansion, and the Euler factors use unique factorization essentially.
- **F-GLOBAL — PASS only with a moving complete cutoff.** (G_X) includes every integer and every prime factor through (X\). The Euler constant tail beyond a fixed prime cutoff (R) is bounded by (X/R\); a fixed finite set does not supply the stated (O(\log X)\) scale as (X\to\infty\).

### 6. MILESTONE REACHED

**Below M0 for \(\psi\).** A positive definite arithmetic Gram matrix and a sharp log-determinant asymptotic are proved, but no prime-number-theorem asymptotic for \(\psi\) or nontrivial error for \(\psi(X)-X\) follows.

### 7. WHY

Taking the determinant changes the observable from a quadratic total to a spectral product, but its exact factorization is a totient product. Taking logarithms turns that product into \(T(X)+C(X)\), where (T) is precisely the Chebyshev divisor average and (C) is a small, absolutely convergent Euler correction. The candidate therefore inherits the original loss at the inversion step. This is intrinsic to this gcd determinant invariant; other minors, projections, or determinant ratios are not ruled out.

### 8. LESSON

A positive spectral determinant is useful for prime extraction only if its logarithm yields an independently evaluable terminal prime channel. Before pursuing another determinant, derive its exact arithmetic factorization and compare it with the Chebyshev divisor average; here the determinant is a repackaging of that average, and Mobius inversion remains the only displayed route back to \(\psi\).

### 9. FAILURE CLASS

**New class 8: positive gcd-Gram determinant repackages the Chebyshev divisor average.** The determinant factorization and \(O(\log X)\) log-determinant estimate are proved; no general no-go for other positive spectral invariants is claimed.

## Pass 12 — Exponentially sharpened gcd-determinant ratio filter

### 1. MECHANISM

Let (G_n=(\gcd(i,j))_{1\le i,j\le n}). Pass 11 proved \(\det G_n=\prod_{k\le n}\varphi(k)\), so the consecutive determinant ratio is exactly
\[
\Delta_n=\frac{\det G_n}{\det G_{n-1}}=\varphi(n),\qquad n\ge2.
\]
Normalize it by (n-1) and raise to the scale (n):
\[
r_n=\frac{\Delta_n}{n-1}=\frac{\varphi(n)}{n-1},\qquad w_n=r_n^n\in(0,1].
\]
For primes (p), (w_p=1). For composites, the weight decays fast enough that their total contamination is bounded independently of (X). Thus the two positive sums
\[
A(X)=\sum_{2\le n\le X}w_n,\qquad
B(X)=\sum_{2\le n\le X}w_n\log n
\]
separate prime count and prime logarithmic mass:
\[
0\le A(X)-\pi(X)\le 7/e,\qquad
0\le B(X)-\theta(X)\le22/e.
\]
This uses a local determinant ratio plus nonlinear damping, not the global log determinant of Pass 11. No sign-changing divisor coefficients occur in either observable.

### 2. DERIVATION

The determinant ratio follows directly by dividing \(\det G_n=\prod_{k\le n}\varphi(k)\) by its predecessor. For a prime (p), \(\varphi(p)=p-1\), hence (r_p=w_p=1).

If (n) is composite, it has a prime factor (p\le\sqrt n), and
\[
\varphi(n)=n\prod_{q\mid n}(1-1/q)\le n(1-1/p)\le n-\sqrt n.
\]
Therefore
\[
1-r_n=\frac{n-1-\varphi(n)}{n-1}\ge\frac{\sqrt n-1}{n-1}=\frac1{\sqrt n+1},
\]
and
\[
0<w_n\le\left(1-\frac1{\sqrt n+1}\right)^n
\le e^{-n/(\sqrt n+1)}\le e^{1-\sqrt n}.
\]
The last majorant is summable. For (x\ge4), it is decreasing, so
\[
\sum_{n\ge4}e^{1-\sqrt n}
\le e^{-1}+\int_4^\infty e^{1-\sqrt x}\,dx=7/e.
\]
Also \(\log n\le\sqrt n\), and \(\sqrt x e^{1-\sqrt x}\) decreases on (x\ge4), giving
\[
\sum_{n\ge4}\log(n)e^{1-\sqrt n}
\le 2/e+\int_4^\infty\sqrt x e^{1-\sqrt x}\,dx=22/e.
\]
Since the prime terms in (A) and (B) are exactly (1) and \(\log p\), respectively, the two bounded-contamination inequalities follow.

For the tail beyond a cutoff (X\ge4), integral comparison gives
\[
0\le\sum_{\substack{n>X\\ n\text{ composite}}} w_n
\le 2e(\sqrt X+1)e^{-\sqrt X},
\]
and
\[
0\le\sum_{\substack{n>X\\ n\text{ composite}}} w_n\log n
\le 2e(X+2\sqrt X+2)e^{-\sqrt X}.
\]
Thus both contaminations converge rapidly to finite constants; these are error bounds for prime extraction, not estimates of the sums (A(X)) or (B(X)) from known lattice volumes.

Writing \(\psi(X)=\theta(X)+H(X)\) for the higher-prime-power contribution, the elementary bound
\[
0\le H(X)=\sum_{p^k\le X,\,k\ge2}\log p\le\sqrt X\log X
\]
follows by bounding the contribution of each prime (p\le\sqrt X) by \(\log X\), and counting at most \(\sqrt X\) such primes. Hence
\[
|\psi(X)-B(X)|\le\sqrt X\log X+22/e.
\]
This is a square-root-scale representation error relative to (B(X)), not an estimate of \(\psi(X)-X\). Indeed, (B(X)=\theta(X)+O(1)), so proving (B(X)\sim X) is equivalent to proving the prime number theorem; no independent evaluation of (B) has been obtained.

### 3. NUMERIC CHECK

An exact sieve computed primes and \(\varphi(n)\) through (10^6), and verified for every (2\le n\le10^6) that \(\varphi(n)=n-1\) exactly on the primes. The composite weights through (10^4) were summed at 55-digit precision. For larger (n), the omitted tails were not set to zero: the proved integral bounds at (10^4) are (2.043\times10^{-41}) unweighted and (2.063\times10^{-39}) with the \(\log n\) weight.

| (X) | exact \(\pi(X)\) | composite mass (A-\pi) | exact \(\theta(X)\) | composite mass (B-\theta) | diagnostic \(\psi(X)-B(X)\) |
|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 10 | 4 | 0.288380581355581212 | 5.34710753071746868 | 0.470485061266144057 | 2.014421588521856 |
| 100 | 25 | 0.300673742956950634 | 83.7283903990639229 | 0.510875965850493135 | 9.806044864442976 |
| 1,000 | 168 | 0.300704222674760602 | 956.245265120058868 | 0.511023423972925380 | 39.92462370314345 |
| 10,000 | 1,229 | 0.300704222674760744 | 9,895.99137915698731 | 0.511023423972926409 | 116.8942906821545 |
| 100,000 | 9,592 | 0.300704222674760744 to (+2.043\times10^{-41}) | 99,685.3892686125508 | 0.511023423972926409 to (+2.063\times10^{-39}) | 365.6637336214305 |
| 1,000,000 | 78,498 | 0.300704222674760744 to (+2.043\times10^{-41}) | 998,484.175025634292 | 0.511023423972926409 to (+2.063\times10^{-39}) | 1,101.911446574657 |

The prime counts and prime-power classifications are exact; displayed logarithmic sums use 55-digit arithmetic. The observed composite masses stabilize quickly, consistent with the proven summable majorants. Numerical stabilization is not used to infer PNT or any asymptotic for \(\theta\).

### 4. ERROR ANALYSIS

The mechanism proves exact extraction up to bounded positive contamination for \(\pi\) and \(\theta\), and gives \(\psi(X)=B(X)+O(\sqrt X\log X)\) as a representation by a new explicit statistic. It does **not** prove (B(X)=X+o(X)), nor any bound for \(\psi(X)-X\); those require estimating (B(X)), which is equivalent at leading order to the PNT because (B(X)=\theta(X)+O(1)). The exact missing step is an independent ordinary-lattice evaluation or asymptotic for the nonlinear totient-ratio sum (B(X)). If evaluated only by expanding \(\varphi(n)\), the prime distribution reappears in the same summands. Therefore this pass reaches below M0, despite the \(O(\sqrt X\log X)\) transfer error to (B).

### 5. FILTERS

- **F-LATTICE — PASS, with precise dependence.** The local determinant ratio is defined on the ordinary divisibility lattice (1,\ldots,n), using the exact gcd identity and its totient factorization. It does not transfer unchanged to a Beurling system lacking the ordinary integer gcd/divisibility matrix and the bound “a composite (n) has a prime factor at most \(\sqrt n\).”
- **F-MULT — PASS.** The formula for \(\varphi(n)\), the prime/composite separation, and the determinant factorization use unique factorization essentially.
- **F-GLOBAL — PASS only for the full factorization at every (n\le X).** No fixed finite set of primes determines all \(\varphi(n)\); the construction uses every prime divisor of each index as (X\) grows. A fixed-prime truncation has no proved uniform bound.

### 6. MILESTONE REACHED

**Below M0 for \(\psi(X)-X\).** The new positive statistic approximates \(\theta(X)\) within a constant and \(\psi(X)\) within \(O(\sqrt X\log X)\), but there is no independent estimate of the statistic relative to (X). This is not M6: the M6 target is an error bound for \(\psi(X)-X\), not a representation error relative to (B(X)).

### 7. WHY

The exponential power (n) turns the totient deficit of any composite into a summable weight, escaping the Pass 10 truncated-divisor blindness: the filter uses the full determinant ratio, not only small divisors. It successfully isolates primes but transfers the unresolved content into the global sum (B(X)). The failure is now evaluability rather than channel contamination: a statistic within (O(1)) of prime log mass still requires a PNT-strength estimate to determine its leading term. This is intrinsic to the current statistic’s available evaluation, not a no-go for all positive exact lattice transforms.

### 8. LESSON

Positive determinant ratios can give a sharply prime-selective nonlinear weight without Möbius inversion. The next candidate must preserve this full-factorization selectivity while deriving an independent exact lattice evaluation of the aggregate weight; merely proving another tail bound cannot determine its leading mass.

### 9. FAILURE CLASS

**New class 9: positive nonlinear totient filter isolates primes but leaves its aggregate unevaluated.** The prime weights, composite decay, convergent contamination, and numerical checks are established; no PNT-scale asymptotic or universal obstruction is claimed.

## Pass 13 — Centered residue-class energy of the log lattice

### 1. MECHANISM

For (Xge2), set (q=lfloorsqrt X
floor), and for residues (amod q) define
\[
A_a(X)=\sum_{\substack{2\le n\le X\\n\equiv a\pmod q}}\log n,
\quad T(X)=\sum_{2\le n\le X}\log n,
\quad V_q(X)=\sum_{a\bmod q}\left(A_a(X)-\frac{T(X)}q\right)^2.
\]
This is a positive, nonzero-frequency additive Fourier energy of the **known log-integer lattice**, rather than the Pass 9 Parseval energy whose coefficients were already \(\Lambda(n)\). The finite value is independently evaluable from products of integers in arithmetic progressions. Factoring \(\log n=\sum_{d\mid n}\Lambda(d)\) gives vectors of prime-power residue counts. The norm square is positive, but its prime-power channel Gram has signed cross terms; the prime-only diagonal is not a lower bound for the total.

### 2. DERIVATION

Let (L_d=\lfloor X/d\rfloor) and let (v_d\in\mathbb R^q) have entries
\[
(v_d)_a=\#\{1\le k\le L_d:dk\equiv a\pmod q\}.
\]
The exact identity \(\log n=\sum_{d\mid n}\Lambda(d)\) implies
\[
A(X)=\sum_{d\le X}\Lambda(d)v_d,
\qquad V_q(X)=\left\|P_q\sum_{d\le X}\Lambda(d)v_d\right\|_2^2,
\quad P_q=I-\frac1q\mathbf1\mathbf1^T.
\]
Expanding the square gives the exact prime-power diagonal plus cross terms:
\[
V_q(X)=D_{\rm all}(X)+2\sum_{d<e\le X}\Lambda(d)\Lambda(e)\langle P_qv_d,P_qv_e\rangle,
\quad D_{\rm all}=\sum_{d\le X}\Lambda(d)^2\|P_qv_d\|^2.
\]
The selected prime diagonal is \(D_{\rm prime}=\sum_{p\le X}(\log p)^2\|P_qv_p\|^2\), a subset of the diagonal terms only. It is not itself the total energy.

There is a closed diagonal formula. Put \(g=(d,q)\), \(m=q/g\), and write \(L_d=mh+r\), (0\le r<m). Exactly (m) residues are attainable; (r) receive count (h+1), and the other (m-r) receive count (h). Thus
\[
\|P_qv_d\|^2=mh^2+2rh+r-\frac{L_d^2}{q}.
\]
For the lattice evaluation, (A_a(X)) is the logarithm of a finite product of terms (a+kq\); hence (T(X)) and (V_q(X)) require no prime data. Stirling gives (T(X)=X\log X-X+O(\log X)). A coarse explicit bound follows from (A_a\le(X/q+1)\log X):
\[
0\le V_q(X)\le\sum_aA_a(X)^2\le (X/q+1)X(\log X)^2,
\]
so with (q=\lfloor\sqrt X\rfloor), (V_q(X)=O(X^{3/2}\log^2X)). This controls the new energy, not \(\psi(X)-X\).

### 3. NUMERIC CHECK

Prime and prime-power support and the Möbius values were generated by exact integer sieves through (10^6). Residue energies and all reported logarithmic channels were independently accumulated at 50-digit Decimal precision; the channel-vector identity was checked at (X=1000,q=31), with maximum residue error (4\times10^{-47}). The table reports the exact-sieve value of \(\psi(X)-X\) only as a finite diagnostic.

| X | q | V_q(X) | prime diagonal | all prime-power diagonal | cross energy | \(\psi(X)-X\) |
|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 100 | 10 | 17.6572471043784613 | 933.2811179570524631 | 1001.7033162088156129 | −984.0460691044371516 | −5.9546887706426078 |
| 500 | 22 | 91.1270038219172600 | 11072.4090105789995528 | 11657.1623278950518902 | −11566.0353240731346301 | 1.6521166497476359 |
| 1,000 | 31 | 107.6991560357973637 | 23192.5105471696535777 | 23431.1359911399849373 | −23323.4368351041875737 | −3.3190877528247597 |
| 5,000 | 70 | 514.0891637059459330 | 452674.6297047086591198 | 477523.3030454955100327 | −477009.2138817895640997 | −2.0403077620923287 |
| 10,000 | 100 | 482.0417254512950778 | 767794.9289245909060056 | 993325.7930652619758931 | −992843.7513398106808153 | 13.3966932631147837 |
| 100,000 | 316 | 4693.8668909627873425 | 15259036.6076924001780688 | 19076225.0933030555938029 | −19071531.2264120928064604 | 51.5640256579542509 |
| 1,000,000 | 1,000 | 8380.5097335542966402 | 593675222.1063067940078436 | 874811922.2887645324742477 | −874803541.7790309781776075 | −413.4025043670779669 |

At (10^6), the prime diagonal is about (1.4116\times10^{-5}) times (V_q), while the cross energy cancels about (99.999042\%) of the full prime-power diagonal. This is finite evidence of severe cancellation, not an asymptotic claim. The computed \(\psi(X)-X\) values do not support any inferred error law.

### 4. ERROR ANALYSIS

The exact statistic has a known lattice evaluation and the bound above, but no inequality here bounds \(\psi(X)-X\) nontrivially. The only immediate bound remains (0\le\psi(X)\le T(X)=X\log X-X+O(\log X)), hence \(|\psi(X)-X|=O(X\log X)\), below M0. The precise failure is that expanding a positive norm into arithmetic channels introduces cross terms of either sign; numerically they nearly cancel the large positive prime-power diagonal. The norm's positivity therefore cannot be assigned to its prime-only diagonal. No Tauberian or inversion step improves the target error.

### 5. FILTERS

- **F-LATTICE — PASS.** The residue classes and products of arithmetic progressions use the ordinary integer lattice and exact congruence counts; this evaluation is not available unchanged in a general Beurling system.
- **F-MULT — FAIL as an extraction mechanism.** Multiplicativity enters at \(\log n=\sum_{d\mid n}\Lambda(d)\) to label prime-power channels, but the observable and its independent lattice evaluation use only \(\log n\); no Euler-product information is needed to calculate the total, and no channel is recovered from it.
- **F-GLOBAL — PASS only for the complete moving lattice.** The identity uses every integer and all prime powers through (X); no fixed finite-prime truncation is asserted to converge.

### 6. MILESTONE REACHED

**Below M0.** The candidate yields an exactly evaluable positive lattice energy and a coarse (O(X^{3/2}\log^2X)) bound for that energy, but no prime-number theorem and no nontrivial bound for \(\psi(X)-X\).

### 7. WHY

Changing from the von Mangoldt Fourier polynomial to the log-lattice residue spectrum makes the total independently computable, but sacrifices prime-specific observability. Re-expansion through \(\Lambda\) restores that observability only as a Gram decomposition with large signed cross interference. This is a new measured instance of the distinction between a positive total and positive arithmetic channels; it is related to Pass 9's additive-energy limitation but is not the same Parseval diagonal. It is not a no-go for other coupled lattice identities.

### 8. LESSON

For a positive lattice norm expanded into prime channels, compute the full centered channel Gram, not only its diagonal. A future candidate must either make the relevant channel vectors orthogonal by an exact arithmetic identity or bound their cross sum independently at the target scale; positivity of the aggregate is insufficient.

### 9. FAILURE CLASS

**New class 10: positive residue-lattice energy has large cross-channel cancellation.** The finite decomposition and its numerical cancellation are established; no universal obstruction or prime-error ceiling is claimed.

## Pass 14 — Selberg's positive two-channel symmetry formula (benchmark replay)

### 1. MECHANISM

This pass reconstructs the classical positive identity behind Selberg's elementary PNT proof. Define (L(n)=\log n), the von Mangoldt function (Lambda), and
\[
\Lambda_2=\mu*L^2,\qquad S_2(X)=\sum_{n\le X}\Lambda_2(n).
\]
The exact coefficient identity is
\[
\boxed{\Lambda_2(n)=\Lambda(n)\log n+(\Lambda*\Lambda)(n)}.
\]
Thus (S_2) is the sum of two nonnegative channels: a prime-power/log channel and an ordered pair-of-prime-powers channel. Selberg's symmetry estimate is (S_2(X)=2X\log X+O(X)). Removing higher prime powers changes the sum by (O(X)), yielding the familiar prime-only presentation
\[
\sum_{p\le X}\log^2p+\sum_{pq\le X}\log p\log q=2X\log X+O(X),
\]
where the second sum is over ordered prime pairs, including (p=q). This is a known classical baseline, not a new PNT proof by this project.

### 2. DERIVATION

The integer factorization identity 
\[
\log n=\sum_{d\mid n}\Lambda(d)
\]
follows by writing (n=\prod_p p^{a_p}): the (a_p) divisors (p,p^2,\ldots,p^{a_p}) each contribute \(\log p\). Multiply by \(\log n\) and use \(\log(n/d)=\sum_{u\mid n/d}\Lambda(u)\):
\[
\log^2n=\sum_{d\mid n}\Lambda(d)\log d+
\sum_{v\mid n}\sum_{d\mid v}\Lambda(d)\Lambda(v/d).
\]
Möbius inversion gives the boxed identity. Its local coefficients are nonnegative: for (n=p^a), 
\(\Lambda_2(n)=(2a-1)\log^2p\); for (n=p^a q^b), (p\ne q), it is (2\log p\log q); with at least three distinct prime bases it is zero.

Summing the definition and reversing the finite divisor sums gives the exact integer-lattice expression
\[
S_2(X)=\sum_{d\le X}\mu(d)A(X/d),\qquad
A(y)=\sum_{m\le y}\log^2m.
\]
Integral comparison, using \(\int_1^y\log^2t\,dt=y(\log^2y-2\log y+2)-2\), yields
\[
A(y)=y(\log^2y-2\log y+2)+O(\log^2(2y)).
\]
The accumulated remainder is (O(X)), since \(\sum_{d\le X}\log^2(2X/d)=O(X)\). Put
\[
M_j(X)=\sum_{d\le X}\frac{\mu(d)}d\log^j(X/d),\quad j=0,1,2.
\]
The elementary Möbius-inversion/harmonic-sum estimates are
\[
M_0(X)=O(1),\quad M_1(X)=O(1),\quad M_2(X)=2\log X+O(1).
\]
For clarity, (M_0=O(1)) follows from the exact identity \(\sum_{d\le X}\mu(d)\lfloor X/d\rfloor=1\) and \(\lfloor X/d\rfloor=X/d+O(1)\). Applying the same Möbius inversion to \(X\) and (X\log X), and using
\[
\sum_{n\le X}\frac Xn=X\log X+\gamma X+O(1),\qquad
\sum_{n\le X}\frac Xn\log(X/n)=\tfrac X2\log^2X+\gamma X\log X+O(X),
\]
gives (M_1=O(1)) and (M_2=2\log X+O(1)). Substitution into the expansion of (A) gives
\[
S_2(X)=X(M_2-2M_1+2M_0)+O(X)=2X\log X+O(X).
\]
All constants in these remainder terms are absolute; this pass does not optimize them. This is the standard Selberg symmetry calculation; see Selberg's original proof and the detailed derivation in Balady's exposition.

For the prime-only form, the omitted one-channel higher powers contribute (O(\sqrt X\log^3X)=O(X)). In the pair channel, terms with at least one higher prime power are bounded by
\[
O\!\left(X\sum_{p,\,a\ge2}\frac{\log p}{p^a}\right)=O(X),
\]
using the elementary Chebyshev estimate \(\psi(t)=O(t)\) and convergence of the displayed prime-power series. Thus the prime-only formula has the same (O(X)) error order.

### 3. NUMERIC CHECK

An exact Eratosthenes sieve classified primes and prime powers through (X=10^6); logarithmic sums used 55-decimal-digit mpmath arithmetic. The table compares the positive two-channel coefficient sum with (2X\log X), and separately shows the prime-only truncation. An independent direct evaluation of \(\sum_{n\le X}(\mu*\log^2)(n)\) agreed with the positive channel sum to the displayed precision at (X=10,100,1000,10000); absolute residuals were (3.27\times10^{-55},1.05\times10^{-53},3.35\times10^{-52},2.68\times10^{-50}), respectively. Those checks validate finite identities, not asymptotics.

| 100 | 602.97017506025916073 | 486.37388113686763562 | 116.596293923391525 | 921.034037197618274 | −318.063862137359113 | −3.18063862137359113 |

The normalized residual stays near (-3.15) on these cutoffs, consistent with the proved (O(X)) remainder. No asymptotic constant or trend is inferred from these six values.

### 4. ERROR ANALYSIS

The symmetry identity has an (O(X)) error, smaller than its (2X\log X) main term by a logarithmic factor. Selberg–Erdős use the identity, Chebyshev bounds, and an iterative Tauberian/bootstrap argument to prove \(\psi(X)=X+o(X)\), so the classical mechanism reaches M0. This pass does not extract an explicit decay rate from that bootstrap: the displayed identity by itself is not an estimate of \(\psi(X)-X\), and the iteration only supplies the qualitative PNT conclusion here. It therefore reaches no M1–M6 milestone. The exact loss for the present error objective is the absence of a quantified contraction/rate in the qualitative bootstrap, not the failure of the known PNT argument.

### 5. FILTERS

- **F-LATTICE — PASS.** Exact integer divisibility, the floor identity, and harmonic sums over the ordinary lattice enter the evaluation. The argument is not an unchanged argument for arbitrary Beurling integers with only regular counting.
- **F-MULT — PASS.** The von Mangoldt divisor identity and its Möbius inversion use unique factorization essentially.
- **F-GLOBAL — PASS only for the complete moving prime system.** Every prime power contributes to \(\Lambda\) and the pair convolution; no fixed finite-prime truncation or uniform limit is asserted.

### 6. MILESTONE REACHED

**M0, literature-established.** The reconstructed symmetry formula is the classical input to the Selberg–Erdős elementary PNT proof. This pass recovers the identity and checks it; it does not claim the PNT as a new result or independently formalize the full Tauberian iteration.

### 7. WHY

The positive two-channel decomposition is strong enough, when coupled to the global symmetry identity, to support the classical qualitative PNT bootstrap. Its (O(X)) arithmetic remainder does not itself carry a quantified decay for \(R(X)=\psi(X)-X\). The remaining error-rate loss is an artifact of the qualitative proof extraction in this pass, not a no-go theorem for Selberg-type identities; later elementary methods obtain quantitative subexponential errors by adding further arguments.

Source audit: the Archive of Formal Proofs page for `Selberg_Asymptotic_Formula` states the formula, but its proof imports `Prime_Number_Theorem.Prime_Counting_Functions` and explicitly invokes `ψ.asymptotics`; it is therefore not independent formal evidence for deriving the PNT from Selberg's formula. Selberg's original paper and Balady's derivation are the cited proof sources for the benchmark.

### 8. LESSON

The quadratic positive convolution is a genuine M0 bridge and should be the baseline against which new positive identities are compared. To advance beyond M0, preserve its full multiplicative coupling while proving a quantified improvement to the Selberg–Erdős bootstrap; a sharper numerical fit to the (O(X)) remainder is not enough.

### 9. FAILURE CLASS

**New class 11: Selberg symmetry reaches M0 but this replay supplies no explicit error improvement.** The classical PNT result is literature-established; the numerical checks do not prove the asymptotic or any M1+ estimate. No no-go theorem is claimed.

## Pass 15 — Squarefree log-lattice mass and composite-channel dominance

### 1. MECHANISM

Let \(\mu^2(n)\) be the squarefree indicator and define the nonnegative statistic
\[
Q(X)=\sum_{n\le X}\mu^2(n)\log n.
\]
Its exact arithmetic-channel split is
\[
Q(X)=\theta(X)+C_{\rm sf}(X),\qquad
\theta(X)=\sum_{p\le X}\log p,\qquad
C_{\rm sf}(X)=\sum_{\substack{n\le X,\ n\text{ squarefree composite}}}\log n.
\]
Thus the prime channel occurs with positive coefficient one. The candidate tests whether the exact one-dimensional squarefree lattice sum evaluates that channel selectively or is dominated by squarefree composites.

### 2. DERIVATION

The exact identity \(\mu^2(n)=\sum_{d^2\mid n}\mu(d)\) gives, with \(D=\lfloor\sqrt X\rfloor\), \(Y_d=X/d^2\), and \(A(y)=\sum_{m\le y}\log m\),
\[
Q(X)=\sum_{d\le D}\mu(d)\bigl(A(Y_d)+2\log d\,\lfloor Y_d\rfloor\bigr).
\]
For \(y\ge1\), Stirling's formula and \(n=\lfloor y\rfloor\) give the explicit bound
\[
|A(y)-(y\log y-y)|\le 2\log(2y)+2.
\]
Replacing \(\lfloor Y_d\rfloor\) by \(Y_d\) costs at most \(2\log d\); the main terms combine exactly because
\[
Y_d(\log Y_d-1)+2Y_d\log d=\frac X{d^2}(\log X-1).
\]
Therefore
\[
\left|Q(X)-X(\log X-1)\sum_{d\le D}\frac{\mu(d)}{d^2}\right|
\le D(2\log(2X)+2).
\]
Since \(\sum_{d\ge1}\mu(d)d^{-2}=1/\zeta(2)=6/\pi^2\), and \(\sum_{d>D}d^{-2}\le1/D\le2/\sqrt X\) for \(X\ge4\),
\[
\boxed{\left|Q(X)-\frac6{\pi^2}X(\log X-1)\right|
\le \sqrt X\,(4\log X+2\log2),\quad X\ge4.}
\]
This is a complete lattice asymptotic with an explicit error bound. Independently, the elementary Chebyshev estimate \(\theta(X)=O(X)\) implies
\[
C_{\rm sf}(X)=\frac6{\pi^2}X\log X+O(X),
\]
so the positive aggregate is asymptotically composite-dominated: the prime channel is only \(O(X)\), while the total is of order \(X\log X\). This uses no PNT.

### 3. NUMERIC CHECK

An exact sieve classified primes and squarefree integers through \(10^6\); logarithms and sums were evaluated at 55-digit Decimal precision. The full-precision run is archived in `scripts/scale_separation_pass15_output.txt`.

| 1,000 | 3,586.925553870351 | 956.245265120059 | 2,630.680288750292 | 3,591.484545214437 | −4.558991344087 | −0.020870450527 |
| 10,000 | 49,939.518717376653 | 9,895.991379156987 | 40,043.527338219666 | 49,912.884275705917 | 26.634441670736 | 0.028917977615 |
| 100,000 | 639,114.908725833049 | 99,685.389268612551 | 539,429.519457220498 | 639,109.230992674631 | 5.677733158418 | 0.001559514025 |
| 1,000,000 | 7,790,868.568487872858 | 998,484.175025634292 | 6,792,384.393462238566 | 7,790,896.192282900894 | −27.623795028036 | −0.001999476958 |

The measured prime share \(\theta(X)/Q(X)\) at these cutoffs is respectively 0.56634, 0.38524, 0.26659, 0.19816, 0.15597, and 0.12816. These are diagnostics only; the proved Chebyshev bound, not the observed trend, establishes the \(O(1/\log X)\) upper bound for the asymptotic prime share.

### 4. ERROR ANALYSIS

The mechanism proves an asymptotic for \(Q(X)\), but not for its prime subchannel. Since \(Q(X)=\theta(X)+C_{\rm sf}(X)\) and \(C_{\rm sf}(X)=(6/\pi^2)X\log X+O(X)\), its leading term comes from composites. Recovering \(\theta(X)\) from this equality requires evaluating the composite channel to accuracy \(o(X)\); the lattice estimate controls only the total to \(O(\sqrt X\log X)\), and gives no independent composite evaluation at that precision. Consequently it gives no nontrivial bound for \(\psi(X)-X\); adding the standard higher-prime-power estimate \(\psi(X)-\theta(X)=O(\sqrt X\log^2 X)\) does not repair the missing estimate for \(\theta(X)-X\). No Tauberian step is available because the obstruction occurs before inversion: the known positive aggregate is dominated by the unknown composite channel. The candidate reaches below M0.

### 5. FILTERS

- **F-LATTICE — PASS.** The derivation uses the exact ordinary-integer identity \(\mu^2(n)=\sum_{d^2\mid n}\mu(d)\), floor cutoffs, and the log-factorial lattice sum; these are not implied by regular generalized-integer counts.
- **F-MULT — PASS.** Squarefreeness, Möbius inversion, and \(\sum\mu(d)d^{-2}=1/\zeta(2)\) use unique factorization/Euler-product structure essentially.
- **F-GLOBAL — PASS only for the moving complete system.** The sum includes every squarefree integer through \(X\) and all square divisors through \(\sqrt X\); no fixed finite-prime approximation is claimed uniform.

### 6. MILESTONE REACHED

**Below M0 for \(\psi(X)-X\).** The exact positive statistic has the explicit asymptotic above, but it does not yield \(\psi(X)\sim X\) or any prime-error estimate.

### 7. WHY

Squarefree restriction makes the integer-side density evaluable, but not prime-selective: almost all of the leading \(X\log X\) mass is in squarefree composites, whereas the prime channel is \(O(X)\). This is a proved obstruction for this aggregate statistic, not a no-go for all squarefree or lattice mechanisms. The loss is intrinsic to using the unweighted squarefree log mass; alternative weights/projections remain open.

### 8. LESSON

When a positive multiplicative statistic has a known lattice asymptotic, compare the target channel's rigorous order with the total before treating the asymptotic as prime information. A next candidate must suppress the squarefree-composite bulk while retaining a positive prime channel and an independently evaluable lattice aggregate.

### 9. FAILURE CLASS

**New class 12: squarefree log-lattice aggregate is composite-dominated.** The explicit asymptotic and Chebyshev bound prove that the target prime channel is lower order in this statistic; no universal no-go is claimed.

## Pass 16 — Central-binomial prime-band valuation

### 1. MECHANISM

For an integer \(n\ge1\), use the positive factorial-lattice statistic
\[
B(n)=\log\binom{2n}{n}.
\]
Legendre's formula gives the exact prime-power expansion
\[
B(n)=\sum_{p^k\le2n}\left(\left\lfloor\frac{2n}{p^k}\right\rfloor-2\left\lfloor\frac n{p^k}\right\rfloor\right)\log p.
\]
Every coefficient is 0 or 1. In particular, the prime channel \(n<p\le2n\) occurs with coefficient exactly one, so \(\theta(2n)-\theta(n)\le B(n)\). Unlike the previous squarefree aggregate, this is a dyadic prime-band detector with an independently evaluated factorial total.

### 2. DERIVATION

The exponent of \(p\) in \(m!\) is \(\sum_{k\ge1}\lfloor m/p^k\rfloor\). Subtracting twice the exponent in \(n!\) from the exponent in \((2n)!\) proves the expansion. For every integer \(q\ge1\), \(\lfloor2n/q\rfloor-2\lfloor n/q\rfloor\in\{0,1\}\), by writing \(n=aq+r\), \(0\le r<q\). Hence all channels are nonnegative. If \(n<p\le2n\), the first-power coefficient is one and all higher powers exceed \(2n\), establishing the positive target channel.

Stirling's expansion with \(|R_m|\le1/(360m^3)\) in
\(\log(m!)=(m+\tfrac12)\log m-m+\tfrac12\log(2\pi)+1/(12m)+R_m\)
gives
\[
B(n)=2n\log2-\tfrac12\log(\pi n)-\frac1{8n}+E_n,
\qquad |E_n|\le\frac{17}{2880n^3}.
\]
Thus the total is known to an explicit tiny error, while the exact positive identity gives only an upper bound for the selected dyadic prime band.

Summing the upper bound on dyadic intervals yields a standard Chebyshev estimate. If \(2^J\ge X\) is the least power of two above \(X\), then
\[
\theta(X)\le\theta(2^J)\le\sum_{j=1}^J\log\binom{2^j}{2^{j-1}}
\le\sum_{j=1}^J2^j\log2<4X\log2.
\]
Consequently, for \(X\ge2\),
\[
\psi(X)=\sum_{k\le\log_2X}\theta(X^{1/k})
\le4\log2\left(X+\sqrt X\log_2X\right)=O(X).
\]
This is only a Chebyshev upper bound. There is no matching lower estimate for \(\psi(X)\) or \(\theta(X)\), so the PNT and every listed prime-error milestone remain out of reach.

### 3. NUMERIC CHECK

An exact Eratosthenes sieve supplied the primes through \(10^6\). All Legendre floor coefficients were computed as integers, logarithmic sums and log-factorials at 60-digit mpmath precision, and the prime-power reconstruction residual is at most \(2.55\times10^{-57}\) in the displayed samples. Full raw output is archived in `scripts/scale_separation_pass16_output.txt`.

The exact coefficient identity and Stirling approximation agree within the stated bound. The increasing sampled target-channel share is not used to infer an asymptotic or a lower bound.

### 4. ERROR ANALYSIS

For each dyadic interval the identity gives \(0\le\theta(2n)-\theta(n)\le B(n)=2n\log2+O(\log n)\). Summation proves \(\theta(X)=O(X)\) and hence \(\psi(X)=O(X)\); it does not prove \(\theta(X)\ge cX\), \(\psi(X)\sim X\), or a bound for \(\psi(X)-X\) better than \(O(X)\). The loss occurs when the known total \(B(n)\) is used only as an upper bound: lower-prime and prime-power channels are nonnegative but no sufficiently sharp independent upper bound for their sum is available, so subtracting them to obtain a lower bound would reintroduce the unproved prime information. No Tauberian step repairs this one-sided channel inequality. Milestone: below M0.

### 5. FILTERS

- **F-LATTICE — PASS.** The central binomial coefficient and its Stirling evaluation use the exact consecutive-integer lattice and factorials; the construction is not available unchanged for generalized integers without ordinary binomial coefficients.
- **F-MULT — PASS.** The prime-power channel decomposition uses unique factorization and Legendre valuations essentially.
- **F-GLOBAL — PASS only for the complete moving system.** Every prime power up to \(2n\) contributes to the exact factorization as \(n\) grows; no fixed-prime truncation is claimed uniform.

### 6. MILESTONE REACHED

**Below M0.** The candidate reproduces an elementary Chebyshev upper bound \(\psi(X)=O(X)\), but not the prime number theorem. This upper bound is classical and is not a new result of the search.

### 7. WHY

The factorial lattice gives an extremely accurate total, and the target prime band is a positive subchannel. Positivity therefore certifies an upper bound but cannot make the target equal to the total; lower-scale prime powers contribute a nonnegative remainder whose size is itself prime-distribution data. This is intrinsic to using this single central-binomial total as the certificate, not a no-go for combinations of factorial identities or other positive transforms.

### 8. LESSON

A factorial identity can isolate a precise dyadic prime band with coefficient one. To get a lower estimate, the next candidate must eliminate or independently evaluate the lower-prime-power remainder without subtracting an uncontrolled positive channel; merely sharpening Stirling's error cannot help, since the factorial asymptotic is already much more accurate than the missing channel estimate.

### 9. FAILURE CLASS

**New class 13: factorial valuation gives a dyadic prime upper bound but no matching lower bound.** The exact positive channel identity, Stirling evaluation, and Chebyshev consequence are proved; no universal no-go is claimed.

## Pass 17 — Prime-exponent energy of adjacent factorial ratios

### 1. MECHANISM

Take the exact adjacent central-binomial ratio
\[
\Delta_n=\log\binom{2n}{n}-\log\binom{2n-2}{n-1}
=\log\frac{2(2n-1)}n,
\]
and its positive lattice energy \(A(M)=\sum_{n\le M}\Delta_n^2\). Its multiplicative factorization has prime-exponent coordinates
\[
a_{2,n}=1-v_2(n),\qquad a_{p,n}=v_p(2n-1)-v_p(n)\quad(p\text{ odd}),
\qquad \Delta_n=\sum_p a_{p,n}\log p.
\]
The diagonal prime-coordinate energy \(E(M)=\sum_{n\le M}\sum_p a_{p,n}^2\log^2p\) is nonnegative. Since \(\gcd(n,2n-1)=1\), each odd-prime square splits into two nonnegative valuation squares. In particular every odd prime \(M<p\le2M\) contributes exactly \(\log^2p\) through the progression \(2n-1\), giving a positive dyadic prime-band subchannel.

### 2. DERIVATION

For odd \(p\), \(v_p(n)v_p(2n-1)=0\), so
\[
a_{p,n}^2=v_p(n)^2+v_p(2n-1)^2.
\]
The valuation identity \(v^2=\sum_{k=1}^v(2k-1)\) gives the exact residue-count formula
\[
E(M)=\log^2 2\left(M-\left\lfloor\frac M2\right\rfloor+\sum_{k\ge2}(2k-3)\left\lfloor\frac M{2^k}\right\rfloor\right)
+\sum_{p\text{ odd}}\log^2p\sum_{k\ge1}(2k-1)\left(\left\lfloor\frac M{p^k}\right\rfloor+\left\lfloor\frac{M+(p^k-1)/2}{p^k}\right\rfloor\right).
\]
The sums are finite: the first odd-prime floor vanishes for \(p^k>M\), and the second for \(p^k>2M-1\). The count in the second progression follows because \(2n\equiv1\pmod{p^k}\) has residue \((p^k+1)/2\).

The total \(A(M)\) is independently smooth. Write \(L=\log4\) and \(u_n=\log(1-1/(2n))\). Then \(u_n=-1/(2n)+r_n\), with \(|r_n|\le1/(4n^2)\), and \(u_n^2\le1/n^2\). Thus
\[
A(M)=ML^2-L\log M+O(1),
\quad \left|A(M)-(ML^2-L\log M)\right|
\le L\left(1+\frac{\pi^2}{12}\right)+\frac{\pi^2}{6}.
\]
This known total is not the positive diagonal energy: exactly,
\[
A(M)=E(M)+2\sum_{n\le M}\sum_{p<q}a_{p,n}a_{q,n}\log p\log q,
\]
and the cross-channel term has no fixed sign.

### 3. NUMERIC CHECK

An exact Eratosthenes sieve supplied primes through \(2\times10^6\); floor-count coefficients were exact integers, the total lattice sum used 55-digit Decimal arithmetic, and prime logarithms used 60-digit mpmath. The floor formula for \(E\) matched direct factorization at \(M=10,100,1000\) with zero displayed discrepancy. Full output is in `scripts/scale_separation_pass17_output.txt`.

At the largest cutoff the positive diagonal is about 104 times the known smooth total, and the signed cross term cancels it almost completely. These are finite measurements, not a claimed limiting law.

### 4. ERROR ANALYSIS

The lattice statistic has the explicit asymptotic \(A(M)=M\log^2 4-\log4\log M+O(1)\), but it does not estimate \(E(M)\) or the prime-band sum: the identity includes an uncontrolled signed cross term. The computed prime band is a positive component of \(E\), not of \(A\) with a nonnegative remainder, so no upper or lower bound for that band follows from \(A\) alone. Consequently this candidate gives no nontrivial estimate for \(\psi(X)-X\), no Tauberian input, and remains below M0. The precise loss is loss of positivity when the prime-coordinate norm is reassembled into the known scalar square.

### 5. FILTERS

- **F-LATTICE — PASS for the tested transform.** The smooth total comes from exact adjacent factorial ratios; the channel formula uses exact residue counts for \(n\) and \(2n-1\), structure absent from general Beurling integers.
- **F-MULT — PASS.** The decomposition uses prime valuations and unique factorization essentially.
- **F-GLOBAL — PASS only for the complete moving system.** All odd primes up to \(2M\) enter the exact diagonal as \(M\) grows; no fixed-prime approximation is asserted uniform.

### 6. MILESTONE REACHED

**Below M0.** The exact smooth total and positive prime-coordinate energy are defined and computed, but their signed relation yields no PNT or prime-error bound.

### 7. WHY

Squaring a known scalar factorial increment is positive, and its prime-coordinate diagonal is also positive, but these are different quadratic objects: the cross terms have mixed signs and can overwhelm the diagonal. The tested statistic therefore hides prime-scale energy behind coherent cancellation. This is a specific failure of this reconstruction, related to but distinct from the residue-class cross-cancellation in Pass 13; no general no-go for quadratic positivity is proved.

### 8. LESSON

Do not infer a positive arithmetic diagonal from a positive square of its signed sum. A viable quadratic candidate must make prime-coordinate channels orthogonal by an exact identity, or independently bound the full cross Gram at the target scale; the known smooth value of the scalar square does neither.

### 9. FAILURE CLASS

**New class 14: prime-exponent diagonal is hidden by signed cross-channel cancellation.** The decomposition is exact and the severe cancellation is measured through \(10^6\); no universal obstruction is claimed.

## Pass 18 — Recursive quartic positive divisor transform

### 1. MECHANISM

Let `L(n)=log n`, and for arithmetic functions use Dirichlet convolution `*`. Start from `Lambda_1=Lambda=mu*L`, and iterate the positive operator
\[
\mathcal T(F)(n)=F(n)\log n+(F*\Lambda)(n).
\]
The new candidate is the next iterate
\[
\Lambda_4=\mathcal T(\Lambda_3)=\Lambda_3\,L+\Lambda_3*\Lambda=\mu*L^4.
\]
It is nonnegative pointwise, and its support is integers with at most four distinct prime factors. By (1*\mu=\varepsilon),
\[
\sum_{d\mid n}\Lambda_4(d)=\log^4n,
\qquad
T_4(X):=\sum_{n\le X}\log^4n=\sum_{d\le X}\Lambda_4(d)\left\lfloor\frac Xd\right\rfloor.
\]
This is a positive exact lattice transform. Its exponent-one prime channel is
\[
P_4(X)=\sum_{p\le X}(\log p)^4\left\lfloor\frac Xp\right\rfloor,
\]
with separate positive higher-prime-power and at-least-two-prime-factor channels. The question tested here is whether one more positive recursion step suppresses those complementary channels or localizes the divisor scale.

### 2. DERIVATION

For `$k\ge1$`, define $\Lambda_k=\mu*L^k$ and (D_f(s)=\sum_{n\ge1}f(n)n^{-s}), for $\Re s>1$. Then
\[
D_{\Lambda_k}(s)=(-1)^k\frac{\zeta^{(k)}(s)}{\zeta(s)},\qquad D_\Lambda(s)=-\frac{\zeta'(s)}{\zeta(s)}.
\]
Pointwise multiplication by (L) satisfies (D_{F L}=-D'_F), while Dirichlet convolution satisfies (D_{F*\Lambda}=D_FD_\Lambda). Therefore
\[
D_{\Lambda_k L+\Lambda_k*\Lambda}
=-D'_{\Lambda_k}+D_{\Lambda_k}D_\Lambda
=(-1)^{k+1}\frac{\zeta^{(k+1)}}{\zeta}=D_{\Lambda_{k+1}}.
\]
Uniqueness of Dirichlet coefficients proves the recurrence. Since $\Lambda\ge0$, (L(n)\ge0), and convolution preserves nonnegativity, induction proves (\Lambda_4(n)\ge0).

For (n=\prod_{i=1}^r p_i^{a_i}), write (x=\log n) and $\ell_i=\log p_i>0$. Directly from $\mu*L^4$,
\[
\Lambda_4(n)=\sum_{S\subseteq\{1,\ldots,r\}}(-1)^{|S|}\left(x-\sum_{i\in S}\ell_i\right)^4.
\]
This is the `r`-fold backward finite difference of `x^4`. It vanishes for `$r>4$`. For (1\le r\le4), the repeated-integral formula is
\[
\Lambda_4(n)=\int_0^{\ell_1}\cdots\int_0^{\ell_r}
\frac{4!}{(4-r)!}\left(x-t_1-\cdots-t_r\right)^{4-r}
\,dt_r\cdots dt_1\ge0,
\]
because (x-\sum_i t_i\ge\sum_i(a_i-1)\ell_i\ge0). In particular, on prime powers,
\[
\Lambda_4(p^a)=\bigl(a^4-(a-1)^4\bigr)(\log p)^4
=(4a^3-6a^2+4a-1)(\log p)^4.
\]
Finally, (1*\Lambda_4=(1*\mu)*L^4=\varepsilon*L^4=L^4). Summing over (n\le X) and reversing the finite divisor sum gives the displayed floor identity exactly; no remainder enters this transform.

For the known all-integer total, (f(t)=\log^4t) is increasing on ([1,\infty)), and
\[
\int_1^X f(t)\,dt\le\sum_{n=1}^Xf(n)\le\int_1^Xf(t)\,dt+f(X).
\]
The integral is
\[
I_4(X)=X(\log^4X-4\log^3X+12\log^2X-24\log X+24)-24,
\]
so the explicit error is (0\le T_4(X)-I_4(X)\le\log^4X).

### 3. NUMERIC CHECK

The smallest-prime-factor sieve and all factorizations were exact through `X=10^6`. Logarithms and accumulations used 55-digit mpmath arithmetic. The pointwise divisor identity was checked for every `$2\le n\le2000$`; its maximum absolute residual was (1.67047794381\times10^{-52}). The local formula produced zero negative coefficients through `$10^6$`. The floor transform was independently accumulated and matched (T_4) with residual at most (1.7516231\times10^{-44}) at the displayed cutoffs. These are finite identity checks, not asymptotic evidence.

### 4. ERROR ANALYSIS

The exact positive transform evaluates (T_4(X)), and the smooth integer-lattice approximation has the explicit error (0\le T_4-I_4\le\log^4X). But its prime channel is the broad weighted sum `P_4(X)=sum_{p<=X}log^4(p) floor(X/p)`, not (\theta(X)) or (\psi(X)); the complementary higher-power and composite channels remain positive and are not independently evaluated. At `$X=10^6$`, composites account for about 70.2% of the known total, while the exponent-one prime channel accounts for about 29.4%. These finite proportions prove no limiting dominance statement.

No Tauberian or inversion step yields an estimate for (\psi(X)-X) from this identity. Removing the floor weights or subtracting the composite channels requires additional information about primes, and Möbius inversion returns signed coefficients. Thus this pass supplies no new prime-counting error bound and no milestone M0–M6; in particular it does not improve the background Chebyshev bound (\psi(X)=O(X)). The precise loss is the same channel-evaluation gap as the cubic positive transform, now with a fourth-factor channel and a reciprocal-scale floor weight.

### 5. FILTERS

- **F-LATTICE — PASS.** The exact evaluation is the ordinary integer identity with multiplicity $\lfloor X/d\rfloor$, and its explicit error comes from the ordinary evenly spaced lattice. It does not transfer unchanged to a generalized-integer system without those floor counts.
- **F-MULT — PASS.** The construction uses $\mu$, $\Lambda$, Dirichlet convolution, unique factorization, and the support classification by distinct prime bases essentially.
- **F-GLOBAL — PASS only for the complete moving system.** Every (d\le X) and every prime factor through (X) participates. No fixed-prime truncation or cutoff-independent finite approximation is proved.

### 6. MILESTONE REACHED

**Below M0 for (\psi(X)-X).** The quartic positive identity is exact and its integer-side total has an explicit (O$\log^4X$) error, but it gives no PNT conclusion or nontrivial error for (\psi(X)-X).

### 7. WHY

The recursive rule (F\mapsto F\log+F*\Lambda) preserves coefficient positivity but broadens the arithmetic support from one through three distinct prime bases to one through four. In the exact lattice sum, the floor kernel still weights all divisor scales; at `X=10^6`, just over half the total comes from (d\le X/10). The prime information remains embedded alongside a substantial composite channel. This is a repeated, measured limitation of the tested generalized-Mangoldt positive-transform family, not a theorem ruling out all higher-order or differently projected identities.

### 8. LESSON

When iterating a positive convolution identity, prove the recursive sign and support properties, then measure both target-channel share and divisor-scale localization. Positivity survives this quartic step, but that alone does not improve selectivity. A next candidate must change the extraction observable or produce an independently controlled positive complement; merely increasing the polynomial degree adds channels without providing their separate evaluation.

### 9. FAILURE CLASS

**Existing class 1, second instance: positive higher-order divisor weights do not isolate primes.** Pass 18 extends the tested cubic case to the recursively generated quartic transform. The positivity and exact lattice identity are proved; the measured channel mixture is finite evidence, not a universal no-go theorem.

## Pass 19 — Trinomial factorial prime-band transform

## 1. Mechanism

For an integer n>=1 set Y=3n and

```text
A_3(n) = log((3n)!/(n!)^3).
```

Legendre's valuation formula gives an exact sum of nonnegative prime-power channels. For q=p^a,

```text
c_n(q) = floor(3n/q) - 3 floor(n/q) = floor(3 {n/q}) in {0,1,2},
A_3(n) = sum_{p^a<=3n} c_n(p^a) log p.
```

For every prime in the terminal band 3n/2 < p <= 3n, c_n(p)=1. Thus the selected positive subchannel is

```text
Theta(3n) - Theta(3n/2) = sum_{3n/2 < p <= 3n} log p.
```

All remaining prime and prime-power channels are also nonnegative. The candidate asks whether the exactly evaluable trinomial total can control the selected band from below.

## 2. Derivation

The p-adic valuation of a factorial is v_p(m!)=sum_{a>=1} floor(m/p^a). Therefore

```text
v_p((3n)!/(n!)^3) = sum_{a>=1} [floor(3n/p^a)-3 floor(n/p^a)].
```

Writing n/q=m+r with m integer and 0<=r<1 makes each bracket floor(3r), hence it is 0, 1, or 2. Multiply by log p and sum over primes to obtain the displayed identity, since log((3n)!/(n!)^3)=sum_p v_p((3n)!/(n!)^3)log p. This proves positivity for every channel and every n.

If p>3n/2, then p>n and p<=3n gives floor(3n/p)=1 and floor(n/p)=0, so the coefficient is exactly one. This proves the target-band inclusion with no approximation.

Stirling's formula with remainder |R_m|<=1/(360m^3) in

```text
log(m!) = (m+1/2)log(m)-m+(1/2)log(2 pi)+1/(12m)+R_m
```

gives

```text
A_3(n) = 3n log(3) - log(n) - log(2 pi/sqrt(3)) - 2/(9n) + E_n,
|E_n| <= 41/(4860 n^3).
```

Indeed, the exact asymptotic expansion of the residual begins with 2/(243 n^3). For the rigorous bound, the entire residual is R_(3n)-3R_n, so its absolute value is at most 1/(360(3n)^3)+3/(360n^3)=41/(4860n^3). Thus the total has an explicit O(n) evaluation with the stated rigorous remainder.

## 3. Numerical check

An exact smallest-prime-factor sieve and exact integer floor coefficients were used through Y=1,000,002. Natural logarithms and factorial logarithms used 60-digit mpmath arithmetic. The run checked c_n(p^a) in {0,1,2} at every channel in each displayed n, independently summed the channels, and compared against log((3n)!/(n!)^3).
