At Y=1,000,002, the complete channel mass by q=p^a ranges over q<=Y/10, (Y/10,Y/3], (Y/3,Y/2], and (Y/2,Y] in shares 0.0938189957599, 0.148191172878, 0.303344753808, and 0.454645077554. The target band is 0.454365407748 of the total; other exponent-one prime channels are 0.544554438388 and higher prime powers 0.00108015386334. The independently summed identity residual is -9.787956702e-55. The complete raw run is `scripts/scale_separation_pass19_output.txt`; the reproducible script is `scripts/scale_separation_pass19.py`.

These computations check finite identities and channel proportions only; they do not establish limiting proportions.

## 4. Error analysis

The exact identity and positivity imply only

```text
Theta(3n)-Theta(3n/2) <= A_3(n) = 3n log(3)+O(log n).
```

Taking n through powers of two gives geometric intervals (3n/2,3n] that tile the positive axis up to endpoints. Summing the O(n) upper bounds over these intervals yields the classical Chebyshev estimate Theta(X)=O(X), and then psi(X)=O(X) by psi(X)=sum_{k<=log_2 X} Theta(X^(1/k)). It gives no lower bound for the target band and no nontrivial bound for psi(X)-X. The precise loss is the uncontrolled positive remainder consisting of all other prime and prime-power channels. Sharper Stirling errors do not address that remainder. No Tauberian or signed inversion step repairs the missing complementary-channel estimate.

## 5. Filters

- **F-LATTICE: PASS.** The exact total uses ordinary factorials and the ordinary consecutive-integer lattice; it is not an identity for a general Beurling integer sequence.
- **F-MULT: PASS.** Legendre valuations and the decomposition into p-adic channels use unique factorization essentially.
- **F-GLOBAL: PASS only for the complete moving system.** Every prime power through 3n is included; no fixed finite set of primes gives a uniform limit.

## 6. Milestone reached

**Below M0 for psi(X)-X.** The argument recovers at most a classical Chebyshev upper bound, not the PNT. It yields no improvement to any error milestone.

## 7. Why

Increasing the factorial arity moves the exact coefficient-one prime band but does not remove the many other positive channels. The known total is therefore an upper certificate for the selected prime sum, not a lower certificate. This is a repeated instance of the single-total factorial limitation from Pass 16, not a no-go for combinations of factorial identities or independently evaluated channel projections.

## 8. Lesson

Do not change only the factorial arity after a one-sided channel bound. The next useful factorial-based candidate must control the complementary channels independently or combine identities so that they cancel through a proved positive projection. Record that requirement without assuming such a projection exists.

## 9. Failure class

**Existing class 13, second instance: factorial valuation gives a prime-band upper bound without a matching lower bound.** Pass 19 shifts the coefficient-one band from (n,2n] in Pass 16 to (3n/2,3n]. Positivity and an exact total persist, but the complement remains uncontrolled. This is evidence about these two totals, not a universal no-go.

## Pass 20 — Consolidation: what positivity of a known scalar total can and cannot say

This is the required tenth-pass consolidation, not a new arithmetic mechanism. After Pass 19 the maximum failure-class frequency is two, shared by four classes: higher-order divisor weights (Passes 1, 18), norm-lattice leakage (4, 6), truncated-divisor rough-composite blindness (7, 8), and factorial prime-band upper bounds (16, 19). I consolidate the factorial class because its two instances have the same exact logical form and the latest computation provides a second independent arity. The other tied classes remain as previously classified; no claim is made that one is statistically more prevalent.

## Restricted no-go theorem: scalar total plus positivity

Let A be a known nonnegative scalar and suppose the only information about a selected component T and its complement R is

```text
A = T + R,     T >= 0,     R >= 0.
```

Then the set of values of T compatible with those data is exactly [0,A]. Necessity follows from T>=0 and R=A-T>=0; sufficiency follows because for every t in [0,A], the assignment T=t and R=A-t satisfies all three conditions. Therefore those data imply T<=A but imply no strictly positive lower bound for T. This is a complete proof of an information-level no-go for deductions that use only a known total and nonnegativity of its components.

For Pass 16, T is theta(2n)-theta(n) and A=log binom(2n,n). For Pass 19, T is theta(3n)-theta(3n/2) and A=log((3n)!/(n!)^3). In each case the exact Legendre expansion proves A=T+R with R>=0, and Stirling evaluates A. Hence the present argument extracts the upper bound T<=A. The abstract theorem does **not** say that arbitrary assignments in [0,A] arise from primes, and it does not prove that no further arithmetic identity can bound R. The arithmetic prime-error no-go is therefore only **PARTIAL**: an independent upper bound for the actual complementary channels, or a coupled identity that eliminates them, could still succeed.

## Beurling-filter check

The logical theorem about A=T+R is abstract and transfers to any setting with a nonnegative decomposition. Its applications in Passes 16 and 19 use ordinary factorials, Legendre's valuation formula, and the exact integer prime factorization; these are ordinary-lattice and multiplicative inputs and are not asserted to hold unchanged for general Beurling systems. No Beurling counterexample to the specific factorial argument is claimed. The filter therefore does not strengthen the arithmetic no-go beyond the proved scalar-information limitation.

## Audit of the repeated factorial obstruction

- Pass 16: the central-binomial valuation gives the coefficient-one band n<p<=2n. The total has a Stirling evaluation; other prime-power channels are positive and unbounded by this identity alone.
- Pass 19: the trinomial valuation gives the coefficient-one band 3n/2<p<=3n. Its total is also explicitly evaluated; the other channels remain positive and uncontrolled.
- At the displayed largest cutoffs, the selected shares were about 72.0% (Pass 16 at X=10^6) and 45.44% (Pass 19 at Y=1,000,002). These finite shares are diagnostics only and do not prove a limiting proportion or a lower bound.
- Common loss: positivity makes each target a subchannel of a known total. It supplies an upper bound, while the lower bound needs an independent estimate of the rest. Changing the factorial arity shifts the target interval but does not remove this logical dependency.

## Consolidated root-cause synthesis

1. Several exact positive identities evaluate a total or produce a PSD energy, but do not separately evaluate the prime component. Positivity alone yields one-sided upper bounds (Passes 1, 3–8, 15–16, 18–19).
2. Scale leakage and channel contamination are distinct: smoothing and norm-lattice kernels put mass on smaller divisor scales, while factorial and higher-order Mangoldt identities can have broad positive complementary channels (Passes 2, 4–6, 15, 18–19).
3. Prime specificity often returns only through a signed inverse, an uncontrolled complementary-channel subtraction, or an aggregate already equivalent in strength to the desired prime estimate (Passes 1, 7–8, 11–12).
4. A positive quadratic form can still hide its arithmetic diagonal behind signed cross terms; positivity of the scalar energy is not positivity of each coordinate contribution (Passes 9, 13, 17).
5. A fixed truncated divisor feature vector is rigorously blind on rough numbers (Pass 10), but this pointwise theorem does not rule out global counts, growing cutoffs with extra information, or other lattice statistics.
6. The Beurling counterexamples constrain arguments that transfer from multiplicativity plus regular generalized-integer counts; they do not constrain arguments whose decisive estimates genuinely use ordinary integer floor counts or factorial lattices.
7. All numerical checks are finite and at cutoffs at most about 10^6. They validate identities and profile channels, not limiting laws or error exponents.

## Selection-bias audit

The twenty passes are an adaptive search record, not a random or exhaustive sample of positive mechanisms. Divisor transforms, scalar lattice totals, sieves, and factorial valuations are overrepresented; several candidates are close relatives (for example the two norm-lattice totals, two truncated-divisor sieves, and the two factorial valuations). Failure-class frequencies are descriptive bookkeeping, not statistical evidence. The cutoffs and scale bands were selected for interpretable computation after candidate construction, and finite trends were not treated as asymptotics. The objective itself biases the search toward exact positive lattice formulas, so a future pass should explore a genuinely different observable rather than incrementing a parameter within a failed family.

## Positive scale-separation mechanisms still unexcluded

1. Coupled factorial identities or linear combinations with an exact positive projection that controls the complementary channels independently.
2. Endpoint-localized positive weights whose exact divisor transform has a proved quantitative tail bound.
3. Lattice projections, differences, or nonzero-lag additive Fourier correlations with independently evaluable off-diagonal terms.
4. Growing-cutoff sieve statistics supplemented by a proved rough-composite estimate or global statistic beyond the truncated divisor-feature vector.
5. Projected or locally weighted primitive and higher-dimensional norm-lattice transforms; the two scalar totals tested do not rule these out.
6. Ordinary-integer-lattice methods that use exact floor counts in an essential way and therefore do not transfer unchanged to Beurling systems.
7. Quantitative refinements of the Selberg symmetry method; the classical qualitative PNT benchmark does not rule out sharper extraction or error analysis.

## Filters and milestone

- **F-LATTICE:** The factorial cases pass through essential ordinary factorial/floor structure; no transfer to general Beurling integers is claimed.
- **F-MULT:** The target-channel decomposition uses unique factorization and prime valuations essentially.
- **F-GLOBAL:** Both identities include all prime powers up to their moving cutoff; fixed-prime truncation is not uniform.
- **Milestone:** **Below M0 for a new prime-counting result.** The consolidation proves the restricted information-level theorem, not a PNT or an improved bound for psi(X)-X. The classical Chebyshev upper bound is the only consequence for the tested factorial totals.

## Consolidation verdict

**PARTIAL.** The statement that a known scalar total plus nonnegativity alone yields only an upper bound for a selected component is proved. The stronger claim that factorial valuation mechanisms cannot obtain a lower bound using additional arithmetic structure is open. The next pass must not merely change factorial arity: it must supply an independent, rigorous estimate of the complement or a positive coupled observable that eliminates it, while preserving exact ordinary-lattice and multiplicative input.

## Pass 21 — Pascal-row carry aggregate

### 1. Mechanism

Instead of one binomial coefficient, aggregate the full Pascal row:

```text
P(n) = sum_{k=0}^n log binom(n,k)
     = (n+1)log(n!) - 2 sum_{k=0}^n log(k!)
     = (n+1)log Gamma(n+1) - 2log G(n+2),
```

where G(n+2)=product_{j=1}^n j! is the integer Barnes G value. Legendre's formula gives a positive prime-power expansion with a different kernel from the individual factorial ratios:

```text
P(n) = sum_{q=p^a<=n} W_n(q) log p,
W_n(q) = floor(n/q) * (q - 1 - (n mod q)) >= 0.
```

The exponent-one prime channel in the terminal half-scale is

```text
sum_{n/2 < p <= n} (2p-n-1) log p,
```

because W_n(p)=2p-n-1 there. This is a triangularly weighted prime band, not the unweighted Chebyshev band. The new feature is aggregation over all splits k+(n-k)=n, which converts each prime-power valuation into the number of carries across a whole Pascal row.

### 2. Derivation

For q=p^a and n=Aq+r, with 0<=r<q, the q-adic contribution to log binom(n,k) is

```text
floor(n/q)-floor(k/q)-floor((n-k)/q),
```

which equals 1 exactly when k mod q>r and otherwise 0. Among k=0,...,n there are A complete residue blocks modulo q, each with q-1-r such residues, followed by a partial block with residues 0,...,r. The number of carries is therefore A(q-1-r), proving W_n(q). Summing over prime powers proves the identity and its termwise positivity.

The Barnes G identity follows by summing the definition of each binomial coefficient over k. For the asymptotic, put H(t)=-t log t-(1-t)log(1-t), continuously extended at 0 and 1. The binomial probability inequalities give

```text
n H(k/n)-log(n+1) <= log binom(n,k) <= n H(k/n).
```

H has total variation 2log(2), and integral_0^1 H(t)dt=1/2. The bounded-variation Riemann-sum estimate yields

```text
n^2/2 - 2n log(2) - (n+1)log(n+1)
    <= P(n) <= n^2/2 + 2n log(2).
```

The kernel's first difference makes the relation to the old Chebyshev identity explicit. Directly from the quotient/remainder formula,

```text
W_{n+1}(q)-W_n(q) = -floor(n/q) + n * 1_{q divides n+1}.
```

Summing with weight Lambda(q) gives

```text
P(n+1)-P(n) = -sum_{q<=n} Lambda(q)floor(n/q) + n sum_{q|n+1}Lambda(q)
             = -log(n!) + n log(n+1),
```

using the exact Chebyshev divisor identity and sum_{q|m}Lambda(q)=log(m). Thus the Pascal-row aggregate is a positive discrete integral of the same exact factorial/divisor relation; taking one difference returns that relation, not a new independently evaluable prime statistic.

For every q, W_n(q)<=n. Hence the total contribution H_n from exponents a>=2 satisfies

```text
0 <= H_n <= n * sum_{a=2}^{floor(log_2 n)} theta(n^(1/a))
     <= n^(3/2)log(n) + n^(4/3)log(n)^2/log(2),
```

using only theta(y)<=y log(y) and separating a=2 from a>=3. Thus the exponent-one prime statistic

```text
F(n)=sum_{p<=n} W_n(p)log(p)
```

has the unconditional evaluation F(n)=n^2/2+O(n^(3/2)log(n)); the explicit inequalities above give the constants. This does not evaluate an unweighted prime interval.

### 3. Numeric check

An exact smallest-prime-factor sieve classified all prime powers through n=1,000,000. Channel weights and quotient/remainder data were exact integers; logarithms and Barnes G totals used 60-digit mpmath. The prime-power sum was independently compared with the Barnes G expression. The expression was also checked against direct summation of the Pascal row for every 1<=n<=30.

| n | Barnes G total | prime-power carry sum | residual | terminal-half prime channel | all other channels | target share |
|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 10 | 38.0171228000391835 | 38.0171228000391835 | -9.96e-60 | 5.83773044716594 | 32.17939235287324 | 0.1535552934 |
| 100 | 4775.80698496398304 | 4775.80698496398304 | 6.37e-58 | 1989.449642079659 | 2786.357342884324 | 0.4165682676 |
| 1,000 | 496624.376395971019 | 496624.376395971019 | -6.93e-55 | 242303.4622896127 | 254320.9141063583 | 0.4879008639 |
| 10,000 | 49954755.3379233693 | 49954755.3379233693 | 5.22e-53 | 25027819.79195569 | 24926935.54596768 | 0.5010097562 |
| 100,000 | 4999432455.53102545 | 4999432455.53102545 | 1.67e-50 | 2499738591.692913 | 2499693863.838112 | 0.5000044733 |
| 1,000,000 | 499993173301.07788 | 499993173301.07788 | 6.03e-48 | 249419994532.0891 | 250573178768.9888 | 0.4988468 |

At n=1,000,000, the total mass by prime-power scale q=p^a in q<=n/10, n/10<q<=n/2, and n/2<q<=n was 0.100055691008, 0.400805768282, and 0.499138540710 of P(n). The exponent-one prime channel was 0.998876493486 of the total, and higher prime powers were 0.00112350651351. These are finite proportions only. The full output and script are archived in `scripts/scale_separation_pass21_output.txt` and `scripts/scale_separation_pass21.py`.

### 4. Error analysis

Since W_n(p)<=n and the higher-power contribution has the explicit bound above,

```text
theta(n) >= n/2 - 2log(2) - ((n+1)/n)log(n+1)
            - sqrt(n)log(n) - n^(1/3)log(n)^2/log(2).
```

This is a Chebyshev-order lower bound, not theta(n)~n. For an upper bound, if 3n/4<p<=n then W_n(p)=2p-n-1>=n/3 for n>=6. Thus

```text
theta(n)-theta(floor(3n/4)) <= 3P(n)/n <= 3n/2 + 6log(2).
```

Iterating over n, floor(3n/4), floor((3/4)^2 n), ... gives theta(n)<=6n+O(log n); summing prime powers gives psi(n)<=6n+O(sqrt(n)log n). Together these are only a linear bracket and imply at best |psi(n)-n|=O(n). They do not reproduce the PNT, give a saving, or reach M0. The exact loss is that P(n) determines a weighted average across all quotient bands; the triangular terminal-band weight cannot be replaced by 1, and the positive scale contributions cannot be subtracted. The exact first difference of P(n) also collapses to the already-known Chebyshev factorial identity, rather than an independent prime estimate.

### 5. Filters

- **F-LATTICE: PASS.** The observable sums over the ordinary Pascal row and evaluates by ordinary factorials; its exact row structure is unavailable in a general Beurling integer system.
- **F-MULT: PASS.** The positive channel expansion is Legendre's unique-factorization valuation formula.
- **F-GLOBAL: PASS only for the complete moving system.** Every prime power q<=n enters; no fixed-prime truncation approximates this identity uniformly.

### 6. Milestone reached

**Below M0.** The candidate proves an asymptotic for a weighted prime-supported aggregate and an elementary two-sided Chebyshev-order bracket, but not psi(x)~x.

### 7. Why

Summing a whole row suppresses the higher-prime-power channels quantitatively, but the remaining prime channel is still an average over all multiplicative scales. At n=10^6 the last half-scale is about half the total, while the lower half contributes the other half. The new issue is not uncontrolled prime powers; it is the broad carry kernel and lack of an inversion theorem with a useful error bound. This is a specific limitation of this Pascal-row aggregate, not a no-go for positive kernels with a proven invertible scale transform.

### 8. Lesson

When a positive aggregate has a piecewise carry kernel, derive its Mellin or difference transform before treating the endpoint band as an estimator. Here one discrete difference reproduces the Chebyshev factorial identity. A next candidate must either prove a stable positive deconvolution of the scale kernel or change the observable so the selected scale is directly controlled without signed subtraction.

### 9. Failure class

**New class 15: Pascal-row carry energy evaluates a broad weighted prime average but does not determine an unweighted scale band.** The exact identity, positive kernel, explicit aggregate bound, and finite checks are established. No general obstruction for other Pascal-row weights or positive deconvolutions is claimed.

## Pass 22 — Pascal carry kernel and Wiener–Mellin Tauberian inversion

### 1. Mechanism

Pass 21's exponent-one prime channel is
\[
F(n)=\sum_{p\le n}\Big\lfloor\frac np\Big\rfloor
                  (p-1-(n\bmod p))\log p.
\]
For \(0<x\le1\), put
\[
\phi(x)=\Big\lfloor\frac1x\Big\rfloor x\left(1-\left\{\frac1x\right\}\right),
\qquad K(u)=\begin{cases}e^{-u}\phi(e^{-u}),&u\ge0,\\0,&u<0.\end{cases}
\]
Both are nonnegative and \(0\le\phi\le1\), so \(K\in L^1(\mathbb R)\), \(K\ge0\). Define the positive prime measure
\[
\nu=\sum_p\frac{\log p}{p}\,\delta_{\log p}.
\]
For integer \(n\ge2\), direct substitution gives
\[
(K*\nu)(\log n)=\frac1n\sum_{p\le n}\phi(p/n)\log p,
\]
and the exact weight identity \(W_n(p)=n\phi(p/n)-\lfloor n/p\rfloor\) yields
\[
\boxed{\frac{F(n)}{n^2}=(K*\nu)(\log n)-\frac1{n^2}\sum_{p\le n}\Big\lfloor\frac np\Big\rfloor\log p.}
\]
Pass 21 proved \(F(n)=n^2/2+O(n^{3/2}\log n)\); the final correction is at most \(n^{-1}\sum_{m\le n}\log m/m=O((\log n)^2/n)\). Hence \((K*\nu)(\log n)\to1/2\). The interpolation from integer \(n\) to real \(x\) is valid: between consecutive integers all \(\lfloor x/p\rfloor\) are constant, and the derivative of \(x^{-1}\sum_{p\le x}\phi(p/x)\log p\) is \(O((\log x)^2/x)\), using Pass 21's \(\theta(x)\le6x+O(\log x)\); at integer \(m\), its jump is at most \(m^{-1}\sum_{p\mid m}\log p\le\log(m)/m\). Thus
\[
(K*\nu)(u)\longrightarrow\frac12\quad(u\to\infty).
\]

### 2. Derivation and Tauberian step

On \(1/(a+1)<x\le1/a\), \(\phi(x)=a((a+1)x-1)\). Integrating each interval gives
\[
\int_{1/(a+1)}^{1/a}\phi(x)x^{s-1}\,dx
=\frac{a^{-s}(s-a)+a(a+1)^{-s}}{s(s+1)}.
\]
The series is absolutely integrable for \(\Re s>0\); summing the telescoping terms proves
\[
M(s):=\int_0^1\phi(x)x^{s-1}\,dx
=\frac{(s-1)\zeta(s)}{s(s+1)},\qquad \Re s>0,
\]
with the removable value \(M(1)=1/2\). Under the Fourier convention \(\widehat K(t)=\int K(u)e^{-itu}\,du\), substitution \(x=e^{-u}\) gives \(\widehat K(t)=M(1+it)\). Its nonvanishing for real \(t\ne0\) follows from \(\zeta(1+it)\ne0\); at zero it equals \(1/2\).

For completeness, the needed zero-free-line fact follows from Euler-product positivity, not from the PNT. For \(\sigma>1\),
\[
\log\bigl(|\zeta(\sigma)|^3|\zeta(\sigma+it)|^4|\zeta(\sigma+2it)|\bigr)
=\sum_{p,k\ge1}\frac{3+4\cos(kt\log p)+\cos(2kt\log p)}{k p^{k\sigma}}\ge0,
\]
since the numerator is \(2(1+\cos(kt\log p))^2\). If \(t\ne0\) and \(\zeta(1+it)=0\) with order \(r\ge1\), the product inside the logarithm is \(O((\sigma-1)^{4r-3})\to0\) as \(\sigma\downarrow1\), contradicting its lower bound 1. Thus \(\widehat K\) has no real zeros.

For \(|t|\ge2\), Euler summation gives the elementary bound \(\zeta(1+it)=O(\log(2+|t|))\). Since \(|(1+it)(2+it)|\asymp t^2\),
\[
|\widehat K(t)|=|M(1+it)|=O\!\left(\frac{\log(2+|t|)}{|t|}\right)\longrightarrow0.
\]
The multiplier therefore has no bounded inverse on the full Fourier line. This does not obstruct qualitative Wiener uniqueness, which needs only pointwise nonvanishing, but it means that theorem alone gives no uniform quantitative inverse norm.

The measure \(\nu\) is translation-bounded: for \(y\ge1\),
\[
\nu([\log y,\log y+1])\le\theta(ey)/y=O(1),
\]
by Pass 21's elementary Chebyshev upper bound. The positive-measure Wiener Tauberian lemma now applies: if \(K\in L^1\), \(\widehat K\) has no real zeros, \(K*\nu(u)\to c\), and \(\nu\) is translation-bounded, then every translate \(\nu(u+\cdot)\) converges vaguely to \((c/\int K)\,dt\). One proof takes vague limits of translates, smooths \(K\) by a Gaussian to handle its jumps, and uses Wiener uniqueness; positivity and translation-boundedness control the tails. Here \(c=1/2=\int K\), so the limit is Lebesgue measure.

Finally,
\[
\frac{\theta(e^u)}{e^u}=\int_{(-\infty,0]}e^r\,d\nu(u+r)\longrightarrow\int_{-\infty}^0e^r\,dr=1.
\]
The omitted tail is uniformly \(O(e^{-A})\) by translation-boundedness. Thus \(\theta(x)\sim x\), and the elementary higher-prime-power estimate \(0\le\psi(x)-\theta(x)\le\sqrt{x}\log x\) gives \(\psi(x)\sim x\).

### 3. Numerical check

`scale_pass22.py` uses an exact smallest-prime-factor sieve through \(10^6\), 60-digit logarithms, and checks the prime-channel/convolution identity at the listed cutoffs. The finite Mellin interval sums use \(A=20{,}000\); because \(0\le\phi\le1\), their omitted tail on \(\Re s=1\) is at most \(1/(A+1)\).

| n | \(F(n)/n^2\) | convolution | correction | algebraic residual |
|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 100 | 0.407005091129940673 | 0.437440361309078411 | 0.0304352701791 | 0 |
| 1,000 | 0.476259840758221165 | 0.481470277516683392 | 0.00521043675846 | \(1.56\times10^{-61}\) |
| 10,000 | 0.494040238022185351 | 0.494787507612106739 | 0.000747269589921 | \(5.45\times10^{-61}\) |
| 100,000 | 0.498067039689951095 | 0.498164665796787416 | 0.0000976261068363 | \(1.71\times10^{-60}\) |
| 1,000,000 | 0.499431427714164184 | 0.499443489408420402 | 0.0000120616942562 | \(4.20\times10^{-60}\) |

Mellin-transform partial-sum discrepancies at \(t=0,0.5,1,5,14,50\) were respectively \(2.50\times10^{-5},2.24\times10^{-5},1.77\times10^{-5},4.90\times10^{-6},1.78\times10^{-6},5.00\times10^{-7}\), each below the rigorous tail bound \(4.99975\times10^{-5}\). The Fourier-line multiplier magnitudes at those points were \(0.5,0.4440515,0.3461150,0.1421942,0.0236065,0.0104548\). These finite calculations check identities and truncation bounds; they are not the proof of nonvanishing or the Tauberian limit.

### 4. Error analysis and milestone

The argument gives the qualitative prime number theorem, hence **M0**. It gives no explicit rate for \(\theta(x)-x\) or \(\psi(x)-x\), so it reaches no M1–M6 error milestone. The exact bottleneck for a rate is quantitative inversion: Wiener nonvanishing supplies density/uniqueness but no uniform lower bound for \(|M(1+it)|\) as \(|t|\to\infty\), and the proof's compactness step has no effective modulus. A power saving would require zero-free information in a strip (or comparable bounds on the inverse multiplier), beyond the line nonvanishing used for M0.

The arithmetic inputs remain nonnegative: the carry weights, prime measure, and kernel are positive; the zero-free-line argument is a sum of nonnegative Euler-product terms. The final Wiener-Tauberian step is harmonic deconvolution, not a pointwise-positive identity; it uses Fourier analysis and cannot honestly be described as a wholly sign-free inversion. It uses no Möbius inversion or subtraction of unknown prime channels. This distinction is central to the mission's strict positivity requirement.

### 5. Filters

- **F-LATTICE — PASS.** The starting statistic is the exact Pascal-row factorial identity and uses the ordinary integer lattice; the floor/carry profile is not available unchanged in general Beurling systems.
- **F-MULT — PASS.** The prime-power expansion, Euler-product nonvanishing argument, and prime measure use unique factorization essentially.
- **F-GLOBAL — PASS only for the complete moving system.** The limit includes every prime through the moving cutoff; no fixed finite-prime approximation is used.
- **Strict sign-free inversion — PARTIAL.** All arithmetic measures and weights are nonnegative, but Wiener uniqueness is Fourier-analytic deconvolution rather than a positive-coefficient inverse.

### 6. Root cause, classification, and next constraint

This is a genuine qualitative inversion of Pass 21's weighted prime average, not a numerical extrapolation. It recovers M0 by adding a Wiener Tauberian step and the classical zero-free-line argument. It does not improve on the known PNT and does not meet the stronger demand for an entirely positive inverse. The new limitation is quantitative: the Mellin multiplier is nonzero on the boundary but becomes small at high frequency, while no effective inverse norm follows from its nonvanishing alone.

**Failure class 16:** positive Pascal carry convolution reaches M0 through Wiener–Mellin inversion but provides no quantified inverse; this is a limitation of the present kernel and argument, not a no-go theorem for all positive deconvolutions. **Next constraint:** seek an effective lower bound or frequency-localized inverse for the carry multiplier using only controlled arithmetic input; any claimed M1+ rate must state and prove the required multiplier bound and must not assume a zero-free strip equivalent to that rate.

### 7. Source and output files

Full computation: `scripts/scale_separation_pass22.py` and `scripts/scale_separation_pass22_output.txt`.

## Pass 23 — Finite-band inverse conditioning for the Pascal carry kernel

### 1. Mechanism

Continue Pass 22's carry kernel with Fourier multiplier
\[
M(t)=\widehat K(t)=\frac{it\,\zeta(1+it)}{(1+it)(2+it)},\qquad M(0)=\frac12.
\]
For a finite bandwidth \(T\), define the band inverse \(R_T\) on Fourier-side \(L^2\) by the multiplier \(1/M(t)\) on \(|t|\le T\), and zero outside. This is a precisely specified bounded operator: since \(M\) is continuous and nonzero on every compact real interval,
\[
\|R_T\|_{2\to2}=\left(\min_{|t|\le T}|M(t)|\right)^{-1}<\infty.
\]
It is the most direct frequency-localized inversion of the Pass 22 convolution, with no fitted parameters.

### 2. Exact conditioning obstruction

The boundary zero-free theorem from Pass 22 proves nonvanishing at each finite frequency, hence existence of each fixed-band inverse. But Euler summation gives \(\zeta(1+it)=O(\log(2+|t|))\), and therefore
\[
|M(t)|=O\!\left(\frac{\log(2+|t|)}{|t|}\right)\to0.
\]
Consequently \(\inf_{t\in\mathbb R}|M(t)|=0\), and \(\|R_T\|_{2\to2}\to\infty\) as \(T\to\infty\). This is a proved failure of uniform \(L^2\) stability for this direct inverse. A band-limited reconstruction error is amplified by at least the reciprocal minimum; a quantitative prime-error result additionally needs a proved rate for the Pass 22 convolution remainder and a bound on the discarded high-frequency target tail. Pass 22 supplies only a qualitative limit, so those estimates are not available here.

### 3. Numerical check

The script evaluates \(|M(t)|\) at 22 decimal digits on the uniform grid \(0\le t\le200\), spacing 0.1. The minima below are grid-sampled diagnostics, not certified continuous minima; they must not be read as rigorous lower bounds.

| T | sampled minimum | t at sampled minimum | reciprocal sampled value | |M(T)| |
|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 1 | 0.34611504846 | 1.0 | 2.8892127182 | 0.34611504846 |
| 2 | 0.21945473578 | 2.0 | 4.5567483264 | 0.21945473578 |
| 5 | 0.14219417253 | 5.0 | 7.0326370076 | 0.14219417253 |
| 10 | 0.13607459234 | 10.0 | 7.3489104969 | 0.13607459234 |
| 20 | 0.02285106125 | 14.1 | 43.761643675 | 0.04822412933 |
| 50 | 0.008985838897 | 49.7 | 111.2862151 | 0.01045480085 |
| 100 | 0.004426423627 | 94.8 | 225.9160181 | 0.01633845858 |
| 200 | 0.001824036477 | 185.4 | 548.2346502 | 0.01400508923 |

The sampled conditioning grows strongly, with local dips affecting the running minimum. This is finite evidence only; the analytic decay above, not the table, proves divergence of the full-band inverse norm.

### 4. Error analysis and milestone

The candidate confirms that finite-band inversion exists but loses uniform stability as the band expands. It yields no quantitative modulus for the translate limit and no M1+ estimate for \(\psi(x)-x\). Pass 22's qualitative PNT conclusion remains the project's M0 benchmark; this pass adds no stronger prime theorem.

### 5. Filters

- **F-LATTICE — PASS through inherited kernel.** The carry kernel is derived from the ordinary Pascal-row factorial identity.
- **F-MULT — PASS through inherited prime measure.** The convolution measure is supported on primes with weights \(\log p/p\), and its derivation uses unique factorization.
- **F-GLOBAL — PASS only in the full moving system.** The limit still uses all primes; a fixed finite-prime truncation is not justified.
- **Strict sign-free inversion — FAIL for this direct inverse.** The reciprocal Fourier multiplier changes phase and is not a positive-coefficient reconstruction; its operator norms also diverge with bandwidth.

### 6. Root cause, classification, and next constraint

This is a quantitative confirmation of Pass 22's known inverse-conditioning obstruction, not a new obstruction: the multiplier is nonzero but tends to zero. Compact-band inverses are well-defined, yet no cutoff schedule can be justified from a merely qualitative convolution limit. The next candidate must either prove an explicit convolution remainder and balance it against a certified lower bound for the multiplier on a growing band, or use a different positive observable with a stable inverse; sampled minima cannot serve as certified bounds.

**Failure class 16, second instance:** Pascal carry Mellin–Wiener inversion has no uniform direct \(L^2\) inverse. The full-line no-uniform-bound statement is proved from multiplier decay; numerical minima are sampled only.

### 7. Source and output files

Reproducible script and output: `scripts/scale_separation_pass23.py` and `scripts/scale_separation_pass23_output.txt`.

## Pass 24 — Positive postfilter obstruction for the carry kernel

### 1. Mechanism

Test whether the Pass 22 smoothed prime measure can be sharpened by any **positive linear postfilter**. Normalize the carry kernel as a probability density, (k=2K), since (int K=M(1)=1/2). Let (lambda) be any probability measure on the log scale. The proposed reconstruction is (k*lambda), with the intended target a point mass (or any translate of one). This is the broadest positive-measure convolution postfilter; its definition has no fitted arithmetic weights.

### 2. Derivation and exact obstruction

The Fourier transform of a probability measure satisfies (|widehatlambda(t)|le1). Therefore
\[
|\widehat{k*\lambda}(t)|=|\widehat k(t)|\,|\widehat\lambda(t)|\le|\widehat k(t)|.
\]
The exact Pass 22 multiplier gives
\[
|\widehat k(1)|=2\left|\frac{i\zeta(1+i)}{(1+i)(2+i)}\right|
=0.692230096929134\ldots<1.
\]
A point mass at any location has Fourier-transform modulus exactly 1 at every frequency. Hence at (t=1), every positive postfilter remains at least
\[
1-|\widehat k(1)|=0.307769903070866\ldots
\]
away in Fourier amplitude from a point mass. In particular, no sequence of positive probability postfilters (lambda_j) can make (k*lambda_j) converge weakly to a point mass: weak convergence would force convergence of the characteristic functions at (t=1), contradicting this uniform gap. This proves a no-go for positive-measure postfilter deblurring of this fixed kernel. It does not rule out signed filters, nonlinear arithmetic transformations, or changing the measured kernel itself.

### 3. Numerical check

An exact Eratosthenes sieve generated all primes through (10^6). For each cutoff, form the positive empirical prime measure
\[
\lambda_X=\frac{1}{\sum_{p\le X}(\log p)/p}\sum_{p\le X}\frac{\log p}{p}\,\delta_{\log p}.
\]
The table records the characteristic-function magnitudes at frequency 1. It checks finite positive filters only; the universal obstruction is the exact Fourier inequality above.

| X | prime count | |hat(lambda_X)(1)| | |hat(k*lambda_X)(1)| | gap to point mass |
|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 10 | 4 | 0.89292089225422 | 0.61810671579519 | 0.38189328420481 |
| 100 | 25 | 0.41788020258023 | 0.28926925313688 | 0.71073074686312 |
| 1,000 | 168 | 0.063871301924769 | 0.044213637522373 | 0.95578636247763 |
| 10,000 | 1,229 | 0.19710719759135 | 0.13644353449409 | 0.86355646550591 |
| 100,000 | 9,592 | 0.11426011665369 | 0.079094291626318 | 0.92090570837368 |
| 1,000,000 | 78,498 | 0.056546943639577 | 0.039143496276671 | 0.96085650372333 |

Logarithmic weights were accumulated at 40 decimal digits; the sieve and prime membership are exact. Each measured output obeys the proved (0.6922300969ldots) amplitude ceiling. The finite values do not prove a limiting law.

### 4. Error analysis and milestone

The positive postfilter cannot undo even one fixed amount of Fourier smoothing, so it cannot furnish the endpoint-localized prime extractor sought from this kernel. This route gives no new estimate for (psi(x)-x). The existing M0 result from Pass 22 remains the only PNT milestone; no M1+ rate is obtained.

### 5. Filters

- **F-LATTICE — PASS through inherited kernel.** (K) comes from the ordinary Pascal-row factorial identity and its exact integer floor counts.
- **F-MULT — PASS through inherited measure.** (lambda_X) is built from prime atoms with ((\log p)/p) weights, and the carry measure depends essentially on unique factorization.
- **F-GLOBAL — PASS only for the complete limit.** The finite (lambda_X) uses primes through (X); the limiting argument would require all primes, and no fixed-prime truncation is claimed.
- **Strict positive inversion — FAIL for this class.** The Fourier-modulus inequality proves positive convolution postfilters cannot converge to a point mass.

### 6. Root cause, classification, and next constraint

The obstruction is new relative to Pass 23's operator-norm calculation but belongs to its quantitative-inversion root-cause family: positivity itself prevents deblurring, independently of any inverse norm estimate. The next candidate must change the observable/kernel or exploit arithmetic information beyond post-convolution by a positive measure. It may use a signed inverse only if the sign mechanism is derived from a separate exact positive identity and every loss is quantified; it must not call a positive smoothing a deconvolution.

**Failure class 16, third instance:** the Pascal carry multiplier has no uniformly stable direct inverse, and positive-measure postfilters cannot approximate its point-mass inverse. The positive-postfilter no-go is proved for this fixed kernel; it is not a no-go for all prime-extraction mechanisms.

### 7. Source and output files

Reproducible script and output: `scripts/scale_separation_pass24.py` and `scripts/scale_separation_pass24_output.txt`.

## Pass 25 — Positive short-window difference of the log-integer lattice

### 1. Mechanism

For integers (Xge 4) and (1le H<X/2), set
\[
S(X,H)=\sum_{X-H<n\le X}\log n,
\qquad
w_{X,H}(q)=\left\lfloor\frac Xq\right\rfloor-
\left\lfloor\frac{X-H}{q}\right\rfloor.
\]
Applying the exact Chebyshev identity (\log n=\sum_{q\mid n}\Lambda(q)) only on this short interval gives
\[
\boxed{S(X,H)=\sum_{q\le X}\Lambda(q)w_{X,H}(q).}
\]
Here (q) ranges over prime powers, and every term is nonnegative. For (X-H>X/2), every prime power (q\in(X-H,X]) has (w_{X,H}(q)=1), so the target channel is exactly (\psi(X)-\psi(X-H)). For (X/2<q\le X-H), the weight is zero; the entire complementary contribution comes from (q\le X/2). This is a short-window differencing mechanism, distinct from smoothing the original integer sum by an exponential weight.

### 2. Derivation and explicit error

Interchange the finite sums:
\[
S(X,H)=\sum_{X-H<n\le X}\sum_{q\mid n}\Lambda(q)
=\sum_{q\le X}\Lambda(q)\#\{n:X-H<n\le X,\ q\mid n\}.
\]
The count is exactly (w_{X,H}(q)\). Thus
\[
S(X,H)=\psi(X)-\psi(X-H)+C(X,H),
\quad
C(X,H)=\sum_{q\le X/2}\Lambda(q)w_{X,H}(q)\ge0
\]
when (X-H>X/2). The target is therefore bounded above by (S(X,H)), but this identity gives no lower bound unless (C(X,H)) is independently controlled.

The known integer-side statistic has a short explicit asymptotic. For (H<X),
\[
H\log X-S(X,H)=-\sum_{j=0}^{H-1}\log(1-j/X),
\]
and (0\le-\log(1-u)\le u/(1-u)\) gives
\[
\boxed{0\le H\log X-S(X,H)
\le\frac{H(H-1)}{2(X-H+1)}.}
\]
Also (0\le w_{X,H}(q)\le\lceil H/q\rceil); this upper bound decays only reciprocally in the small-divisor range and does not control its aggregate below the target scale. For (H=\lfloor\sqrt X\rfloor), the proved consequence is only
\[
0\le\psi(X)-\psi(X-H)\le S(X,H)\le H\log X.
\]
No estimate for (C(X,H)) at (o(H\log X)) is derived, so summing or differencing these inequalities does not yield a bound for (\psi(X)-X\).

### 3. Numerical check

An exact least-prime-factor sieve classified all prime powers through (10^6); logarithms and sums used 55-digit Decimal arithmetic. The left side and prime-power expansion were accumulated independently. “Earlier-scale bands” partition the complement; their displayed proportions plus the target proportion sum to 1 (rounding aside).

| (X) | (H=\lfloor\sqrt X\rfloor) | integer log-window (S) | prime-power expansion | residual | target (\psi(X)-\psi(X-H)) | target/total | (q\le H) share | (H<q\le X/10) share | (X/10<q\le X/2) share |
|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 100 | 10 | 45.586735935354 | 45.586735935354 | (1.0\times10^{-53}) | 4.574710978503 | 0.10035180 | 0.38922440 | 0.00000000 | 0.51042380 |
| 1,000 | 31 | 213.670612727552 | 213.670612727552 | (0\) at shown precision | 34.456887544773 | 0.16126171 | 0.40380337 | 0.18968097 | 0.24525396 |
| 10,000 | 100 | 920.537387231034 | 920.537387231034 | (2.0\times10^{-52}) | 82.834951832118 | 0.08998543 | 0.42168742 | 0.25634096 | 0.23198619 |
| 100,000 | 316 | 3,637.586222688842 | 3,637.586222688842 | (-9.0\times10^{-51}) | 333.825875424745 | 0.09177126 | 0.44979500 | 0.29428624 | 0.16414749 |
| 1,000,000 | 1,000 | 13,815.010891464307 | 13,815.010891464307 | (-2.0\times10^{-50}) | 897.975128059742 | 0.06499996 | 0.46010077 | 0.34382573 | 0.13107354 |

The band (X/2<q\le X-H) contributes exactly zero. At (X=10^6), the target is about 6.50% of the known window total; prime powers (q\le H) contribute about 46.01%, and (H<q\le X/10) another 34.38%. The computation checks the finite identity and demonstrates the scale mix at these cutoffs; it proves no limiting proportion.

### 4. Error analysis and milestone

The window statistic is evaluated to (H\log X+O(H^2/X)) with the explicit inequality above, and it contains the desired short-interval Chebyshev mass with coefficient one. It gives an upper bound for that mass, not a two-sided estimate: the complement (C(X,H)) is positive and uncontrolled. For (H=\sqrt X), this is at most (O(\sqrt X\log X)) for a window of length (\sqrt X); it is not an error estimate for (\psi(X)-X). **Below M0 for a new prime-counting result.**

### 5. Filters

- **F-LATTICE — PASS.** The interval multiple count is the exact difference of ordinary floor counts; the integer-side estimate uses consecutive integers.
- **F-MULT — PASS.** The decomposition into (\Lambda(q)) over prime powers uses unique factorization and the von Mangoldt divisor identity essentially.
- **F-GLOBAL — PASS only for the complete moving system.** Every prime power (q\le X) is included. No uniform fixed-prime truncation is used or justified.

### 6. Why and failure class

The short interval localizes the *target channel* exactly, but not the total: lower prime powers contribute whenever one of their multiples falls in the window. Their weights satisfy only (w(q)\le\lceil H/q\rceil), and the measured earlier-scale mass dominates the target at all displayed cutoffs. The root cause is the positive complement in a known-total decomposition: positivity gives the desired channel's upper bound while its lower bound requires evaluating the complement. This repeats the lower-scale leakage of Failure Class 2 (Pass 2) in a window-difference kernel; it is a further instance, not a new universal obstruction. No claim is made that every positive interval or coupled-lattice method fails.

### 7. Lesson and next constraint

A positive short-window difference of the integer log-lattice sum can expose (\psi(X)-\psi(X-H)) exactly, but it cannot by itself remove contributions from small prime powers. The next candidate must control those multiples independently at (o(H\log X)), or combine windows through a proved positive identity that cancels the complement; a numerical target share or an upper bound is insufficient.

### 8. Reproducibility

Script: `scripts/scale_separation_pass25.py`. Raw output: `scripts/scale_separation_pass25_output.txt`.

## Pass 26 — Pairwise gcd energy on a short terminal interval

### 1. Mechanism

Let H = floor(sqrt(X)), I = {X-H+1, ..., X}, and define the nonnegative pair energy

    A(X,H) = sum_{a,b in I} log(gcd(a,b)).

Use the exact arithmetic identity log(m) = sum_{q|m} Lambda(q), where q ranges over prime powers. Put

    w(q) = floor(X/q) - floor((X-H)/q).

Reversing the finite sums gives the positive quadratic scale transform

    A(X,H) = sum_{q<=X} Lambda(q) w(q)^2.

This differs from Pass 25's linear short-window weight: the multiple count is squared because both entries of the pair must be divisible by q.

### 2. Derivation and error analysis

For every pair (a,b), log(gcd(a,b)) = sum_{q|a, q|b} Lambda(q). Summing over I x I, each q contributes Lambda(q) times the number of interval elements divisible by q, squared. That count is exactly w(q), proving the identity. Every term is nonnegative.

If X-H > X/2, then w(q)=1 for X-H < q <= X, and w(q)=0 for X/2 < q <= X-H. Thus the target psi(X)-psi(X-H) is an exact terminal subchannel. For q<=H, w(q)<=ceil(H/q)<=2H/q; for q>H, w(q)<=1. Therefore

    psi(X)-psi(X-H) <= A(X,H)
    A(X,H) <= 4H^2 sum_{q prime power} Lambda(q)/q^2 + psi(X)
           = 4H^2*(-zeta'(2)/zeta(2)) + psi(X).

The upper bound uses the exact convergent prime-power series; using only psi(X)<=X log(X) gives O(X log(X)) when H=floor(sqrt(X)). There is no independent asymptotic or closed evaluation here for A(X,H), so positivity does not give a lower bound for the target or an estimate for psi(X)-X.

### 3. Numerical check

The script uses an exact smallest-prime-factor sieve through 10^6, exact prime-power classification, and 60-digit Decimal logarithms. The transformed sum was independently checked by direct pairwise gcd summation at X=100 and 1,000; residuals were 6.0e-58 and 6.4e-56. The identity accumulation residuals in the table are rounding-level, at most 6.0e-54.

| X | H | A(X,H) | psi window | target/A | q<=H share | H<q<=X/10 | X/10<q<=X/2 | X/2<q<=X-H | terminal share |
|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 100 | 10 | 77.310932484900 | 4.574710978503 | 0.0591728858 | 0.6398534446 | 0.0000000000 | 0.3009736697 | 0 | 0.0591728858 |
| 1,000 | 31 | 646.893795918902 | 34.456887544773 | 0.0532651384 | 0.8030747847 | 0.0626520911 | 0.0810079859 | 0 | 0.0532651384 |
| 10,000 | 100 | 6,067.961835645697 | 82.834951832118 | 0.0136511986 | 0.9122673527 | 0.0388880888 | 0.0351933599 | 0 | 0.0136511986 |
| 100,000 | 316 | 58,537.219399180080 | 333.825875424745 | 0.0057027969 | 0.9658094775 | 0.0182873666 | 0.0102003590 | 0 | 0.0057027969 |
| 1,000,000 | 1,000 | 576,802.508090713117 | 897.975128059742 | 0.0015568156 | 0.9870688604 | 0.0082349785 | 0.0031393456 | 0 | 0.0015568156 |

The listed shares partition the energy. At X=10^6, q<=H contributes 98.7069% of the total, while the desired terminal window contributes 0.1557%. The log-integer window sum at that cutoff is 13,815.010891464307, so the pair energy is 41.7519 times that scalar statistic; this candidate supplies no independent evaluation for the pair energy. These are finite measurements, not limiting claims.

### 4. Filters

- F-LATTICE — PASS. The exact count w(q) uses consecutive ordinary integers and a floor difference.
- F-MULT — PASS. The prime-power channels use log(gcd(a,b)) = sum_{q|a,b} Lambda(q), relying on unique factorization.
- F-GLOBAL — PASS only for the complete moving sum. Every prime power q<=X is included; no fixed finite-prime truncation or uniform limit is proved.

### 5. Milestone, root cause, and lesson

Below M0. The target prime-power interval is explicitly present, but A(X,H) has no independent evaluation and its positive complement is uncontrolled. Squaring the interval multiple count sharply magnifies small divisors: at X=10^6, q<=H carries 98.71% of the observed energy. This is another instance of Failure Class 2, not a general no-go for pairwise gcd or positive quadratic methods.

Constraint for the next pass: a quadratic interval statistic must come with an independently evaluated baseline or a proved positive projection that removes q<=H; the exact target subchannel and finite proportions alone do not establish prime distribution.

### 6. Reproducibility

Script: scripts/scale_separation_pass26.py. Raw output: scripts/scale_separation_pass26_output.txt.

## Pass 27 — Adjacent-block contrast in the positive log-gcd Gram form

### 1. MECHANISM

For integer X >= 100, let h=floor(sqrt(X)/2). Use two adjacent blocks
J1={X-2h+1,...,X-h} and J2={X-h+1,...,X}; give entries of J1 coefficient +1 and entries of J2 coefficient -1. For the positive-semidefinite kernel G(a,b)=log(gcd(a,b)), define

    E(X)=sum_{a,b in J1 union J2} eps(a) eps(b) log(gcd(a,b)).

This is a quadratic form of a signed contrast vector, but its exact prime-power expansion has only nonnegative terms. For each prime power q, let c(q) be the number of multiples of q in J1 minus the number in J2. Then

    E(X)=sum_{q=p^k<=X} Lambda(q)c(q)^2 >= 0.

The terminal channel q>X-2h has c(q)^2=1, so it is exactly psi(X)-psi(X-2h). Equal-length adjacent intervals imply |c(q)|<=1 for every q; if q divides h, then c(q)=0. This suppresses a substantial part of the low-divisor energy seen in Pass 26.

### 2. DERIVATION AND ERROR ANALYSIS

For any positive integers a,b, log(gcd(a,b))=sum_{q|a, q|b} Lambda(q). Insert this finite identity into E and reverse the finite sums. The coefficient of Lambda(q) is

    (sum_{a in J1, q|a}1 - sum_{a in J2, q|a}1)^2 = c(q)^2,

which proves the nonnegative expansion exactly. Each interval contains either floor(h/q) or ceil(h/q) multiples of q, hence |c(q)|<=1. Translation by h preserves q-divisibility when q|h, giving c(q)=0 in that case.

Because q>X-2h>X/2, such a prime power has no second multiple <=X. Its own location belongs to exactly one of J1,J2, so c(q)^2=1 and the terminal sum is exactly psi(X)-psi(X-2h). For the prime-only terminal window, the square-power contribution is at most (log X)/2: the interval of possible square roots has length 2h/(sqrt(X)+sqrt(X-2h))<1, so it contains at most one integer. The total contribution from exponents k>=3 is at most X^(1/3) log(X) log_2(X), by bounding the number of candidate powers for each exponent by X^(1/k) and each weight by log(X). Thus the terminal prime-power channel differs from the prime channel by O(X^(1/3)log^2 X), explicitly below sqrt(X) in scale.

There is no independent evaluation of E(X). The universal bound |c(q)|<=1 gives only E(X)<=psi(X), which does not bound psi(X)-X nontrivially. Consequently no M0-M6 prime-counting error follows. The exact loss is the unevaluated middle band h<q<=X-2h; at X=10^6 it accounts for 85.54% of E.

### 3. NUMERICAL CHECK

An exact smallest-prime-factor sieve generated the prime-power channels through X=10^6. Logarithms and sums used 55-digit Decimal arithmetic. The expansion was checked against direct pairwise gcd summation at X=100 and X=1,000; residuals are 2.0e-53 and 2.0e-52 respectively.

| X | h | E(X) | q<=h contribution | h<q<=X-2h contribution | terminal psi window | target/E | low share | middle share | terminal share |
|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 100 | 5 | 32.119932151679 | 2.484906649788 | 25.060314523388 | 4.574710978503 | 0.1424259228 | 0.0773633841 | 0.7802106930 | 0.1424259228 |
| 1,000 | 15 | 139.222565825472 | 0.693147180560 | 104.072531100139 | 34.456887544773 | 0.2474949901 | 0.0049786985 | 0.7475263114 | 0.2474949901 |
| 10,000 | 50 | 584.696965032299 | 20.724348498657 | 481.137664701525 | 82.834951832118 | 0.1416715953 | 0.0354445974 | 0.8228838073 | 0.1416715953 |
| 100,000 | 158 | 2,174.364511915113 | 82.423331739030 | 1,758.115304751337 | 333.825875424745 | 0.1535280187 | 0.0379068603 | 0.8085651210 | 0.1535280187 |
| 1,000,000 | 500 | 7,973.381305237383 | 255.148183974461 | 6,820.257993203180 | 897.975128059742 | 0.1126216211 | 0.0319999978 | 0.8553783812 | 0.1126216211 |

At X=10^6, q<=h contributes 3.20%, compared with 98.71% for Pass 26's unsigned pair energy. The middle band remains dominant at 85.54%. The finite ratios are observations, not asymptotic claims.

### 4. FILTERS

- F-LATTICE — PASS: c(q) is an exact difference of ordinary consecutive-interval floor counts.
- F-MULT — PASS: the gcd logarithm identity and prime-power von Mangoldt coefficients use unique factorization essentially.
- F-GLOBAL — PASS for each moving cutoff: every prime power q<=X is included; no fixed finite-prime truncation is used.
- Positive expansion — PASS: after expansion every summand Lambda(q)c(q)^2 is nonnegative, though the test vector itself has both signs.

### 5. MILESTONE REACHED

Below M0. The exact positive quadratic form exposes a terminal prime-power window with a sub-sqrt upper bound for its higher-power contamination, but the full energy has no independent evaluation and gives no estimate for psi(X)-X.

### 6. FAILURE CLASS, ROOT CAUSE, AND LESSON

New Failure Class 17: adjacent-block differencing cancels some low divisors but leaves an unevaluated middle-scale band. This is distinct from Pass 26's squared unsigned interval count, which magnified low-q mass; here q|h vanishes exactly and the observed low-q share drops to 3.20%, yet the middle band dominates. The finite data show a mechanism-level obstruction, not a universal no-go theorem.

Constraint for the next pass: preserve the exact terminal coefficient while either annihilating a growing low-and-middle divisor range through a proved positive Gram construction, or independently evaluate the residual energy. Quantify the residual floor-difference support before running a larger computation; do not infer localization from suppression of q<=h alone.

### 7. REPRODUCIBILITY

Script: scripts/scale_separation_pass27.py. Raw output: scripts/scale_separation_pass27_output.txt.

## Pass 28 — LCM-aligned adjacent-block contrast

### 1. MECHANISM

For integer X >= 100, put B=floor(sqrt(X)/2). Let R be the largest integer for which L=lcm(1,...,R)<=B, then set h=floor(B/L)*L. Form adjacent blocks J1={X-2h+1,...,X-h}, J2={X-h+1,...,X}, and the signed contrast vector +1 on J1, -1 on J2. For q a prime power, let c(q) be the difference between the counts of q-multiples in J1 and J2. The log-gcd Gram quadratic form is

    E_LCM(X)=sum_{a,b} eps(a)eps(b)log(gcd(a,b))
            =sum_{q=p^k<=X} Lambda(q)c(q)^2 >= 0.

The LCM-aligned step makes every prime power q<=R divide h, hence c(q)=0 exactly. The terminal band q>X-2h still has c(q)^2=1 and equals psi(X)-psi(X-2h).

### 2. DERIVATION AND ERROR ANALYSIS

The identity log(gcd(a,b))=sum_{q|a,q|b}Lambda(q), summed against eps(a)eps(b), gives E_LCM=sum_q Lambda(q)(sum_{a in J1,q|a}1-sum_{a in J2,q|a}1)^2. This proves the nonnegative expansion. Translating an interval by h preserves divisibility by q when q|h, so that channel is zero. As in Pass 27, adjacent equal-length blocks give |c(q)|<=1.

For q>X-2h>X/2, exactly one multiple q occurs in the two blocks, so the terminal coefficient is one. The terminal square-power contribution is at most (log X)/2: the possible square-root interval has length 2h/(sqrt(X)+sqrt(X-2h))<1. Exponents k>=3 contribute at most X^(1/3)log(X)log_2(X). Hence the terminal prime-power mass differs from the prime mass by O(X^(1/3)log^2 X), which is o(sqrt(X)).

The complete E_LCM has no independent lattice-side evaluation. The bound |c(q)|<=1 gives only E_LCM<=psi(X), which does not bound psi(X)-X nontrivially. The exact loss is the uncancelled band h<q<=X-2h. In the X=10^6 computation this middle band is 86.11% of the energy; the terminal target is 11.04%.

### 3. NUMERICAL CHECK

An exact smallest-prime-factor sieve generated the prime powers through X=10^6; logarithms and channel sums used 55-digit Decimal arithmetic. The transformed identity was checked against direct pairwise gcd summation at X=100 and 1,000, with residuals 0.0e-50 and 0.0e-49 at the displayed precision.

| X | R | lcm(1,...,R) | h | E_LCM | q<=R | R<q<=h | h<q<=X-2h | terminal psi window | terminal/E |
|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 100 | 2 | 2 | 4 | 26.979104366224 | 0 | 1.098612288668 | 21.305781099053 | 4.574710978503 | 0.1695649684 |
| 1,000 | 4 | 12 | 12 | 125.533509948739 | 0 | 5.347107530717 | 92.607841341540 | 27.578561076482 | 0.2196908307 |
| 10,000 | 4 | 12 | 48 | 549.281089300929 | 0 | 17.546294668309 | 458.100233841625 | 73.634560790995 | 0.1340562459 |
| 100,000 | 6 | 60 | 120 | 1,737.557471476586 | 0 | 51.332697391961 | 1,432.969257973515 | 253.255516111109 | 0.1457537493 |
| 1,000,000 | 7 | 420 | 420 | 6,756.582206135886 | 0 | 191.992456128857 | 5,818.575008319219 | 746.014741687811 | 0.1104130341 |

At X=10^6, the zeroed q<=R band is exact, but the middle band remains dominant at 86.12%. The R<q<=h band is 2.84%, and the terminal share is 11.04%. These finite proportions are diagnostic only. Relative to Pass 27, the terminal ratio is essentially unchanged (11.04% versus 11.26%); the LCM alignment has not materially improved selectivity.

### 4. FILTERS

- F-LATTICE — PASS: all multiple counts are exact floor differences over ordinary integer intervals.
- F-MULT — PASS: the log-gcd expansion into von Mangoldt prime-power channels uses unique factorization.
- F-GLOBAL — PASS for each moving cutoff: the full prime-power range q<=X is included.
- Positive expansion — PASS: every expanded term Lambda(q)c(q)^2 is nonnegative; the test vector has signs.

### 5. MILESTONE REACHED

Below M0. The construction annihilates every q<=R exactly and isolates a terminal prime-power window with sub-sqrt higher-power contamination, but it neither evaluates the full energy independently nor yields a bound for psi(X)-X.

### 6. FAILURE CLASS, ROOT CAUSE, AND LESSON

Failure Class 17, second instance. LCM alignment makes the cancellation condition systematic for q<=R, but R grows only logarithmically with the available step and the uncancelled intermediate divisors still dominate. This is a limitation of this particular two-block contrast; it is not a no-go theorem for higher-order filters or coupled identities.

Constraint for the next pass: do not spend complexity only on making the step divisible by more tiny prime powers. A new candidate must alter the middle-band coefficients themselves and provide either a positive bound or independent lattice evaluation for the residual.

### 7. REPRODUCIBILITY

Script: scripts/scale_separation_pass28.py. Raw output: scripts/scale_separation_pass28_output.txt.

## Pass 29 — Period-annihilating polynomial block contrast

### 1. MECHANISM

Set B=floor(sqrt(X)/2). Choose the largest R with D+1<=B, where D=R(R+1)/2. Define P_R(z)=product_{m=1}^R(1-z^m)=sum_{j=0}^D a_j z^j, and h=floor(B/(D+1)); thus W=(D+1)h<=B. Partition the terminal interval (X-W,X] into D+1 adjacent blocks J_j of length h, ordered from low to high, and assign coefficient a_j to J_j. Define the log-gcd Gram energy E_R=sum_{a,b in union J_j} eps(a)eps(b)log(gcd(a,b)). For each prime power q, c_q=sum_j a_j times the number of q-multiples in J_j. Then E_R=sum_{q=p^k<=X}Lambda(q)c_q^2>=0.

If q<=R is a prime power, its block-count sequence has period d=q/gcd(q,h)<=q<=R. The polynomial P_R contains the factor 1-z^d, so it annihilates every such periodic channel: c_q=0. For q in the terminal band (X-W,X], q>X/2 and only q itself occurs in the support; if it lies in J_j then c_q=a_j. The terminal channel is therefore the weighted localized prime-power sum with weight a_j^2 on block J_j.

### 2. DERIVATION AND ERROR ANALYSIS

For an integer interval J_j=[u_j,u_j+h-1], the number of multiples of q is A_j(q)=floor((u_j+h-1)/q)-floor((u_j-1)/q). Translating by h changes the block index; after d=q/gcd(q,h) steps the translation is dh, divisible by q. Hence A_j(q) is d-periodic. Every d-periodic sequence is a linear combination of modes zeta^j with zeta^d=1. Since d<=R, P_R(zeta)=0 for each such root, and sum_j a_j A_j(q)=0. This proves exact annihilation for every prime power q<=R.

For any q the channel identity follows by expanding log(gcd(a,b))=sum_{q|a,q|b}Lambda(q) and reversing finite sums. This makes E_R a positive semidefinite quadratic form despite the signed block vector. Equal-length adjacent block counts differ by at most one per step, but the polynomial combination can have large coefficients; no general small bound on c_q is claimed outside the annihilated period range.

The terminal weighted prime-power component is

    T_R(X)=sum_{j=0}^D a_j^2 * sum_{X-W+jh < q <= X-W+(j+1)h, q=p^k} Lambda(q).

The terminal higher-power contamination has the explicit coarse bound

    0 <= T_R,higher(X) <= A_R^2 * (0.5 log X + X^(1/3)log(X)log_2(X)),
    A_R=max_j |a_j|.

For squares, the interval of possible roots has length W/(sqrt(X)+sqrt(X-W))<1, so at most one integer square root occurs. For exponents k>=3, count at most X^(1/k) candidates per k and bound each weight by log X, giving the displayed estimate. The factor A_R^2 makes this bound too large to show negligible contamination uniformly for the chosen R(X); the fact that the tested terminal higher-power sums are zero is finite evidence only.

The total energy E_R has no independent ordinary-lattice evaluation. It gives no asymptotic for T_R or psi(X)-X. The exact loss is twofold: the surviving intermediate channels acquire large polynomial coefficients, and the positive energy itself is not independently evaluated. This candidate reaches below M0.

### 3. NUMERICAL CHECK

An exact smallest-prime-factor sieve generated prime powers through X=10^6; all block counts and polynomial coefficients were exact integers, with 55-digit Decimal logarithmic sums. Direct pairwise gcd evaluation checked the transform at X=100 and 1,000; residuals were 1.0e-53 and 2.0e-53 at displayed precision. Terminal higher-power contributions were zero at all listed cutoffs, which is not asserted as a theorem for all X.

| X | R | degree D | h | support width W | max abs(a_j) | sum a_j^2 | E_R | q<=R | R<q<=sqrt(X) | sqrt(X)<q<=X-W | terminal prime | terminal higher powers | terminal/E_R |

The coefficient L2 energy is 96,712 and max coefficient is 41. These data show that the high-order annihilator transfers mass into large residual channels rather than isolating the terminal prime window.

### 4. FILTERS

- F-LATTICE — PASS: every block count is an exact ordinary-integer floor difference.
- F-MULT — PASS: the log-gcd expansion into von Mangoldt prime powers uses unique factorization.
- F-GLOBAL — PASS at each moving cutoff: all q<=X are included; no fixed-prime truncation is used.
- Positive expansion — PASS: every expanded term Lambda(q)c_q^2 is nonnegative, although the vector across blocks is signed.

### 5. MILESTONE REACHED

Below M0. This is an exact positive form that annihilates all prime powers q<=R and reveals a weighted terminal window. It does not independently evaluate the total, control the weighted higher-power tail uniformly, or yield an estimate for psi(X)-X.

### 6. FAILURE CLASS, ROOT CAUSE, AND LESSON

New Failure Class 18: period-annihilating block polynomials cancel low-period channels but their coefficients amplify the surviving middle scales. This is a distinct obstruction from Pass 28's LCM step: it cancels every q<=R through a periodicity polynomial, with R=31 at X=10^6, but the middle bands still dominate. No universal no-go theorem is claimed.

Constraint for the next pass: keep the exact periodic-channel annihilation while controlling the coefficient norm and the residual c_q values on the middle band. A larger annihilating degree alone is not progress if its coefficient growth expands the residual energy; derive a norm or orthogonality bound before choosing the degree.

### 7. REPRODUCIBILITY

Script: scripts/scale_separation_pass29.py. Raw output: scripts/scale_separation_pass29_output.txt.

## Pass 30 — Consolidation of the two tied highest-frequency classes

The failure-class table has a tie: Class 2 (positive smoothing/window leakage) and Class 16 (Pascal carry inversion) each have three recorded candidates. This consolidation attempts restricted general statements for both; it is not a new candidate mechanism.

### Class 2: fixed positive dilate weights have a reciprocal-scale kernel

Let w:[0,1] -> [0,infinity) be Lipschitz, with A = integral from 0 to 1 of w(t) dt > 0, Lipschitz constant L, and M = sup |w|. Define

    S_w(X) = sum_{n<=X} w(n/X) log(n)
    K_X(q) = sum_{1<=j<=floor(X/q)} w(jq/X).

The exact Mangoldt expansion is S_w(X) = sum_{q=p^k<=X} Lambda(q) K_X(q). Put h=q/X and m=floor(1/h). On each of the m intervals of length h, the right-endpoint Riemann-sum error is at most L h^2/2, so the total error on [0,mh] is at most Lmh^2/2 <= Lh/2. The omitted interval [mh,1] contributes at most Mh. Thus

    |K_X(q) - A X/q| <= L/2 + M, for every 1<=q<=X.

Consequently, when q/X tends to zero, K_X(q) is asymptotic to A X/q. A fixed nonnegative dilate weight with positive integral cannot produce an endpoint-flat divisor kernel: its small-scale kernel grows reciprocally. This is a no-go only for this fixed-weight transform family. It does not prove that actual prime-power mass at small q dominates for all X, and it does not cover X-dependent shrinking windows such as Pass 25; those retain their exact floor-difference kernel.

Numerical check: use w(t)=1-t, so A=1/2 and L=M=1, giving |K_X(q)-X/(2q)|<=3/2. An exact SPF sieve identified every prime power through X=10^6; direct sums used 55-digit Decimal arithmetic. At X=10^6, the direct lattice sum and prime-power transform differed by 3.17e-46. The channel shares in q-bands q<=X/10, X/10<q<=X/2, and q>X/2 were 87.995063%, 9.975732%, and 2.029205%. These finite data are diagnostics, not asymptotic prime-mass claims. Full table and reproducible code: scripts/scale_separation_pass30_output.txt and scripts/scale_separation_pass30.py.

### Class 16: positive postfilters cannot undo a strict Fourier contraction

Let k be a probability measure on the additive log-scale, and suppose that at some frequency t0 its Fourier transform satisfies |khat(t0)|=rho<1. For every probability measure lambda,

    |(k*lambda)hat(t0)| = |khat(t0) lambdahat(t0)| <= rho.

A point mass at any location has Fourier-transform modulus 1 at t0. Therefore no sequence of positive probability postfilters k*lambda_j can converge weakly to a point mass. This generalizes the fixed-kernel positive-postfilter obstruction from Pass 24. Separately, Pass 23 proves the direct inverse for the Pascal carry multiplier is unbounded because that multiplier tends to zero. This consolidation supplies no rate for any different inverse.

### Error analysis, filters, and milestone

Neither result estimates the prime complement or yields a new bound for psi(X)-X; this pass is below M0 as a new result. F-LATTICE and F-MULT are essential to the first application (ordinary multiple counts and log(n)=sum_{q|n} Lambda(q)); F-GLOBAL requires all prime powers at each moving cutoff. The Fourier postfilter lemma is abstract harmonic analysis and transfers beyond the integer lattice, so it excludes only that specified positive-convolution inversion family; it is not itself a prime-extraction mechanism. The Beurling filter limits claims about the arithmetic application, not the abstract convolution inequality.

### WHY synthesis

Across Classes 2 and 16, positivity preserves one-sided order but does not supply an inverse: fixed dilate weights create a 1/q scale kernel, and positive convolution cannot increase Fourier amplitude to that of a point mass. These are proved restrictions of two construction families, not a theorem that all positive methods fail. The recurring gap is an independently evaluated complement or a stable, arithmetically justified reconstruction.

### Selection-bias audit

The thirty-pass ledger is adaptive, not an exhaustive or random sample. It overrepresents exact divisor transforms, lattice totals, and positive quadratic variants; later candidates were deliberately shaped by earlier failures. The highest-frequency tie is a bookkeeping count, not evidence that these classes dominate mathematics. Cutoffs stop at 10^6, selected weights were not pre-registered, and finite shares cannot establish limiting behavior. Class 16 contains several related passes from one Pascal carry kernel, so its count is not independent replication.

### Positive mechanisms still unexcluded

1. X-dependent weights with a proved uniform kernel bound, including shrinking-window combinations not covered by the fixed-w theorem.
2. Coupled exact lattice identities that determine the complementary prime-power contribution independently.
3. Signed or nonlinear inversions whose signs and stability follow from separate arithmetic identities rather than being fitted or assumed.
4. Nonzero-lag additive correlations or projected lattice energies with independently evaluable cross terms.
5. Selberg-type refinements that quantify the qualitative bootstrap without assuming the target error estimate.

### Consolidation verdict

PARTIAL. The fixed-Lipschitz-weight kernel asymptotic and positive-postfilter Fourier obstruction are proved with their stated hypotheses. No universal M0 ceiling, new PNT milestone, or search-saturation claim is justified.

| Keep multiplicity-sensitive Omega weights distinct from omega weights: Omega=1 isolates primes, but the aggregate still needs an independent evaluation before positive leakage bounds imply a prime asymptotic. | 34 | Exact channel split and computation through 10^6; M1 remains inherited from uniform Selberg-Delange |
