## Pass 31 — LCM covolume increments as a positive prime-power measure

### 1. MECHANISM

For (n\ge1), let (L_n=\operatorname{lcm}(1,2,\ldots,n)), equivalently the index of the intersection sublattice \(\bigcap_{m\le n}m\mathbb Z=L_n\mathbb Z\) in \(\mathbb Z\). Define the nonnegative increment

\[
\delta_n=\log L_n-\log L_{n-1}=\log(L_n/L_{n-1}),\qquad L_0:=1.
\]
At a new prime-power threshold (n=p^a), the lattice covolume gains one factor (p); otherwise it does not change. Thus \(\delta_n=\Lambda(n)\) exactly and \(\log L_X=\sum_{n\le X}\delta_n=\psi(X)\). Unlike a divisor average, this is a local jump measure of nested integer sublattices. The central question is whether this geometric encoding gives an independently evaluable total or merely renames the target.

### 2. DERIVATION

For every prime (p),
\[
v_p(L_n)=\max\{a:p^a\le n\}=\lfloor\log_p n\rfloor.
\]
Hence
\[
\delta_n=\sum_p\bigl(v_p(L_n)-v_p(L_{n-1})\bigr)\log p.
\]
Each difference is either zero or one. It equals one precisely when (n=p^a); at most one prime-power base can occur for any (n\ge2). Therefore \(\delta_n=\log p\) for (n=p^a), and zero otherwise, which is exactly \(\Lambda(n)\). Summing telescopically proves \(\log L_X=\psi(X)\).

For \(\Re s>1\), absolute convergence gives the exact Dirichlet series
\[
\sum_{n\ge2}\frac{\delta_n}{n^s}=\sum_p\sum_{a\ge1}\frac{\log p}{p^{as}}=-\frac{\zeta'(s)}{\zeta(s)}.
\]
The equality exhibits the Euler product through lattice-covolume jumps. It provides no independent analytic continuation or estimate: the partial sum of the coefficients is already \(\log L_X=\psi(X)\).

The only bound supplied by this construction without further prime-distribution input is the elementary factorial comparison (L_X\mid X!\), so (0\le\psi(X)\le\log(X!)\le X\log X-X+1+\log X) for (X\ge2) (using the standard integral/Stirling upper estimate). Consequently it yields at best \(|\psi(X)-X|\le X\log X+X\), below M0. Any estimate \(\log L_X=X+E(X)\) is exactly the desired estimate \(\psi(X)=X+E(X)\), so no error improvement is obtained by this identity alone.

### 3. NUMERIC CHECK

The reproducible computation uses an exact smallest-prime-factor sieve through \(10^6\) and 60-digit Decimal logarithms. It enumerates prime-power events independently from a factorization-based test: for all 99,999 integers \(2\le n\le100{,}000\), event weights agree exactly with \(\Lambda(n)\), with zero mismatches. It also constructs the integer \(\operatorname{lcm}(1,\ldots,X)\) directly for four smaller cutoffs and compares its logarithm with the prime-power sum:

| \(X\) | decimal digits in lcm | \(\log(\operatorname{lcm})-\sum_{p^a\le X}\log p\) |
|---:|---:|---:|
| 10 | 4 | \(1\times10^{-59}\) |
| 100 | 41 | 0 at displayed precision |
| 1,000 | 433 | \(6\times10^{-57}\) |
| 5,000 | 2,171 | \(-1.2\times10^{-55}\) |

The main cutoff table through \(10^6\) evaluates the prime-power side, not a materialized enormous lcm. Full output and reproducible code are in `scripts/scale_separation_pass31_output.txt` and `scripts/scale_separation_pass31.py`.

| \(X\) | \(\pi(X)\) | atom count | \(\log L_X=\psi(X)\) | \(\psi(X)-X\) | normalized residual \(\frac{\psi(X)-X}{\sqrt X}\) |
|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 10 | 4 | 7 | 7.832014180505469 | −2.167985819495 | −0.6855773125 |
| 100 | 25 | 35 | 94.045311229357392 | −5.954688770643 | −0.5954688771 |
| 1,000 | 168 | 193 | 996.680912247175240 | −3.319087752825 | −0.1049587705 |
| 10,000 | 1,229 | 1,280 | 10,013.396693263114784 | 13.396693263115 | 0.1339669326 |
| 100,000 | 9,592 | 9,700 | 100,051.564025657954251 | 51.564025657954 | 0.1630597664 |
| 1,000,000 | 78,498 | 78,734 | 999,586.597495632922033 | −413.402504367078 | −0.4134025044 |

The data verify finite arithmetic identities only. The normalized residual does not show a monotone trend and is not evidence for a square-root error theorem.

### 4. ERROR ANALYSIS

The exact transformation recovers \(\psi(X)\), but the lattice covolume has no evaluation independent of the prime-power jump list. Thus it does not convert the target into a known total plus a controlled remainder. The only explicit bound from the construction is the factorial upper bound above; no Tauberian step gives a better estimate. The bottleneck is independent evaluability, not positivity or convergence of the finite jumps.

### 5. FILTERS

- **F-LATTICE — PASS:** the object is the index of an intersection of ordinary integer sublattices, with exact divisibility and prime-power thresholds.
- **F-MULT — PASS:** the valuation decomposition into prime powers and the Dirichlet series identity use unique factorization/Euler-product multiplicativity essentially.
- **F-GLOBAL — PASS only with the full moving prime system:** each (L_X) includes every prime power through (X); no fixed finite-prime approximation is used.

### 6. MILESTONE REACHED

Below M0 as a new result. The construction gives an exact representation of \(\psi(X)\) and no nontrivial error estimate for \(\psi(X)-X\).

### 7. WHY

A geometric-looking positive invariant can encode prime information exactly while leaving its global evaluation just as difficult as the original problem. Here the determinant/index is not an independent arithmetic total: its logarithm is literally \(\psi(X)\). This is related to, but distinct from, the totient gcd-determinant repackaging in Pass 11. It is a failure of this observable, not a no-go theorem for other lattice covolumes or spectra.

### 8. LESSON

Before treating an arithmetic covolume as a new route, derive its prime-valuation increments and check whether its logarithm is already the target. Require an independent ordinary-lattice evaluation with an explicit remainder before claiming scale separation.

### 9. FAILURE CLASS

**New class 19: lcm covolume is an exact but unevaluated encoding of the Chebyshev function.** The jump identity is proved; the prime-error no-go beyond this exact encoding remains open.

## Pass 32 — Positive Farey-denominator mass

### 1. MECHANISM

For integers \(q\ge2\), let \(\varphi(q)\) count reduced residues \(1\le a<q\), equivalently Farey fractions of exact denominator \(q\) in \((0,1)\). Give each fraction of denominator \(q\) the positive weight \(\log q/(q-1)\), and define
\[
A(X)=\sum_{2\le q\le X}\frac{\varphi(q)}{q-1}\log q.
\]
For a prime denominator \(q=p\), \(\varphi(p)=p-1\), so the prime-denominator channel is exactly \(\theta(X)=\sum_{p\le X}\log p\). Every composite denominator contributes nonnegatively. The candidate asks whether this primitive-fraction geometry supplies a known positive total whose prime channel can be extracted.

### 2. DERIVATION

Put \(f(n)=\varphi(n)/n\) and \(c=1/\zeta(2)=6/\pi^2\). Möbius inversion of \(\varphi(n)/n=\sum_{d\mid n}\mu(d)/d\) gives, for integer \(N\ge1\),
\[
F(N):=\sum_{n\le N}f(n)=\sum_{d\le N}\frac{\mu(d)}d\left\lfloor\frac Nd\right\rfloor.
\]
Since \(c=\sum_{d\ge1}\mu(d)/d^2\), \( |\lfloor N/d\rfloor-N/d|<1\), \(\sum_{d\le N}1/d\le1+\log N\), and \(N\sum_{d>N}d^{-2}\le1\),
\[
|F(N)-cN|\le2+\log N.
\]
For real \(t\ge1\), replacing \(t\) by \(\lfloor t\rfloor\) gives \( |F(t)-ct|\le3+\log t\). Partial summation therefore yields
\[
\left|\sum_{n\le X}f(n)\log n-cX(\log X-1)\right|
\le\tfrac32(\log X)^2+6\log X+1.
\]
Finally, \(\varphi(n)/(n-1)=f(n)(1+1/(n-1))\), and \(0\le f(n)\le1\). Thus the correction is at most
\[
\sum_{n=2}^X\frac{\log n}{n-1}\le\log X(1+\log X),
\]
which proves the explicit bound
\[
\boxed{\left|A(X)-\frac6{\pi^2}X(\log X-1)\right|\le3(\log X)^2+7\log X+2.}
\]
The prime channel is \(\theta(X)\) exactly. The composite channel is \(A(X)-\theta(X)\ge0\); no identity here evaluates it separately. Positivity only gives \(\theta(X)\le A(X)=O(X\log X)\), weaker than the classical Chebyshev bound \(O(X)\). Inverting the totient mean used to evaluate \(A\) relies on signed Möbius coefficients and does not invert the prime/composite channel split. The precise loss is the uncontrolled composite-denominator remainder. Under the established PNT benchmark, \(A(X)-\theta(X)\sim(6/\pi^2)X\log X\), so the prime share is asymptotic to \(\pi^2/(6\log X)\) and tends to zero. This asymptotic uses PNT as background, not as a consequence of the candidate.

### 3. NUMERIC CHECK

An exact totient sieve computed \(\varphi(q)\) through \(10^6\); logarithms and sums used 60-digit Decimal arithmetic. An independent reduced-residue count checked all \(299\) denominators \(2\le q\le300\), with zero mismatches. The final column is the exact prime-channel share of the positive total. The explicit remainder bound holds at every displayed cutoff.

| \(X\) | \(A(X)\) | \(cX(\log X-1)\) | residual | explicit bound | \(\theta(X)\) | composite channel | prime share |
|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 10 | 10.847549454131 | 7.918767805021 | 2.928781649109 | 34.023789982394 | 5.347107530717 | 5.500441923413 | 0.49293230 |
| 100 | 226.926092336101 | 219.168066285828 | 7.758026050273 | 97.858968627657 | 83.728390399064 | 143.197701937037 | 0.36896766 |
| 1,000 | 3,608.720260631484 | 3,591.484545214437 | 17.235715417046 | 193.505535935792 | 956.245265120059 | 2,652.474995511425 | 0.26498182 |
| 10,000 | 49,940.869119037086 | 49,912.884275705917 | 27.984843331169 | 320.963491906796 | 9,895.991379156987 | 40,044.877739880099 | 0.19815417 |
| 100,000 | 639,152.434054806069 | 639,109.230992674631 | 43.203062131438 | 480.232836540671 | 99,685.389268612551 | 539,467.044786193518 | 0.15596497 |
| 1,000,000 | 7,790,955.387857116613 | 7,790,896.192282900894 | 59.195574215719 | 671.313569837417 | 998,484.175025634292 | 6,792,471.212831482321 | 0.12815940 |

These finite shares are diagnostics; only the PNT-backed asymptotic above proves their limiting behavior. Full output and reproducible code are in `scripts/scale_separation_pass32_output.txt` and `scripts/scale_separation_pass32.py`.

### 4. ERROR ANALYSIS

The weighted Farey total is evaluated with an explicit \(O(\log^2X)\) remainder, but its prime component is only bounded by the whole total. Since that total is order \(X\log X\), this gives \(\theta(X)=O(X\log X)\), not an improvement over established estimates and not an estimate for \(\theta(X)-X\). The composite channel has order \(X\log X\) under the already-known PNT, whereas the target has order \(X\); subtracting it requires an independent asymptotic for the composite denominators. This candidate reaches below M0 as a new mechanism. The exact loss is a logarithmically large positive background, not an error in the Farey total's evaluation.

### 5. FILTERS

- **F-LATTICE — PASS.** \(\varphi(q)\) counts primitive residues/reduced lattice directions and the mean estimate uses the exact ordinary floor count \(\lfloor X/d\rfloor\); this is not transferred to arbitrary Beurling systems.
- **F-MULT — PASS.** The identity \(\varphi(n)/n=\sum_{d\mid n}\mu(d)/d\), the Euler product for \(1/\zeta(2)\), and the prime-denominator channel use unique factorization essentially.
- **F-GLOBAL — PASS.** All denominators through each moving cutoff are included; no fixed finite prime set is substituted for the full system.

### 6. MILESTONE REACHED

**Below M0 as a new result.** The mechanism evaluates a positive total sharply but does not derive PNT or any new error term for \(\psi(X)-X\) or \(\theta(X)-X\).

### 7. WHY

Normalizing by \(q-1\) makes the prime-denominator contribution exactly \(\log p\), but the number and total weight of composite-denominator fractions produce a larger \(X\log X\) background. The explicit ordinary-lattice asymptotic evaluates the whole Farey statistic, not its sparse prime subchannel. The failure is intrinsic to this particular weighting; it is not a no-go for all primitive-lattice or Farey projections.

### 8. LESSON

When a positive Farey or primitive-lattice count gives coefficient one to prime denominators, compare the order of its full total with the target channel before treating total accuracy as prime extraction. A useful next weight must suppress composite denominators to order \(X\) while retaining an independently evaluated positive total and exact ordinary-lattice structure.

### 9. FAILURE CLASS

**New class 20: the Farey primitive-fraction total has a logarithmically large composite background.** The explicit total asymptotic and finite channel split are established; the failure is specific to this weight, with no universal prime-error no-go claimed.

## Pass 33 — Multiplicative small-ω tilt with a moving parameter

### 1. MECHANISM

For integer (Xge 3), let (omega(n)) be the number of distinct prime divisors of (n), set (z_X=(log X)^{-2}), and form the positive statistic

\[
S_z(X)=\sum_{2\le n\le X}z^{\omega(n)}\log n,\qquad U_z(X)=S_z(X)/z.
\]

Unlike Pass 12's nonlinear totient-ratio weight, this is multiplicative and has an Euler product. The integers with exactly one distinct prime factor are prime powers, and their contribution to (U_z) is (P_1(X)=\sum_{p^a\le X}a\log p\). Integers with at least two distinct prime factors contribute a nonnegative leakage (C_z(X)), with (0\le C_z(X)\le z\sum_{n\le X}\log n\). Thus the small parameter suppresses multi-prime composites while preserving a prime-power channel.

### 2. DERIVATION

The Dirichlet series is absolutely convergent for \(\Re s>1\), with

\[
F_z(s)=\sum_{n\ge1}\frac{z^{\omega(n)}}{n^s}
=\prod_p\left(1+\frac{z}{p^s-1}\right)
=\zeta(s)^zG_z(s),
\quad
G_z(s)=\prod_p\left(1+\frac{z}{p^s-1}\right)(1-p^{-s})^z.
\]

The first-order local terms cancel, so \(G_z(s)\) converges absolutely for \(\Re s>1/2\), uniformly on compact subsets for \(0\le z\le1\). In particular,

\[
G_z(1)=\prod_p\left(1+\frac{z}{p-1}\right)(1-1/p)^z,
\qquad 0\le\log G_z(1)\le\frac{\pi^2}{12}z.
\]

For the last bound put \(u=(p-1)^{-1}\). A local logarithm is \(\log(1+zu)-z\log(1+u)\), which lies between 0 and \(z u^2/2\); summing and using \(\sum_{m\ge1}m^{-2}=\pi^2/6\) proves the inequality.

Use the uniform Selberg–Delange expansion through two correction terms:

\[
A_z(t):=\sum_{n\le t}z^{\omega(n)}
=t(\log t)^{z-1}\left(\lambda_0(z)+\frac{\lambda_1(z)}{\log t}+\frac{\lambda_2(z)}{(\log t)^2}+O((\log t)^{-3})\right),
\quad \lambda_0(z)=\frac{G_z(1)}{\Gamma(z)},
\]

uniformly for \(0<z\le1\); for fixed \(j=0,1,2\), \(\lambda_j(z)=O(z)\) as \(z\downarrow0\), since the Selberg–Delange coefficients contain \(1/\Gamma(z-j)\), which vanishes at zero. The uniformity matters: a fixed-\(z\) asymptotic alone would not justify \(z=z_X\).

Partial summation, \(S_z(X)=A_z(X)\log X-\int_1^X A_z(t)\,dt/t\), then gives

\[
U_{z_X}(X)
=X(\log X)^{z_X}\left(\frac{G_{z_X}(1)}{\Gamma(1+z_X)}+O((\log X)^{-1})\right)
+O\!\left(X(\log X)^{z_X-3}/z_X\right)
=X+O\!\left(\frac{X}{\log X}\right).
\]

Here \((\log X)^{z_X}=1+O(\log\log X/(\log X)^2)\), \(G_{z_X}(1)/\Gamma(1+z_X)=1+O(z_X)\), and the last Selberg–Delange remainder is \(O(X/\log X)\) after division by \(z_X=(\log X)^{-2}\). The portion below the uniform asymptotic's fixed lower threshold can be bounded directly by \(O(\sqrt X\log X)\), after applying the expansion only for \(t\ge\sqrt X\).

The exact coefficient split is

\[
U_z(X)=P_1(X)+C_z(X),\quad C_z(X)=\sum_{\substack{2\le n\le X\\\omega(n)\ge2}}z^{\omega(n)-1}\log n,
\quad 0\le C_z(X)\le z\sum_{n\le X}\log n\le zX\log X.
\]

Also

\[
P_1(X)=\psi(X)+R_2(X),\quad R_2(X)=\sum_{a\ge2,\,p^a\le X}(a-1)\log p.
\]

Writing \(L=\lfloor\log_2X\rfloor\), an unconditional elementary bound is

\[
0\le R_2(X)\le \sqrt X\log X+\frac12X^{1/3}\log X\,(L-1)(L-2)
=O(\sqrt X\log X+X^{1/3}\log^3X).
\]

The first term bounds squares; for exponents \(a\ge3\), use at most \(X^{1/a}\le X^{1/3}\) prime bases and sum \(a-1\) through \(L\). Combining the three estimates yields \(\psi(X)=X+O(X/\log X)\). The loss is exactly the deliberately coarse multi-prime leakage \(zX\log X=X/\log X\); this argument does not improve the known prime-number error bounds.

**Analytic input / sign-free audit.** The weights in \(S_z\) and both arithmetic leakage channels are nonnegative. Its aggregate evaluation is not a sign-free lattice argument: it imports the uniform Selberg–Delange theorem for \(\zeta(s)^zG_z(s)\), proved by complex-analytic continuation and contour estimates using the ordinary zeta function's zero-free region. So this is a valid multiplicative extraction calculation but not an independent positive-only proof route; the analytic input is stronger than the M1 conclusion sought.

### 3. NUMERIC CHECK

The reproducible program `scripts/scale_separation_pass33.py` used an exact sieve for primes and \(\omega(n)\) through \(10^6\), with 60-digit Decimal logarithms and sums. It computed \(S_z/z\) by grouping exact integers by \(\omega(n)\), and independently accumulated \(P_1\), \(\psi\), and \(T(X)=\sum_{n\le X}\log n\). At each cutoff it checked the exact decomposition and \(0\le C_z\le zT(X)\). Raw output is in `scripts/scale_separation_pass33_output.txt`.

The composite leakage inequality held at all six cutoffs. At \(X=10^6\), the observed \(U-X\) is about \(0.02046X\), while the rigorous leakage ceiling is \(0.06714X\); finite agreement checks the identities, not the asymptotic estimate.

### 4. ERROR ANALYSIS

The analytic uniform expansion plus partial summation gives the claimed \(O(X/\log X)\) approximation for \(U\); the arithmetic transfer errors are explicitly \(C_z\le X/\log X\) and \(R_2\le\sqrt X\log X+\tfrac12X^{1/3}\log X(L-1)(L-2)\). Thus M1 with \(A=1\) follows under the standard uniform Selberg–Delange theorem. No M2+ improvement is claimed. The controlling analytic input already uses the ordinary zeta zero-free region; the positive statistic does not produce that input independently. Choosing smaller z could reduce composite leakage, but it would amplify the uniform asymptotic remainder after division by z; optimizing that balance requires a separate quantified theorem and is not part of this pass.

### 5. FILTERS

- **F-LATTICE — PASS, with the exact dependency stated.** The sum is over the ordinary integers and factors through the ordinary \(\zeta(s)\); the decisive uniform analytic continuation/zero-free region for this zeta factor is not guaranteed for a Beurling system with only regular integer counts. This does not use a new exact floor, Poisson, or divisor-problem estimate.
- **F-MULT — PASS.** The local factors \(1+z/(p^s-1)\), the definition of \(\omega\), and the prime-power channel all depend on unique factorization/Euler-product multiplicativity.
- **F-GLOBAL — PASS only with the full moving system.** The weight uses every integer and every prime factor through X; no fixed finite-prime truncation is claimed uniform.
- **Mission's positive-only constraint — FAIL as an independent proof mechanism.** The observable is positive, but the aggregate evaluation imports complex-analytic Selberg–Delange/zero-free-region input, which is already stronger than the M1 conclusion.

### 6. MILESTONE REACHED

**M1 (conditional only on the standard uniform Selberg–Delange expansion, which is a known theorem):** \(\psi(X)=X+O(X/\log X)\). This is strictly weaker than the established analytic PNT error and supplies no improvement to known results. The pass records a positive multiplicative extraction with an explicitly located analytic dependency, not a new PNT proof.

### 7. WHY

The moving multiplicative tilt separates integers with one distinct prime factor from those with at least two, and its small-z Euler-product singularity gives the right leading scale. However, the aggregate's mean is evaluated by Selberg–Delange, whose zero-free-region input already encodes substantial prime information; the candidate therefore repackages known analytic input rather than deriving an independent lattice estimate. The M1 error floor in this concrete choice comes from \(zX\log X\), while reducing z requires sharper uniform control of the asymptotic remainder. This is a limitation of this parameter choice, not a no-go for all multiplicative tilts.

### 8. LESSON

Before accepting a prime-selective multiplicative weight, audit the theorem that evaluates its total: a positive coefficient sequence does not make a complex-analytic mean-value theorem sign-free. The next candidate must derive its aggregate from an independent exact ordinary-lattice identity, or prove a strictly weaker analytic input than the target error, and must quantify the z-versus-remainder tradeoff before decreasing z.

### 9. FAILURE CLASS

**New class 21: a positive small-\(\omega\) tilt extracts prime powers only after importing the analytic prime input.** The coefficient split and leakage bounds are exact; the M1 estimate is inherited from uniform Selberg–Delange and is not a new improvement. No universal no-go for multiplicative tilts is claimed.

**References.** Selberg's formula for \(\sum_{n\le x}z^{\omega(n)}\) and its Euler-product constant are reproduced in the Erdős–Nicolas paper hosted by the Rényi Institute: <https://www.renyi.hu/~p_erdos/1980-44.pdf>. For general hypotheses and uniform forms of the Selberg–Delange method, see de la Bretèche–Tenenbaum, *Remarks on the Selberg–Delange method*, <https://arxiv.org/abs/2010.12929>.

## Pass 34 — Multiplicative Ω-tilt isolates primes rather than prime powers

### 1. MECHANISM

Let Ω(n)=∑_p v_p(n) be the number of prime factors with multiplicity. For 0<z≤1 define

\[
S_z(X)=\sum_{2\le n\le X}z^{\Omega(n)}\log n,\qquad U_z(X)=S_z(X)/z,\qquad z_X=(\log X)^{-2}.
\]

This differs from Pass 33: there the ω(n)=1 channel included every prime power; here Ω(n)=1 consists exactly of primes. Thus (U_z) contains θ(X) as its prime channel, and all prime powers and other composites are suppressed together.

### 2. DERIVATION

Unique factorization gives the exact Dirichlet series for Re(s)>1:

\[
F_z(s)=\sum_{n\ge1}\frac{z^{\Omega(n)}}{n^s}
=\prod_p(1-zp^{-s})^{-1}
=\zeta(s)^zG_z(s),\quad
G_z(s)=\prod_p\frac{(1-p^{-s})^z}{1-zp^{-s}}.
\]

The linear terms cancel in each local logarithm, so (G_z) converges locally uniformly for Re(s)>1/2 and (0\le z\le1). At s=1, setting u=1/p gives

\[
\log G_z(1)=\sum_p\sum_{k\ge2}\frac{z^k-z}{k p^k},\qquad -z/2\le\log G_z(1)\le0.
\]

Indeed (0\le z-z^k\le z), (1/k\le1/2) for k≥2, and \(\sum_p\sum_{k\ge2}p^{-k}\le\sum_{n\ge2}1/(n(n-1))=1\). Hence (G_z(1)=1+O(z)).

The coefficient split is exact:

\[
U_z(X)=\theta(X)+C_z(X),\qquad
C_z(X)=\sum_{\substack{2\le n\le X\\\Omega(n)\ge2}}z^{\Omega(n)-1}\log n.
\]

Since (z^{\Omega(n)-1}\le z) on this sum,

\[
0\le C_z(X)\le zT(X),\qquad T(X)=\sum_{n\le X}\log n=X\log X-X+O(\log X).
\]

Thus for (z_X=(\log X)^{-2}), the composite leakage is at most (X/\log X+O(X/\log^2X)).

For the analytic evaluation, write (A_z(t)=\sum_{n\le t}z^{\Omega(n)}). A uniform Selberg–Delange expansion for (0<z\le1), with its coefficients and remainder uniformly (O(z)) after removing the n=1 term, yields

\[
A_z(t)=t(\log t)^{z-1}\left(\frac{G_z(1)}{\Gamma(z)}+O\!\left(\frac{z}{\log t}\right)\right).
\]

Partial summation (S_z(X)=A_z(X)\log X-\int_1^X A_z(t)\,dt/t), applying the expansion for t≥\sqrt X and bounding the initial interval directly, gives

\[
U_{z_X}(X)=X(\log X)^{z_X}\left(\frac{G_{z_X}(1)}{\Gamma(1+z_X)}+O((\log X)^{-1})\right)+O(\sqrt X\log X)
=X+O(X/\log X).
\]

Combining with the exact channel inequality gives θ(X)=X+O(X/\log X), and then ψ(X)=X+O(X/\log X), since ψ(X)-θ(X) is the higher-prime-power sum, bounded by (O(\sqrt X\log X+X^{1/3}\log^2X)). The claimed uniform-in-z Selberg–Delange remainder is the analytic input; without that theorem, the (U_z) asymptotic and the resulting M1 conclusion are conditional. This uses the classical zeta zero-free region and is not an independent sign-free derivation.

### 3. NUMERIC CHECK

The reproducible exact sieve computes Ω(n) and primality through (10^6); logarithms and sums use 60-digit Decimal arithmetic. It checks Ω=1 equals the prime channel and (0\le C_z\le zT(X)) at each cutoff.

| X | z | U=S_z/z | U-X | θ(X) | U-θ | C_z | zT(X) bound | θ-X |
|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 10 | 0.1886116970 | 6.869217222479 | -3.13078278 | 5.347107530717 | 1.52210969 | 1.52210969 | 2.84886889 | -4.65289247 |
| 100 | 0.0471529243 | 89.78300235000 | -10.21699765 | 83.72839039906 | 6.05461195 | 6.05461195 | 17.15137522 | -16.27160960 |
| 1,000 | 0.0209568552 | 993.6716264850 | -6.32837351 | 956.2452651201 | 37.42636136 | 37.42636136 | 123.8996143 | -43.75473488 |
| 10,000 | 0.0117882311 | 10,151.24119237 | 151.2411924 | 9,895.991379157 | 255.2498132 | 255.2498132 | 967.9190137 | -104.0086208 |
| 100,000 | 0.0075444679 | 101,545.4994142 | 1,545.499414 | 99,685.38926861 | 1,860.110146 | 1,860.110146 | 7,931.493212 | -314.6107314 |
| 1,000,000 | 0.0052392138 | 1,012,621.761478 | 12,621.76148 | 998,484.1750256 | 14,137.58645 | 14,137.58645 | 67,143.24085 | -1,515.824974 |

The finite identities and leakage inequality passed all cutoffs. At (10^6), observed (U-X\approx0.01262X), and the rigorous leakage ceiling is about (0.06714X). This is a finite check only; it does not establish the asymptotic estimate.

### 4. ERROR ANALYSIS

Under the stated uniform Selberg–Delange input, the normalized weighted mean has error (O(X/\log X)). Removing the nonnegative composite channel costs at most (zT(X)=X/\log X+O(X/\log^2X)), so this mechanism yields only M1. The conversion from θ to ψ costs (O(\sqrt X\log X+X^{1/3}\log^2X)), smaller than M1. The decisive loss is the deliberately coarse leakage ceiling; decreasing z would require a correspondingly sharper uniform remainder after division by z. No improvement over known elementary or analytic PNT errors is claimed.

### 5. FILTERS

- **F-LATTICE — PASS with qualification.** The statistic is over the exact ordinary integer lattice and uses its classical zeta function's analytic continuation and zero-free region. It is not a universal argument based only on regular integer counts; the analytic theorem is imported.
- **F-MULT — PASS.** The Euler product and the exact Ω-channel split use unique factorization essentially.
- **F-GLOBAL — PASS.** The moving cutoff includes all primes and all integers through X; no finite-prime truncation is claimed uniform.
- **Positive-only independent mechanism — FAIL.** Although all summands and leakage are nonnegative, evaluating the total imports the complex-analytic Selberg–Delange theorem.

### 6. MILESTONE REACHED

**M1, conditional on the standard uniform Selberg–Delange expansion as stated.** The resulting (O(X/\log X)) error is weaker than known elementary and analytic PNT errors. The computation itself reaches no asymptotic milestone.

### 7. WHY

Using Ω instead of ω moves prime powers into the leakage and makes the target channel exactly θ. This improves the channel definition, not the aggregate estimate: the total is evaluated through ζ(s)^z, whose zero-free-region input already carries substantial prime-distribution information. Thus this is a distinct extraction identity but not an independent positive-lattice route. This is a limitation of the present parameterized mean, not a no-go for all multiplicative mechanisms.

### 8. LESSON

Distinguish ω from Ω: Ω=1 isolates primes, whereas ω=1 includes every prime power. For a future candidate, either evaluate the positive aggregate using an exact lattice identity independent of a prime-distribution theorem, or explicitly quantify how its analytic input is weaker than the target conclusion.

### 9. FAILURE CLASS

**New class 22: multiplicity-sensitive positive tilt isolates primes but imports the analytic mean estimate.** Exact extraction and leakage bounds are proved; M1 follows only through uniform Selberg–Delange. This is not a universal no-go.

**References.** For Selberg–Delange expansions with a complex parameter in multiplicative sums, see Tenenbaum's treatment cited in the [survey of the Selberg–Delange method](https://arxiv.org/abs/2010.12929); the exact local factor here is derived above. The method's need for analytic continuation and zero-free-region estimates is part of the imported input, not a conclusion of the finite checks.

## Pass 35 — Largest-prime-factor mass and smooth-cofactor leakage

### 1. MECHANISM

For (n\ge2), let (P^+(n)) be its largest prime factor and define the positive integer-lattice statistic

\[
L(X)=\sum_{2\le n\le X}\log P^+(n).
\]

Unlike the parameter tilts in Passes 33–34, this groups integers by their extremal prime factor and its smooth cofactor. Its exact prime channel is θ(X); prime powers and integers with additional smaller prime factors form a nonnegative smooth-composite remainder.

### 2. DERIVATION

For each prime p, define

\[
\Psi_{<p}(y)=\#\{m\le y: \text{every prime factor of }m\text{ is }<p\},
\]

including m=1. Every n with (P^+(n)=p) has a unique representation (n=p^a m), with a≥1 and all prime factors of m less than p. Therefore the exact lattice identity is

\[
L(X)=\sum_{p\le X}\log p\sum_{a\ge1}\Psi_{<p}(X/p^a).
\]

The single term a=1,m=1 contributes θ(X). Removing it gives

\[
L(X)=\theta(X)+C(X),\quad
C(X)=\sum_{p\le X}\log p\left(\sum_{a\ge1}\Psi_{<p}(X/p^a)-1\right)\ge0.
\]

This is an exact decomposition, but the rough-cofactor counts in C(X) are not evaluated by the identity. The only immediate universal bounds are

\[
\theta(X)\le L(X)\le T(X)=\sum_{n\le X}\log n=\log(X!),
\]

because (1\le P^+(n)\le n). Hence the identity alone gives at best the weaker upper bound θ(X)≤XlogX+O(X); it gives no lower bound for θ(X)-X and no error estimate for ψ(X)-X. The higher prime-power correction ψ-θ is (O(\sqrt X\log X+X^{1/3}\log^2X)), but that small conversion error does not repair the missing estimate for the prime channel.

### 3. NUMERIC CHECK

The reproducible program sieves primes exactly through (10^6), assigns each integer its exact largest prime factor, and sums prime logarithms at 60-digit precision. It independently computes each group count, θ(X), and T(X)=log(X!) at high precision. The exact check is (L=\theta+C), (C\ge0), and (L\le T).

| X | L(X) | θ(X) | composite remainder C | T(X) bound | prime share θ/L | C/θ |
|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 10 | 10.54006438161 | 5.347107530717 | 5.192956850890 | 15.10441257308 | 0.507313 | 0.971171 |
| 100 | 238.3335323007 | 83.72839039906 | 154.6051419016 | 363.7393755556 | 0.351308 | 1.846508 |
| 1,000 | 3,880.435696709 | 956.2452651201 | 2,924.190431589 | 5,912.128178488 | 0.246427 | 3.057992 |
| 10,000 | 53,778.53242049 | 9,895.991379157 | 43,882.54104133 | 82,108.92783681 | 0.184014 | 4.434375 |
| 100,000 | 685,710.1233530 | 99,685.38926861 | 586,024.7340843 | 1,051,299.221899 | 0.145375 | 5.878742 |
| 1,000,000 | 8,320,672.722931 | 998,484.1750256 | 7,322,188.547905 | 12,815,518.38466 | 0.120000 | 7.333305 |

The measured prime share decreases over these cutoffs, while the composite remainder grows relative to θ. This is finite evidence only; it is not an asymptotic proof of that trend.

### 4. ERROR ANALYSIS

No nontrivial error term for ψ(X)-X follows. The positive identity gives only θ(X)≤L(X), while its total is bounded above by (T(X)\asymp X\log X); it supplies no lower bound on the prime channel and no independent evaluation of C(X). Thus it does not reach M0. The precise loss is the unevaluated sum of p-smooth cofactors Ψ_{<p}(X/p^a), whose aggregate can be larger than the prime channel. An asymptotic for this statistic derived using the prime number theorem would be circular for the present purpose.

### 5. FILTERS

- **F-LATTICE — PASS, narrowly.** The identity uses exact ordinary-integer cutoffs (p^a m\le X) and exact counts of cofactors with all prime factors below p. It is tied to the ordinary integer lattice; no transfer to Beurling systems is asserted.
- **F-MULT — PASS.** Unique factorization and the ordered prime factors define (P^+(n)) and the unique p^a m decomposition.
- **F-GLOBAL — PASS.** Every prime p≤X and every admissible cofactor are included; no finite-prime truncation is used.

### 6. MILESTONE REACHED

**Below M0.** The exact decomposition is proved, but it does not recover the PNT or an explicit prime-counting error. Numerical scale profiles do not change this milestone.

### 7. WHY

The largest-prime statistic tags one prime factor of each integer but assigns the same log p to every integer in the p-smooth cofactor family. The exact lattice identity therefore exposes a prime term and a large positive smooth-composite background without evaluating their difference. This is a limitation of this statistic, not a no-go for all extremal-factor or smooth-number transforms.

### 8. LESSON

For a factorization-based positive observable, compute the full cofactor multiplicity of each proposed prime channel before treating that channel as isolated. A future extremal-factor candidate must either evaluate the smooth-composite remainder independently or apply a nonnegative weighting whose remainder has a proved smaller order.

### 9. FAILURE CLASS

**New class 23: extremal-prime lattice statistic has an unevaluated smooth-cofactor background.** The decomposition is exact and all terms are nonnegative; no asymptotic estimate for the complement or universal no-go is proved.

**Reproducibility.** See `scripts/scale_separation_pass35.py` and `scripts/scale_separation_pass35_output.txt`.

## Pass 36 — Least-prime-factor mass and rough-cofactor inclusion-exclusion

### 1. MECHANISM

For (n\ge2), let (P^-(n)) be its least prime factor and set

\[
R(X)=\sum_{2\le n\le X}\log P^-(n).
\]

This is the complementary endpoint to Pass 35's largest-prime statistic. Grouping by the least factor produces an exact θ(X) channel, while the complement consists of prime powers and integers whose other prime factors are all larger than the least one.

### 2. DERIVATION

For prime p and y≥1 define the rough count

\[
\Phi(y,p)=\#\{m\le y: m=1\text{ or every prime factor of }m\text{ is }>p\}.
\]

Every n with (P^-(n)=p) has a unique representation (n=p^a m) with a≥1 and m counted by Φ(X/p^a,p). Hence

\[
R(X)=\sum_{p\le X}\log p\sum_{a\ge1}\Phi(X/p^a,p)
=\theta(X)+C_-(X),
\]

where

\[
C_-(X)=\sum_{p\le X}\log p\left(\sum_{a\ge1}\Phi(X/p^a,p)-1\right)\ge0.
\]

The subtraction removes only the (a=1,m=1) prime n=p. A direct inclusion-exclusion formula is

\[
\Phi(y,p)=\sum_{d\mid P(p)}\mu(d)\left\lfloor\frac yd\right\rfloor,
\qquad P(p)=\prod_{q\le p,\ q\text{ prime}}q.
\]

This is exact, but uses signed Möbius coefficients. Without this inclusion-exclusion or another independent evaluation of rough counts, positivity yields only

\[
\theta(X)\le R(X)\le T(X)=\log(X!)=X\log X-X+O(\log X).
\]

Thus no nontrivial bound for θ(X)-X or ψ(X)-X follows. The higher prime-power conversion satisfies ψ-θ (=O(\sqrt X\log X+X^{1/3}\log^2X)), but this does not address the unevaluated rough-cofactor term.

### 3. NUMERIC CHECK

An exact sieve assigned every n≤2 through (10^6) its least prime factor; logarithms of prime values and sums were evaluated at 60-digit precision. For each X the code checks the exact group identity (R=\theta+C_-), (C_-≥0), and (R\le\log(X!)).

| X | R(X) | θ(X) | rough-composite remainder C₋ | T(X) bound | prime share θ/R | C₋/θ |
|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 10 | 9.218308541625 | 5.347107530717 | 3.871201010908 | 15.10441257308 | 0.580053 | 0.723980 |
| 100 | 150.7647567870 | 83.72839039906 | 67.03636638790 | 363.7393755556 | 0.555358 | 0.800641 |
| 1,000 | 1,832.583210483 | 956.2452651201 | 876.3379453633 | 5,912.128178488 | 0.521802 | 0.916436 |
| 10,000 | 20,298.67175359 | 9,895.991379157 | 10,402.68037443 | 82,108.92783681 | 0.487519 | 1.051201 |
| 100,000 | 217,075.1566447 | 99,685.38926861 | 117,389.7673761 | 1,051,299.221899 | 0.459221 | 1.177603 |
| 1,000,000 | 2,282,466.189708 | 998,484.1750256 | 1,283,982.014682 | 12,815,518.38466 | 0.437458 | 1.285931 |

The finite prime share declines from 0.580 to 0.437; this is descriptive data only and does not establish its limiting behavior. Unlike Pass 35's largest-factor statistic, the rough remainder is closer in size to the prime channel, but remains larger by X=10^6.

### 4. ERROR ANALYSIS

The identity provides only θ(X)≤R(X)≤T(X), so it yields at most θ(X)=O(XlogX), weaker than the classical Chebyshev bound, and no estimate of ψ(X)-X. The exact point where a positive evaluation is lost is Φ: its floor formula is inclusion-exclusion with μ(d) signs. Keeping Φ unevaluated preserves positivity but leaves C₋ uncontrolled; evaluating it by the displayed formula violates the sign-free requirement. No milestone is reached.

### 5. FILTERS

- **F-LATTICE — PASS for the exact identity, FAIL for a sign-free asymptotic evaluation.** The ordinary floor counts ⌊y/d⌋ are exact-lattice input. The direct exact count can be transferred only with a system-specific rough-count law; none is proved for Beurling systems here.
- **F-MULT — PASS.** Unique factorization defines the least prime factor and the rough cofactor decomposition; the exact inclusion-exclusion uses the Möbius function.
- **F-GLOBAL — PASS with explicit moving support.** Φ(y,p) depends on every prime q≤p; as p grows with X, no fixed prime set suffices.

### 6. MILESTONE REACHED

**Below M0.** The exact identity and finite checks do not establish the PNT or any nontrivial prime-counting error.

### 7. WHY

The least-factor grouping makes primes an endpoint contribution, but the complement is a family of p-rough integers. Its exact ordinary-lattice count is obtained by signed inclusion-exclusion over all small primes. Retaining positive counts avoids those signs but supplies no estimate; this is a specific rough-count obstruction, not a no-go for all sieve or extremal-factor methods.

### 8. LESSON

For a positive endpoint channel defined by a least prime factor, expose the exact rough-number count before claiming scale separation. A next candidate must either bound that complement using positive data alone or derive a new identity that evaluates it without Möbius cancellation.

### 9. FAILURE CLASS

**New class 24: least-prime endpoint is obscured by rough cofactors whose exact count uses Möbius signs.** The finite decomposition is exact; no universal no-go is proved.

**Reproducibility.** See `scripts/scale_separation_pass36.py` and `scripts/scale_separation_pass36_output.txt`.

## Pass 37 — Square-root roughness as a positive prime selector

### 1. MECHANISM

For (z=\lfloor\sqrt X\rfloor), define the positive indicator and its lattice sum

\[
w_z(n)=\prod_{p\le z}\bigl(1-\mathbf 1_{p\mid n}\bigr)\in\{0,1\},\qquad
S(X,z)=\sum_{1\le n\le X}w_z(n).
\]

This differs from the prior factor-weighted sums: it is a full product of local exclusion masks at the square-root scale. Any composite n≤2 has a prime factor at most √ n≤z, so (w_z(n)=1) exactly for n=1 and primes z<p≤X. Thus (S(X,z)=1+\pi(X)-\pi(z)) exactly.

### 2. DERIVATION

The equivalence follows from the least-prime-factor theorem for composites: if n=ab with 1<a≤b, then a≤√n≤√X, and a has a prime divisor p≤a≤z. Conversely a prime p>z has no divisor among the mask primes. Therefore

\[
S(X,z)=1+\#\{p:z<p\le X\}=1+\pi(X)-\pi(z).
\]

Expanding the product gives an exact but signed floor formula:

\[
S(X,z)=\sum_{d\mid P(z)}\mu(d)\left\lfloor\frac Xd\right\rfloor,
\qquad P(z)=\prod_{p\le z}p.
\]

The naive replacement of each floor by X/d produces

\[
XV(z),\qquad V(z)=\prod_{p\le z}(1-1/p)=\sum_{d\mid P(z)}\mu(d)/d,
\]

but only the crude explicit bound

\[
|S(X,z)-XV(z)|≤ 2^{\pi(z)},
\]

because there are (2^{\pi(z)}) squarefree divisors of P(z), and each floor error is less than 1. This bound is useless at z=√X: for X=10^6, π(z)=168 and the bound is (2^{168}\approx3.74\times10^{50}). Sharper control requires cancellation among μ(d) or a sieve majorant/minorant; the former is signed, while the latter does not automatically give an asymptotic at this sieve level.

The exact identity (S=1+\pi(X)-\pi(z)) is not an independent evaluation of π(X). It transfers the desired prime count into a positive mask whose full lattice sum remains unevaluated by a sign-free formula. Consequently this mechanism yields no bound for ψ(X)-X beyond the tautological exact rewriting.

### 3. NUMERIC CHECK

The code sieves primes exactly to (10^6), forms each mask by crossing out all multiples of p≤z, and evaluates V(z) as a 60-digit product. It verifies the exact identity (S=1+\#\{p>z\}). The table also reports the observed discrepancy from the naive density prediction XV(z).

| X | z | S(X,z) | primes z<p≤X | identity gap | V(z) | XV(z) | S-XV(z) | S/(XV) |
|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 10 | 3 | 3 | 2 | 0 | 0.3333333333 | 3.333333333 | -0.3333333333 | 0.900000 |
| 100 | 10 | 22 | 21 | 0 | 0.2285714286 | 22.85714286 | -0.8571428571 | 0.962500 |
| 1,000 | 31 | 158 | 157 | 0 | 0.1528521514 | 152.8521514 | 5.147848611 | 1.033679 |
| 10,000 | 100 | 1,205 | 1,204 | 0 | 0.1203172905 | 1,203.172905 | 1.827095251 | 1.001519 |
| 100,000 | 316 | 9,528 | 9,527 | 0 | 0.09651938697 | 9,651.938697 | -123.9386970 | 0.987159 |
| 1,000,000 | 1,000 | 78,331 | 78,330 | 0 | 0.08096526351 | 80,965.26351 | -2,634.263507 | 0.967464 |

The arithmetic identity passed at all six cutoffs. The observed discrepancy is finite data, not a proved error term; it is far smaller than the crude (2^{\pi(z)}) bound but this gap comes from cancellation in the signed expansion.

### 4. ERROR ANALYSIS

The exact relation is π(X)=S(X,z)+π(z)-1. It gives no independent estimate because S is exactly the unsolved prime count after a sieve mask. The floor-to-density expansion has absolute error at most (2^{\pi(z)}), which exceeds X enormously for z=√X. Improving it to a useful remainder requires control of signed Möbius sums or a sieve lower bound; at the square-root cutoff, an asymptotic with a matching lower bound cannot be inferred from the positive mask. No PNT or explicit ψ(X)-X error is derived, so the mechanism is below M0.

### 5. FILTERS

- **F-LATTICE — PASS for the exact identity; FAIL for an evaluated asymptotic.** The mask sum uses exact divisibility and ordinary integer cutoffs. The simple floor expansion's error is (2^{\pi(z)}), not a useful lattice remainder.
- **F-MULT — PASS.** The mask uses the complete set of prime divisors up to z and the fact that every composite has a prime factor at most its square root.
- **F-GLOBAL — PASS only with a moving cutoff.** All primes p≤√X are needed; a fixed finite prime set does not remove all composites as X grows. The cutoff grows with X, and evaluating its full sum is the unresolved work.

### 6. MILESTONE REACHED

**Below M0.** The positive mask is an exact prime selector, but its total is only an alternative expression for π(X)-π(√X); no PNT estimate follows.

### 7. WHY

The construction solves pointwise classification but not aggregate evaluation. The exact indicator is positive, yet evaluating its lattice sum expands into (2^{\pi(z)}) signed floor terms. The naive unsigned error bound is exponentially larger than X, and a sharp sieve estimate would need additional distributional input. This is a concrete truncation-level obstruction, not a universal no-go for all positive sieve methods.

### 8. LESSON

An exact positive indicator for primes is not a prime asymptotic. Before using a sieve mask as a scale-separation mechanism, derive a uniform estimate for its aggregate at the actual moving cutoff, including both upper and lower bounds; do not replace the signed floor expansion by its density product without controlling the full remainder.

### 9. FAILURE CLASS

**New class 25: square-root roughness gives a positive exact prime mask, but the floor-sum cancellation is uncontrolled.** Pointwise prime selection is proved; its aggregate estimate and any useful no-go theorem remain open.

**Reproducibility.** See `scripts/scale_separation_pass37.py` and `scripts/scale_separation_pass37_output.txt`.

## Pass 38 — Positive mixtures of nested roughness cutoffs

### 1. MECHANISM

For cutoffs (z_1<\cdots<z_J), all at least √X, define the square-root roughness masks (S(X,z_j)=\sum_{n\le X}\prod_{p\le z_j}(1-\mathbf1_{p\mid n})) and take a positive mixture (M(X)=\sum_{j=1}^J a_jS(X,z_j)), (a_j\ge0). Since every surviving n>1 is prime, each mask is (1+\#\{p:z_j<p\le X\}). The resulting prime weight is (K(p)=\sum_{j:z_j<p}a_j).

### 2. DERIVATION

Interchanging the finite sums gives

\[
M(X)=\left(\sum_j a_j\right)+\sum_{p\le X}K(p),\qquad K(p)=\sum_j a_j\mathbf1_{p>z_j}.
\]

For p≤q, each indicator ᵽ{p>z_j}≤ᵽ{q>z_j}; nonnegative coefficients imply (K(p)\le K(q)). Thus K is nondecreasing. If K is positive at some prime in a target band (p\le\beta), it cannot vanish at every prime (q>\beta). More quantitatively, for any (p\le q), the tail prime has weight at least K(p). Consequently no nonzero positive combination of these nested tail masks can give a compactly supported prime-band weight or suppress all larger primes below the band weight. A signed difference of masks can form a band, but violates the positive-combination condition.

For the numerical candidate, take J=5, equal weights a_j=1, and (z_j=\lfloor c_j\sqrt X\rfloor), (c_j\in\{1,1.25,1.5,1.75,2\}). Then K(p) is exactly 1,2,3,4,5 on successive threshold bands and remains 5 for all p>z_5. The mixed survivor count is exactly (J+\sum_{p\le X}K(p)).

The identity contains prime counts and has no independent lattice evaluation. Its exact floor expansion is a signed sum for each threshold; averaging with positive coefficients does not cancel these signs. Thus no error term for ψ(X)-X follows.

### 3. NUMERIC CHECK

The exact sieve computes all primes to (10^6), each survivor count, and the induced K(p) weights. It verifies (M=J+\sum_{p\le X}K(p)) and reports how much of the weighted prime sum comes from the tail above z_J.

| X | cutoffs z_j | individual mask counts | mixed M | weighted primes in (z₁,z₅] | weighted tail p>z₅ | tail share of weighted prime sum |
|---:|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 10,000 | 100,125,150,175,200 | 1,205;1,200;1,195;1,190;1,184 | 5,974 | 54 | 5,915 | 0.99095326 |
| 100,000 | 316,395,474,553,632 | 9,528;9,516;9,502;9,492;9,478 | 47,516 | 126 | 47,385 | 0.99734798 |
| 1,000,000 | 1,000;1,250;1,500;1,750;2,000 | 78,331;78,295;78,260;78,227;78,196 | 391,309 | 329 | 390,975 | 0.99915922 |

All quantities are exact integer sieve counts. At (10^6), the weighted prime-tail mass beyond the target cutoff band is 1,189 times the weighted mass inside it. This finite profile illustrates, but is not needed for, the monotonicity proof.

### 4. ERROR ANALYSIS

No PNT or ψ(X)-X error bound is obtained: M(X) is an exact weighted sum of π(X)-π(z_j), not an independently evaluated total. A theorem for this family is proved: nonnegative mixtures of nested tail indicators have a nondecreasing prime kernel, so cannot realize a nonzero compactly supported prime-band kernel. The proof does not rule out positive kernels that are not nested tails, nonlinear combinations, or independent evaluations of the weighted aggregate. Inverting the nested tails to get a band requires signed coefficients.

### 5. FILTERS

- **F-LATTICE — PASS for exact survivor identities; no evaluated asymptotic.** Each mask uses divisibility and ordinary integer cutoffs. The positive mixture leaves the total in terms of prime counts.
- **F-MULT — PASS.** The square-root guarantee uses the fact that every composite has a prime divisor at most its square root.
- **F-GLOBAL — PASS with moving cutoffs.** The largest cutoff grows as 2√X. A fixed set of excluded primes would leave composites as X grows; the family does not claim otherwise.

### 6. MILESTONE REACHED

**Below M0.** The kernel monotonicity theorem rules out band localization within this family, but gives no PNT estimate.

### 7. WHY

Nested tail masks form a positive cone of monotone kernels. Positive mixing preserves monotonicity, while band localization requires the kernel to decrease after the band. This is an algebraic obstruction intrinsic to this particular cone, not to all positive scale-separation methods.

### 8. LESSON

Before searching coefficients for a positive mixture of nested cutoffs, test the order structure of its induced prime kernel. If the generators are all tails, their positive cone cannot localize a finite band; a next candidate must use non-nested generators or a different positive observable, and then still supply an independent total evaluation.

### 9. FAILURE CLASS

**New class 26: positive mixtures of nested prime tails cannot localize a band.** The monotonicity/no-band theorem is proved for this generator family; the computational dominance is descriptive and no universal sieve no-go is claimed.

**Reproducibility.** See `scripts/scale_separation_pass38.py` and `scripts/scale_separation_pass38_output.txt`.

## Pass 39 — Positive local-divisibility difference isolates the top prime band

### 1. MECHANISM

For (y\ge1), define the local-divisibility product

\[
A_y(n)=\prod_{p\le y}(1+\mathbf1_{p\mid n})=2^{\omega_{\le y}(n)}\ge1.
\]

For integer X≥4, let (B_X(n)=A_X(n)-A_{X/2}(n)). Although written as a difference, (B_X(n)\ge0) pointwise because the first product contains every factor in the second plus additional factors ≥1. This is not the tail-mask cone of Pass 38: its induced prime weight is compactly supported on the top band.

### 2. DERIVATION

Expanding either product by squarefree divisors gives

\[
A_y(n)=\sum_{\substack{d\mid n,\ d\text{ squarefree}\\p\mid d\Rightarrow p\le y}}1.
\]

Subtracting the nested sums leaves

\[
B_X(n)=\sum_{\substack{d\mid n,\ d\text{ squarefree}\\d\mid P(X),\ d\nmid P(X/2)}}1.
\]

Every remaining d has a prime factor q>X/2. If d had any other factor at least 2, then d≥2q>X, contradicting d≤n≤X. Thus d=q is itself prime. If q|n and n≤X with q>X/2, then n=q. Therefore

\[
B_X(n)=\begin{cases}1,&X/2<n\le X\text{ and }n\text{ is prime},\\0,&\text{otherwise.}\end{cases}
\]

Consequently the positive exact lattice sum is

\[
\sum_{n\le X}\log n\,B_X(n)=\sum_{X/2<p\le X}\log p=\theta(X)-\theta(X/2).
\]

This gives an exact single-band prime channel, but the left side is not independently evaluated: expanding it reduces to a sum over the same primes p in the band, since ⌊X/p⌋=1. No error term for the right side or for ψ(X)-X follows from the identity alone. The construction also explicitly uses the prime set in its local factors; it is not a prime-free evaluation.

### 3. NUMERIC CHECK

An exact sieve computes ω(n), ω_{≤X/2}(n), and the prime band through (10^6). It verifies for every n≤X that (B_X(n)\ge0), and that B_X(n) is 1 precisely on the primes in ((X/2,X]). Log sums use 60-digit Decimal arithmetic.

| X | positive-mask support count | Σ log(n)B_X(n) | θ(X)-θ(X/2) | identity error | band primes |
|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 10 | 1 | 1.945910149055 | 1.945910149055 | 0 | 1 |
| 100 | 10 | 42.76817096771 | 42.76817096771 | 0 | 10 |
| 1,000 | 73 | 481.6908209654 | 481.6908209654 | 0 | 73 |
| 10,000 | 560 | 4,984.296027450 | 4,984.296027450 | 0 | 560 |
| 100,000 | 4,459 | 49,953.37086566 | 49,953.37086566 | 0 | 4,459 |
| 1,000,000 | 36,960 | 499,166.0552390 | 499,166.0552390 | 0 | 36,960 |

All finite identities match exactly (up to displayed decimal rounding). The zero residual is an algebraic identity check, not evidence of an asymptotic prime estimate.

### 4. ERROR ANALYSIS

The mechanism yields the exact dyadic prime sum θ(X)-θ(X/2), but no independent value or asymptotic for it. Summing such exact bands over dyadic scales merely reconstructs θ(X), still without evaluating the total. The first loss is not a positivity or channel-mixing failure: the channel is perfectly isolated. The missing step is an independent lattice evaluation of the sum, and the product defining B_X already encodes the prime divisors through X. Therefore no M0 or explicit ψ(X)-X error is obtained.

### 5. FILTERS

- **F-LATTICE — PASS for the identity, no asymptotic evaluation.** It uses exact divisibility and the cutoff n≤X; the critical fact is that a divisor q>X/2 cannot have a nontrivial cofactor within the lattice interval.
- **F-MULT — PASS.** The local factors are indexed by rational primes and their divisibility; the squarefree divisor expansion uses unique factorization.
- **F-GLOBAL — PASS only as a moving full-prime construction.** A_y uses every prime p≤y, with y increasing to X. Its exact output contains the target band sum; no fixed-prime truncation or prime-free passage is supplied.

### 6. MILESTONE REACHED

**Below M0.** Exact positive single-band isolation is proved, but the prime number theorem or any explicit error term is not derived.

### 7. WHY

The difference of nested local-divisibility products preserves nonnegativity and cancels every squarefree divisor supported below X/2. The only surviving divisors are primes in the target interval, so the composite leakage is exactly zero. But this cancellation works because the statistic's defining product already distinguishes the prime support; evaluating its total is equivalent to evaluating the target prime sum. This is an exact selector, not an independent prime-distribution mechanism.

### 8. LESSON

Positive algebra can create a perfectly localized prime kernel, so Pass 38's monotonicity obstruction applies only to mixtures of tail masks, not to all positive divisibility constructions. The next step must preserve this localization while replacing prime-indexed input by a statistic with an independently evaluable integer-lattice total; otherwise exact selection remains tautological.

### 9. FAILURE CLASS

**New class 27: positive divisor-product difference isolates a prime band exactly, but its aggregate is the target prime sum itself.** Pointwise isolation is proved; independent total evaluation and any prime error remain absent.

**Reproducibility.** See `scripts/scale_separation_pass39.py` and `scripts/scale_separation_pass39_output.txt`.

## Pass 40 — Consolidation: fixed positive dilate kernels and scale leakage

### Consolidation target and frequency audit

The top failure table has a tie at frequency 3: (i) positive Chebyshev smoothing/short-window leakage and (ii) Pascal-carry positive-postfilter inversion. Pass 30 already consolidated both at their then-stated scopes. This checkpoint strengthens the smoothing result to all fixed bounded Riemann-integrable nonnegative profiles; it does not count as a new mechanism. The Pascal-carry result from Pass 30 remains: a strict Fourier contraction cannot be undone by positive probability postfilters, and no general inverse-rate theorem for other kernels is claimed.

### Restricted theorem: fixed Riemann-integrable dilates cannot localize a prime-power band

Let (w:[0,1]\to[0,\infty)) be a fixed bounded Riemann-integrable function with (A=\int_0^1 w(t)\,dt>0). For integer X and q≤X define

\[
K_X(q)=\sum_{1\le m\le\lfloor X/q\rfloor}w(mq/X).
\]

Put (h=q/X). Then

\[
hK_X(q)=h\sum_{m\le\lfloor1/h\rfloor}w(mh)\longrightarrow A\qquad(h\to0).
\]

Proof: these are right-endpoint Riemann sums on intervals of width h covering ([0,h\lfloor1/h\rfloor]); the uncovered terminal interval has length less than h, so its integral is at most (h\|w\|_\infty). Riemann integrability makes the mesh-h sum converge to A. Therefore, for every sequence q_X/X→0,

\[
K_X(q_X)=(A+o(1))\frac{X}{q_X}.
\]

This proves a restricted no-localization theorem: no fixed bounded Riemann-integrable nonnegative dilate profile of positive area has a kernel supported only on a top prime-power band, or asymptotically flat across all q/X→0. The lower-divisor kernel necessarily grows like X/q. This is only a statement about the transform kernel; it does **not** prove that actual lower prime-power mass dominates, and it does not show that a Tauberian argument using the broad kernel cannot reach M0.

The exact arithmetic identity is

\[
S_w(X)=\sum_{n\le X}w(n/X)\log n
=\sum_{q=p^k\le X}\Lambda(q)K_X(q),
\]

The exact divisor identity is \(\log n=\sum_{q\mid n,\ q=p^k}\Lambda(q)\). Writing n=qm and interchanging the finite sums gives the formula. The identity uses the ordinary integer lattice and multiplicativity through the von Mangoldt function; exact evaluation of S_w gives a broad weighted prime-power sum, not a single-scale estimate by itself.

### Numerical verification

Take the discontinuous but Riemann-integrable profile (w(t)=\mathbf1_{[1/2,1]}(t)), for which A=1/2. The lattice sum is Σ_{X/2≤n≤X}log n and is independently evaluated as a log-factorial ratio. The prime-power side uses exact sieve-generated prime powers and the integer kernel count. At q=X/r with even r,

\[
K_X(q)=r/2+1,\qquad qK_X(q)/X=1/2+1/r.
\]

| X | lattice sum | Σₚ Λ(q)K_X(q) | absolute difference | qK/X for r=10,100,1000 |
|---:|---:|---:|---:|---|
| 10,000 | 44,526.30152113 | 44,526.30152113 | 1.0×10⁻⁵⁵ | 0.600; 0.510; 0.501 |
| 100,000 | 560,314.7986276 | 560,314.7986276 | 2.9×10⁻⁵³ | 0.600; 0.510; 0.501 |
| 1,000,000 | 6,754,342.338199 | 6,754,342.338199 | 3.9×10⁻⁵² | 0.600; 0.510; 0.501 |

The exact transform identity passed through X=10^6. The kernel values approach A as q/X→0, as the theorem predicts. The full script and raw output are in `scripts/scale_separation_pass40.py` and `scripts/scale_separation_pass40_output.txt`.

### ERROR ANALYSIS AND MILESTONE

The theorem prevents this fixed-profile transform from suppressing lower q channels in its kernel. It yields no numerical error term for ψ(X)-X, because it neither evaluates the broad sum as a prime asymptotic nor proves a bound for the complement. A separate Tauberian theorem might use such a broad weighted family, so an M0 ceiling is not proved. Consolidation is **below M0 as new progress**.

### FILTERS AND BEURLING AUDIT

- **F-LATTICE:** the proof is specific to equally spaced integer multiples (m q) and their exact uniform Riemann mesh. A Beurling generalized-integer sequence need not give this kernel or this Riemann sum. No transfer theorem or Beurling counterexample is asserted; this restricted kernel result alone says nothing about M5/M6 for Beurling systems.
- **F-MULT:** the identity (\log n=\sum_{q\mid n}\Lambda(q)) and prime-power indexing are essential.
- **F-GLOBAL:** every prime power q≤X is present; fixed-prime truncations are not claimed uniform.

### WHY SYNTHESIS AND SELECTION-BIAS AUDIT

Across the ledger, positive scalar totals often isolate a nonnegative channel but do not evaluate its complement; exact prime masks can localize a band but their totals equal the prime sum; smooth kernels often retain a reciprocal-scale divisor profile; and signed floor/Möbius expansions reappear when a positive sieve mask is evaluated. The fixed-profile theorem strengthens only the third statement, while Pass 39 shows that non-tail local-divisibility products can evade its kernel obstruction at the cost of encoding the target band directly. Pass 30's Fourier argument separately proves a no-go for positive probability postfiltering of a kernel with a strict Fourier contraction.

The search is adaptive rather than exhaustive: candidates are chosen after earlier failures, the ledger overrepresents divisor sums, sieves, and positive lattice quadratic forms, numerical cutoffs stop at 10^6, and failure-class counts are not independent samples. The frequency tie is bookkeeping, not evidence that these classes dominate mathematical methods. No universal impossibility claim is justified.

### Positive mechanisms not excluded

1. X-dependent profiles with a proved uniform kernel bound, especially profiles shrinking with X; the fixed-profile theorem does not cover them.
2. Coupled exact lattice identities that independently evaluate the complementary prime-power channels.
3. Non-tail local-divisibility products whose positive localized statistic has a separate, non-tautological total evaluation.
4. Signed or nonlinear inversions whose signs and stability follow from an independent arithmetic identity.
5. Nonzero-lag additive correlations or projected lattice energies with independently controlled cross terms.
6. Selberg-type refinements that quantify the qualitative PNT bootstrap without assuming the target error.

### Verdict

**PARTIAL.** A complete Riemann-sum argument proves nonlocalization for every fixed bounded Riemann-integrable profile of positive area. It does not establish a ceiling for the prime number theorem milestone, does not extend to X-dependent profiles, and does not settle the tied Pascal-carry class beyond the Pass 30 Fourier-postfilter restriction.

## Pass 41 — Positive triangular boundary-layer kernel

### Candidate

For integer (Xge 4), put (H=lfloorsqrt X
floor) and
[
w_{X,H}(n)=left(1-
rac{|n-X|}{H}
ight)_+,qquad
S_	riangle(X,H)=sum_{nge1}w_{X,H}(n)log n.
]
This is a positive tent profile centered at (X), unlike Pass 25's rectangular short-window difference. Since (log n=sum_{qmid n}Lambda(q)), finite interchange gives
[
S_	riangle(X,H)=sum_{q=p^kle X+H}Lambda(q)K_{X,H}(q),qquad
K_{X,H}(q)=sum_{mge1}left(1-
rac{|mq-X|}{H}
ight)_+.
]
Every channel is nonnegative. The top band (X-H<q<X+H) has only (m=1), so there (K_{X,H}(q)=1-|q-X|/H). This extracts a tapered prime-power band, rather than a flat count.

### Exact kernel estimate

Let (phi(t)=(1-|t|)_+), of integral 1 and total variation 2. The points (t_m=(mq-X)/H) form a lattice of spacing (q/H). The bounded-variation Riemann-sum inequality gives, uniformly for (qge1),
\[
\left|K_{X,H}(q)-\frac Hq\right|\le 2.
\]
In particular, whenever (q/H\to0), (K_{X,H}(q)=(1+o(1))H/q). Thus the tapered window still has reciprocal small-divisor leakage; its taper changes the phase behavior near (q\asymp H), but does not flatten the small-(q) kernel. This is a kernel statement, not a theorem that actual small-prime mass dominates asymptotically.

The integer-side total has the exact form
\[
S_\triangle=H\log X+\sum_{j=1}^{H-1}\left(1-\frac jH\right)\log\left(1-\frac{j^2}{X^2}\right),
\]
and so it is independently computable by a short log-factorial sum. For (H<X),
\[
0\le H\log X-S_\triangle
\le \frac{\sum_{j=1}^{H-1}j^2(1-j/H)}{X^2\left(1-((H-1)/X)^2\right)}
\le \frac{H^3}{3X^2\left(1-((H-1)/X)^2\right)}.
\]
For (H=\lfloor\sqrt X\rfloor) this correction is (O(X^{-1/2})). Knowing this total still does not independently evaluate the tapered top prime-power channel.

### Numerical verification

An exact smallest-prime-factor sieve enumerated all prime powers through (X+H); the two sides were accumulated independently with 55-digit Decimal arithmetic. The exact identity agreed to residual at most (8\times10^{-50}) through (X=10^6).

| (X) | (H) | lattice total | prime-power total | absolute residual | top-band channel | top share |
|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 100 | 10 | 46.04343555033 | 46.04343555033 | (1.0\times10^{-53}) | 12.47119988043 | 27.08572836% |
| 1,000 | 31 | 214.1379331724 | 214.1379331724 | (<10^{-49}) | 30.46457953283 | 14.22661510% |
| 10,000 | 100 | 921.0332039310 | 921.0332039310 | (5.0\times10^{-52}) | 70.91984864390 | 7.70003170% |
| 100,000 | 316 | 3,638.084183979 | 3,638.084183979 | (2.0\times10^{-51}) | 286.4720822372 | 7.87425655% |
| 1,000,000 | 1,000 | 13,815.51047463 | 13,815.51047463 | (8.0\times10^{-50}) | 970.7074139919 | 7.02621460% |

At (X=10^6), the (q\le H) band contributes 45.806625%, (H<q\le X/10) contributes 33.120742%, and (X/10<q\le X/2) contributes 12.135502%. These finite shares are diagnostics, not limiting estimates.

### Filters, milestone, and failure analysis

- **F-LATTICE: PASS.** Exact triangular weights on consecutive integers and exact multiples.
- **F-MULT: PASS.** Uses the essential identity \(\log n=\sum_{q\mid n}\Lambda(q)\), with (q) restricted to prime powers.
- **F-GLOBAL: PASS for the finite identity.** All prime powers through (X+H) are included; no fixed-prime truncation is used.
- **Milestone: below M0.** This is an exact positive tapered-band decomposition plus a uniform (O(1)) kernel discrepancy. It does not estimate the band independently and proves no new bound for (\psi(X)-X) or (\theta(X)-X).

**Failure mode:** positivity exposes the desired top-band channel but leaves a positive complementary total. The uniform kernel estimate forces (K(q)\sim H/q) for (q=o(H)), so the mechanism does not suppress the small-divisor region. The observed top-band share is only 7.03% at (10^6), but no limiting share is claimed.

**Relation to prior work:** This is a distinct tapered boundary profile, not Pass 25's sharp interval. It lies in the broader positive-window leakage class already recorded in Passes 25, 26, 30, and 40. It strengthens the X-dependent-window analysis with a uniform bounded-variation kernel estimate, but does not create a universal no-go theorem.

**Constraint for the next candidate:** Any positive shrinking profile should be analyzed through its uniform lattice-kernel discrepancy and its small-(q) asymptotic; a taper alone is insufficient. A new candidate must either independently evaluate the complementary channels or prove a uniform aggregate bound beyond finite band-share data.

Reproduction files: `scripts/scale_separation_pass41.py` and `scripts/scale_separation_pass41_output.txt`.

## Pass 42 — Harmonic reciprocal lattice transform

### Mechanism and exact identity

Define
\[
S(X)=\sum_{2\le n\le X}\frac{\log n}{n}.
\]
The exact divisor identity \(\log n=\sum_{q\mid n}\Lambda(q)\) gives
\[
S(X)=\sum_{q=p^k\le X}\Lambda(q)K_X(q),\qquad
K_X(q)=\frac1q H_{\lfloor X/q\rfloor},
\]
where \(H_m=\sum_{j=1}^m1/j\). Indeed, writing \(n=qm\) makes the contribution from each prime power \(q\) equal \((\Lambda(q)/q)\sum_{m\le X/q}1/m\). This harmonic kernel is not a fixed dilate, endpoint window, factorial channel, or block contrast: it weights multiples reciprocally and accumulates a logarithmic factor over quotient scales.

### Integer-side estimate and kernel shape

For \(f(t)=\log(t)/t\), its total variation on \([1,\infty)\) is \(2/e\): it rises from 0 to 1/e at \(t=e\), then decreases to 0. The right-Riemann-sum error is bounded by total variation, so
\[
\left|S(X)-\int_1^X\frac{\log t}{t}\,dt\right|\le\frac2e,
\qquad
\left|S(X)-\frac12(\log X)^2\right|\le\frac2e.
\]
The lattice total is therefore known to an explicit O(1) error.

For every \(1\le q\le X\), the harmonic-number estimate \(0<H_m-\log m-\gamma<1/(2m)\) and \(m=\lfloor X/q\rfloor\) imply
\[
\left|K_X(q)-\frac{\log(X/q)+\gamma}{q}\right|\le\frac3X.
\]
Thus this positive transform is broad rather than endpoint-localized: for \(q=o(X)\), its kernel is approximately \((\log(X/q)+\gamma)/q\). In fact
\[
S(X)-S(X-1)=\frac{\log X}{X}
=\frac1X\sum_{q\mid X}\Lambda(q),
\]
so differencing this known total simply recovers the original divisor identity; it does not independently evaluate the prime channels.

### Numerical check

An exact SPF sieve classified all prime powers through \(10^6\); direct and transformed sums used 55-digit Decimal arithmetic. The exact transform agreed with the independent lattice sum to residual at most \(5.41\times10^{-51}\).

| X | direct lattice sum | prime-power transform | absolute residual | \(\tfrac12\log^2X\) | observed error (bounded by 2/e) |
|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 100 | 10.55397618354915 | 10.55397618354915 | \(1.0\times10^{-53}\) | 10.60379622095680 | −0.0498200374 |
| 1,000 | 23.78917903699571 | 23.78917903699571 | \(9.0\times10^{-53}\) | 23.85854149715279 | −0.0693624602 |
| 10,000 | 42.34282954852016 | 42.34282954852016 | \(2.3\times10^{-52}\) | 42.41518488382718 | −0.0723553353 |
| 100,000 | 66.20096810003602 | 66.20096810003602 | \(1.24\times10^{-51}\) | 66.27372638097998 | −0.0727582809 |
| 1,000,000 | 95.36135705088170 | 95.36135705088170 | \(5.41\times10^{-51}\) | 95.43416598861116 | −0.0728089377 |

At \(X=10^6\), prime-power bands contribute: \(q\le\sqrt X\), 67.986017%; \(\sqrt X<q\le X/10\), 22.430642%; \(X/10<q\le X/2\), 7.942653%; and \(X/2<q\le X\), 1.640688%. The final half-band contributes only 0.726214% at \(X=10^6\). These finite shares illustrate the kernel shape and prove no limiting dominance statement.

### Filters, error, and milestone

- **F-LATTICE — PASS.** The explicit \(2/e\) evaluation is an ordinary-lattice sum/integral estimate; no identical Beurling-system estimate is claimed.
- **F-MULT — PASS.** The transform uses the essential unique-factorization identity \(\log n=\sum_{q\mid n}\Lambda(q)\).
- **F-GLOBAL — PASS for the full moving finite identity.** Every prime power \(q\le X\) is included.
- **Milestone: below M0.** The known total \(S(X)=\tfrac12\log^2X+O(1)\) is a broad harmonic average. Positivity yields no lower estimate for a selected prime band; its first difference is exactly the divisor identity and supplies no new prime-counting information.

**Failure mode:** the harmonic reciprocal weight amplifies small and intermediate prime powers through \((\log(X/q)+\gamma)/q\). The known O(1)-accurate total does not evaluate any component independently. This belongs to the existing positive-smoothing/scale-leakage family, but the specific reciprocal harmonic kernel is new in this ledger.

**Constraint for the next candidate:** do not accept a sharply evaluated weighted total as prime information unless the induced kernel is localized or a Tauberian/inversion argument has an explicit error bound. Differencing must be checked algebraically; here it returns the input divisor identity.

Reproducibility files: `scripts/scale_separation_pass42.py` and `scripts/scale_separation_pass42_output.txt`.
